# Iterations history — Phase 6 (Execution Engine, Paper Trading & Go-Live)

Per-iteration changelog for Phase 6. Appended at each iteration's close-out; see `.claude/rules/iterations-history.md`.

## 2026-07-10 — iter-079: Phase-6 kickoff — adapter verified, key ceremony, go-live pre-registered

- **Phase-6 interactive kickoff executed** (spec `docs/specs/00039-phase6-kickoff-design.md`, attended): all §12 blocking items decided — **engine = NautilusTrader made fully functional** (human's direction; thin-custom stays strictly a fallback, auto-trigger restricted to §7's order-semantics/reconciliation failure class), **host = D2 VPS** (engine as its own resource-limited compose service; §8 hardening verified before the key lands = an iter-081 gate), **trade key activated now** (D3(ii) first half), **D3(iii) committed** (the 6b sleeve trade plan includes ≥1 margin long + ≥1 margin short across ≥2 rollovers, one closed one settled, within the first two 6b weeks), and **go-live criteria pre-registered** (6a concordance gate with journaled-input replay semantics; 6b tracking band; §12's model-consistency criterion with the return-to-Phase-5 hatch; ramp as per-step D3(vi) human decisions, >25 % gated on T2-pass-or-T3-fallback). Decisions log `[iter-079]` (8 entries).
- **Adversarial spec verification before commit** (3-agent pass vs master plan / repo rules / release source) caught pre-registration-poisoning issues, decisively: **the adapter exposes no AddOrder `validate` param**, so the ratified validate-only sweep was unexecutable — substituted a zero-fill sweep pre-run with the human's sign-off; the go-live gate had dropped §12's model-consistency criterion — restored.
- **Key ceremony done** (guided): least-privilege trade key (query + orders + WebSocket; no withdrawal/export ever), IP-allowlisted (VPS + workstation as a verification-window-only exception), stored ansible-vault-encrypted in `group_vars/capture_host/vault.yml`, verified by an authenticated Balance read.
- **§7 adapter verification: PASS on all six probes** (memo `docs/reference/adapter-verification/1.230.0.md`, mdformat-allowlisted): auth/account, reconciliation, WS data (10/10 pairs first-tick at 2.0 s), zero-fill order semantics ×4 incl. **margin short at leverage 2 accepted-rested-canceled**, the ~€10 real round-trip (fill → reconcile → close → flat; total verification cost €0.1618), post-run reconciliation clean. **NautilusTrader 1.230.0 confirmed as the Phase-6 engine**, exact-pinned with a version-bump tripwire test (`tests/test_nautilus_adapter.py`, suite 956 green).
- **Build findings recorded for the shadow-engine build (renumbered to iter-081 — the 00038 trial took iter-080)**: XBT→BTC normalization (`<BASE>/EUR.KRAKEN` IDs); crossing post-only surfaces as accept-then-venue-cancel (never `OrderRejected`); `spot_account_type=MARGIN` reports TradeBalance equity, not wallet balances; adapter nonces outpace millisecond-nonce sidecar scripts; observed taker fee exactly **0.80 %/side** (vs modeled 0.6 %) → appended to T0014's findings for the calibration pass.
- **Topics**: T0018 opened (Phase-6 build sequence + cross-iteration constraints — cadence-agnostic engine keyed to the 00038 verdict, §8-hardening-before-key gate, full 6b-executor scope); T0005 consciously re-deferred to Stage 6b (trigger refreshed). Key-allowlist closure (workstation IP removal) enforced via the iter-079 PR checklist.

## 2026-07-10 — iter-082: the record-44 builder + concordance core (the Phase-6 engine's computational heart)

- **The deployable system is now committed, tested code** (spec `docs/specs/00040-crossfreq-builder-design.md`, attended design + three-agent adversarial spec verification that caught, pre-implementation: the exact raw-vs-ffilled **feed map** (the B sleeve consumes raw prices — Nones drive basket disqualification), the **newest-row contract** (without synthetic boundary/dummy-close appends the forming interval's B/A1 targets silently zero — the row the engine trades), and the **no-peek gap** (replay on a contaminated snapshot reproduces the same wrong target, so the journal needs hard boundary invariants). Decisions log `[iter-082]` (gate restated to 4h semantics, human-ratified; two-iteration scope; `zcrypto engine` naming; vectorize-now).
- **`build_crossfreq_system`** (`cli/portfolio/crossfreq_system.py`): the verified path — record 44's full construction with frozen-figure regression tests pinning every registered figure (Sharpe 1.5609/1.5583, maxDD 13.57 %/18.66 %, cap-breach 1,318, governor-engaged 7,302, five sleeve anchors, dataset-extent guard first: "canonical dataset drifted — STOP"). Emits one extra **newest row** (the forming 4h interval's target) under a three-part no-peek/no-drop invariant test. Build time 111.5 s full history.
- **`build_crossfreq_system_fast`**: **bit-identical** (dataclass-equal), **~58×** (1.9 s vs 111.5 s) — reproduces CPython's exact rational arithmetic for the hot rolling statistics (big-integer window sums finished by the stdlib's own correctly-rounded square root) and vectorizes only order-insensitive arithmetic. Review verified the math against the CPython source, proved the −0.0 accumulator invariant, and ran 3,000+ adversarial bit-identity probes incl. subnormals and None-bearing grids. numpy promoted to a direct dependency.
- **`cli/engine/` (library package)**: the journal contract (schema v1; byte-pinned snapshot content hashes — int64 LE epoch-seconds + float64 LE with None→NaN, one shared helper; **no-peek boundary invariants enforced on metadata AND reconciled against the data actually read** — the review's daily-grid catch), `replay_cycle` (hash-verify → boundary-check → build → newest row located by `cycle_ts`), `compare_targets` (1e-6 absolute, structural fail on asset-set mismatch), `evaluate_gate` (the ratified 4h gate: complete-UTC-days universe, 6 cycles/day, 30-min inclusive deadlines, ≥ 14 consecutive clean days, duplicate-cycle guard). 59 synthetic tests, no dataset dependency.
- **Docs**: runbook gained the record-44 deployable-system section (construction, figures, reproduction, the multiplier-transition P&L disclosure, the equivalence-evidence discipline); record-33 spec marked historical; CLAUDE.md's stale "no CLI subcommands" parenthetical fixed. Suite: **1,064 passed** (from 957). Next: iter-083 — the node (TradingNode, scheduler, price store + REST append before the ≈ Jul 25 window in T0018, journal writing, `zcrypto engine` CLI).

## 2026-07-12 — iter-093: the three-tier topology — Role A (the always-on NAS pull/archive) is LIVE

- **New topology (spec + plan `00048`)**: the durability path moves off the intermittently-online workstation to a **three tiers** — VPS producer (24×7) → always-on **NAS ops node** (Synology, Docker) → intermittent workstation. Failure-domain reasoning: the VPS is uncorrelated with the home ISP; the NAS+workstation share it (≤14 h outage) but the VPS 7-day ring buffer covers that with ~12× margin. Three roles, built incrementally: **A** pull/archive (this iter), **B** gate-verify replay, **C** redundant capture. Human gates (scope A+B+C, RAID-5 durability, reboot-window move, keep-Grafana, uid/gid 1000, plain-POSIX perms) were cleared one-by-one.
- **Role A code** (`cli/archive/`): `zcrypto archive pull SOURCE DEST` — rsync-over-ssh then a manifest hash-verify sweep (`verify_tree` walks `*.parquet`, skips in-progress `.part*`, checks each against its `.sha256` sidecar; a malformed/missing sidecar counts *failed*, never crashes) + a `pull_lag_seconds` dead-man (age of the newest verified segment). **Distinct exit codes**: 2 = transport failure (incl. a missing `ARCHIVE_SSH_KEY`) — a partial pull is never authoritative; 1 = hash mismatch; 0 = all verified. `--no-verify` for sidecar-less sources (the engine journal). Fail-closed ssh: `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` + a pre-seeded read-only `known_hosts`, `IdentitiesOnly=yes`, `CheckHostIP=no`; `--chmod=D0775,F0664` forces the mandated perms (plain-POSIX share = no ACL inheritance).
- **NAS stack** (`infra/nas/`): a standalone `archive-pull` container under Container Manager (`restart: unless-stopped`, no systemd / no NAS-OS config), `user: "1000:1000"`, an in-container scheduler (`pull-entrypoint.sh`, SIGTERM-clean, hourly default) that runs the capture pull always and gates the journal pull on `JOURNAL_SOURCE` (**capture-only** in Increment 1 — the journal has no sidecars and needs a distinct key). Plus an idempotent plain-POSIX `normalize-archive-perms.sh` (0775/0664, group `zcrypto`).
- **Two-variant image ([[T0029]])**: the NAS Atom C3538 (Goldmont) has **no AVX**, so the standard `polars` wheel crashes `Illegal instruction`. Resolved with a `POLARS_RUNTIME` build arg + a 2-entry CI matrix (`.github/workflows/capture-image.yml`): the **default** multi-arch/AVX `polars-runtime-32` image for the VPS is **runtime-untouched**; an amd64 **`-compat`** build swaps to `polars-runtime-compat` (same polars 1.42.1 — `polars-lts-cpu` is deprecated, `rtcompat` is the maintainer's replacement). The shared image also gained `rsync`+`openssh-client` and a fixed **uid-1000** default user (ssh's `getpwuid` exits 255 otherwise) — both inert on the VPS (its compose overrides `user:`; capture/engine don't rsync).
- **VPS (`capture` role)**: a read-only `rrsync -ro` forced-command channel for the segment pull on a **separate** `sync_capture` key (vaulted private, public in group_vars); rsync installed; `deploy` added to the `kraken-capture` group; unattended-upgrades reboot window **04:00 → 02:00 UTC**. Converged with `--check` showing zero capture drift.
- **Deploy evidence (attended)**: NAS container Up on the `-compat` digest; **first pull `checked=1962 ok=1962 failed=0`**, 2.0 GB archived to `/volume1/ZhaoCrypto` owned `1000:1000` at `0664/0775`, host key pinned (`[zcrypto.zhaow.me]:10022`), `lag_s≈6203 s` traced to a real finalized+verified hour-14 segment. Dry-run first proved the rrsync channel end-to-end (jail enforced, 4131 files).
- **Reviews**: whole-branch (Opus 4.8) → READY TO MERGE, then its 3 substantive Minors folded in (exit-2 on missing key, fail-closed ssh, doc accuracy) and re-reviewed (Sonnet 5) → APPROVED.
- **Topics**: **T0028 registered** (Role A re-hashes the whole unbounded archive each cycle → O(archive), `ripe_when` ≈ 250–600 GB); **T0029** partial (AVX resolved; residual = Role B cross-runtime replay determinism); **T0003 synced** (Role A supersedes the workstation-pull half; remainder = alerting drill + ≥7-day clean-run + Roles B/C).

## 2026-07-12 — iter-094: the three-tier topology — Role B (NAS gate-verify + telemetry) is LIVE

- **Determinism resolved by measurement (T0029).** Before building, the pivotal residual — can the NAS's no-AVX `polars-runtime-compat` reproduce the VPS's AVX-computed gate verdict? — was measured against the 8 VPS-journaled cycles on the **real Atom NAS**: the **fast path (the gate path) is bit-identical cross-runtime, `worst |diff| 0.00e+00` on 8/8**, matching the AVX control; the verified path carries inherent ~`1e-18` same-runtime float noise and is too slow on the Atom, so it is dropped from the NAS loop. The exact-rational big-integer builder is a genuine cross-runtime *portability* property. Role B scores the gate on the fast path.
- **`zcrypto engine gate-export`** (`cli/engine/command.py`): the machine-readable sibling of `report`. Extracts report's evidence loop into a shared `_evaluate_journal`, then emits an atomic Prometheus textfile (`zcrypto_gate_status/streak_days/journal_pull_lag_seconds/mismatch_total/export_timestamp_seconds`) + an **independent healthchecks.io dead-man** ping. `clean` is based on gate *health* — `streak > 0 or last_failure is None` (captures every break reason incl. missing/late cycles, doesn't false-page pre-MET or on a recovered gate), not on a counted subset. Exit 0 on a finding; non-zero only on an operational failure.
- **Journal pull** (extends Role A): the entrypoint selects the ssh key **per call** (`CAPTURE_SSH_KEY` vs `JOURNAL_SSH_KEY`, no `command.py` change), reusing the existing VPS `sync` rrsync channel; the `gate-export` step runs after each journal pull (`JOURNAL_SOURCE`-gated).
- **NAS Alloy telemetry stack** (`infra/nas/config.alloy` + two Container-Manager compose services): host metrics via the unix exporter (**load / netdev / disk-free**) + the gate `.prom` via the textfile collector + archive-pull logs → Loki, all `remote_write`/`loki.write` to Grafana Cloud; a GET-only `docker-socket-proxy` fronts Docker.
- **One canonical dashboard** (`infra/grafana/zcrypto-dashboard.json`, uid `zcrypto-main`) + alert rules (`infra/grafana/alerts.yaml`, correct `ProvisionedAlertRule`/`expression` schema) + `infra/scripts/grafana-push.sh` (per-rule provisioning API) — namespaced so T0020 later adds the VPS rows to the same file.
- **Retired the workstation gate-ops timer** (`infra/systemd/zcrypto-engine-gateops.*` deleted; README section retired with disable steps).
- **Subagent-driven execution + adversarial review caught 11 real bugs** across per-task, a 3-reviewer whole-branch workflow, and its fix wave — e.g. sidecar-failures invisible to `mismatch_total`; `env()`→`sys.env()` (Alloy boot crash); Alloy silently root (host-secret exposure); the Loki keep-regex that couldn't match the compose-prefixed container name; the dead-man missing missing/late gate breaks; the alert-rule `expression` field + wrong provisioning endpoint (alerts never firing).
- **Attended live deploy** on the Atom NAS surfaced **6 more Synology-only bugs** (all in the compose/config, none in the Role B code): cadvisor SIGSEGVs on DSM's cgroup-less kernel (removed); `NanoCPUs`/`cpu_shares` unsupported (dropped); `/var/lib/docker` is `/volume1/@docker` (mount dropped); Alloy uid 473 isn't a DSM user so the ACL denies it write (a real DSM user is required — the live fix ran as uid 1000, later hardened to the dedicated `zcrypto-dummy` uid 1031:1000 under T0030); the DSM ACL isn't honored in-container so `textfile`/`alloy-data` need real POSIX `chmod 0775`; the socket-proxy needs `NETWORKS: 1` for Loki discovery.
- **Live verification (end-to-end):** `prometheus_remote_storage_samples_failed_total 0` + `samples_pending 0` (host + gate metrics accepted by Grafana Cloud); `loki_write_sent_entries_total 7`, `dropped 0`; healthchecks.io `zcrypto-gate-verify: up`; `gate.prom` `streak_days 2`, `status 0` (not-MET), **`mismatch_total 0`** — the NAS's compat replay agrees with the VPS in production. All 3 containers `running rc=0`. The NAS runs the branch `-compat` image (`@sha256:dfda2580…`); re-pin to the develop-built digest after merge.
- **Topics**: **T0029 → resolved** (both the AVX two-variant AND the determinism residual, now measured-closed); **T0003 synced** (Role B landed; remainder = ≥7-day clean-run + alerting drill + Role C); **T0020 synced** (the NAS observability slice — Alloy + the shared dashboard/alerts + the push script + the creds path — landed, and the dashboard/alerts **push ran live**: the single dashboard + all 7 alert rules + the `email` contact point provisioned on `zcrypto2026.grafana.net`, verified by API query; remainder = the VPS `obs` role + app `/metrics` + the capture exporter flip); **T0021 synced** (the disk-watch mechanism moved from the retired gate-ops read to the NAS Alloy disk-free alert); **T0030 → resolved** (Alloy hardened to run as the dedicated non-key-owning `zcrypto-dummy` uid 1031:1000, verified live: it ships metrics + logs but can't read the 0600 rrsync pull keys via `/host/root`). Container/cadvisor metrics are not collected on the NAS (DSM cgroup incompatibility).

- **OPS-1 — provisioned + converged**: new `ops_host` ansible group + `ops` role; the home i7-13700 (Debian 13, reinstalled from Ubuntu for fleet homogeneity) converged with `base`/`chrony`/`docker` only (home-LAN box — no hardening/firewall/fail2ban, owner's call; SSH stays port 22 with a minimal deploy-user bootstrap play). Hostname collision fixed (`zcrypto-red` → `zcrypto-ops`); fleet reboot-window assert generalized to `capture_host + ops_host` (21:25/22:25/02:25 pairwise distinct); charter enforced in inventory comments + site-play pre_task: **no trade key, never `engine_host`, pull-only**.
- **OPS-2 — liquidations, with a mid-flight pivot**: built the Binance `forceOrder` WS recorder (`zcrypto liquidations`, `LIQ_SCHEMA`, SegmentWriter reuse) — then discovered **Binance geo-fences futures market-streams from every egress we own** (home DE + Linode; spot/vision/REST all flow — proven with three independent clients). Recorder **shelved in place**; replaced by the Coinalyze poller `zcrypto liquidations-poll` (`LIQ_AGG_SCHEMA`, 1-min buckets, `convert_to_usd`, closed-bucket discipline `t+60<=now-120` — load-bearing because SegmentWriter dedup keeps the first row; overlap safety = dedup for the open hour + the writer's late-event floor for finalized hours, locked by a byte-identity regression test). Deployed live: first cycle back-filled 1878 buckets, 10/10 coins, 244 finals+manifests; dead-man `zcrypto-liquidations` green. NAS replication over a dedicated `sync_liquidations` rrsync `-ro` channel (ops port 22, per-call `LIQUIDATIONS_SSH_PORT`), optional-channel pattern in `infra/nas/{compose.yaml,pull-entrypoint.sh}`.
- **OPS-3 — the first-ever replay oracles off the Atom**: `zcrypto archive verify-replay` (canonical reconciled-first book continuity: snapshot-anchored / ts-ordered / checksum-present / structurally-replayable per hour — deliberately NO CRC re-derivation, deferred to [[T0045]] with the raw-string schema change) + two daily systemd timers (03:41 continuity-replay, 05:23 `engine replay --path verified`) emitting textfile metrics + dead-men (25 h/6 h). The ops node seeded from the NAS over a new pull-only channel (DSM lacks `rrsync` — Debian's python3 rrsync shipped to the NAS and invoked explicitly): capture-segments 3.5G, red 424M, reconciled, journal. First runs of both replays started detached.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-107: `zcrypto engine soak-check` — the realized-OOS-vs-backtest report ([[T0064]] stand-in)

- **New read-only `zcrypto engine soak-check`** (spec/plan `00058`, core in `cli/engine/soak.py`): the stand-in for the never-run out-of-time holdout — it places the deployed strategy's realized shadow-soak behaviour against its backtest expectation. It is **decision-support, never validation, never the concordance gate** (that stays `engine report`), and it **consumes no holdout budget** (reads only the journal, the live store, and the frozen `data/ohlc-full`; never touches `data/ohlc-holdout-*`). Built subagent-driven over 7 TDD tasks (implementers Sonnet 5, reviewers Opus 4.8), each task adversarially reviewed with a fix-and-reverify loop, plus a final whole-branch review.
- **Behavioural-fingerprint design.** At L≈84 4h cycles a Sharpe/return statistic can't discriminate, so the headline is **position-derived structural metrics** judged against their own overlapping-window backtest null on the frozen canonical: each metric's realized window-mean vs the distribution of historical window-means (two-sided band, `--band 0.90` → `[p5,p95]` inner `[p10,p90]`; too-good flags too; effective-n and band-width disclosed; a multiplicity line counts outliers-vs-chance).
- **The off-by-one keystone.** Forward 4h returns are **timestamp-keyed** on the store (`start=closes[T−4h]`, `end=closes[T]`, both pinned to the journal's hash-verified 240-snapshot `last_ts == cycle_ts−4h`); a `_chain_consistent` cross-check (`end(T)==start(T+4h)`) VOIDs on a fragmented scored sequence. A null-close in the store skips the cycle rather than crashing.
- **The governor-cost (D4) bias cancels by construction.** The non-gating P&L null is recast onto the live cost convention (`net_live = governed_net + mult·fee·Σ|Δcapped| − fee·Σ|Δfinal_targets|`), derived from the builder's returned quantities alone — `capped` reconstructed from `sleeve_positions` via `apply_position_caps(⅓·ΣB/A1/A2)`, never `final_targets/multipliers` (the `mult==0` trap). A `reconcile_ok` invariant (`mult·capped==final_targets` to 1e-9) VOIDs a bad reconstruction.
- **Distrust-the-instrument VOID gates + honesty lock.** Every run runs three self-tests that VOID on a ran-and-failed check: the frozen build reproduces registry **record 44's exact integer diagnostics** (`governor_engaged_bars=7302`, `cap_breach_bars=1318`, read from the registry not hardcoded), the journaled `final_targets` reproduce under `replay_cycle` (1e-6), and the null reconciles. A near-zero-exposure window → INDETERMINATE (the record-33 `[0,0]` echo). Every report prints the bare-fact banner (trial 44 has zero OOS holdout evidence; paper is its only OOS test) and the rendered text is **vocabulary-locked** (never validated/passed/confirmed/proven — note "Provenance" contains "proven", so the section is "REALIZED-SERIES WINDOW", and self-test prose like "identity check passed" is never echoed to the text, only to the non-locked JSON). Refusal is **exit 0**; only a broken-instrument VOID is exit 1.
- **Verified end-to-end on the real journal (2026-07-20):** all three self-tests `ok` (the frozen build reproduced record 44 live), longest clean run L=14 (2.17 days — the cutover/restarts fragmented the 44-cycle journal), `chain_ok` True, and it **honestly refused** — "NO VERDICT — L=14 < floor=30" — at exit 0. The 5-metric table + P&L line populate once ≥30 consecutive clean cycles accumulate (approaching the ~07-25 gate).
- **Scope decisions (reversible, surfaced to the owner) + follow-ups.** v1 **gates on 5 position metrics** (gross/net exposure, active fraction, turnover, HHI) — the journal carries `final_targets` (=`mult×capped`) but not the per-bar multiplier or a pre-cap book, so **governor-engagement + cap-breach** (undetectable/ambiguous from the traded weights) are reported as clearly-labeled **backtest context**, not realized comparisons: registered [[T0072]] (recover them via a build on the latest journal snapshot). The spec's **secondary block-bootstrap null** + `--null`/`--path` options + regime-context section are built-but-unwired (the primitive `block_bootstrap_null` exists + is tested, docstring-flagged): registered [[T0073]]. Turnover + P&L use **interior bars** (drop the first full-entry bar symmetrically on both sides) instead of the spec's flat-start-per-window normalization.
- **Closeout.** [[T0064]] flipped **partial** — the OOS-consistency instrument now exists and self-proves on live data; the recording at the gate + the human go-live judgment remain (its `ripe_when` stands). README `## Usage` gained the `soak-check` entry. PR #154.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-108: soak-check gates the realized governor/cap fingerprint ([[T0072]], spec 00059)

- **The two remaining structural metrics became real realized-vs-backtest comparisons.** `zcrypto engine soak-check` gated on 5 metrics computable from the journaled `final_targets`; governor-engagement and cap-breach needed strategy *internals* the journal doesn't store (the per-bar multiplier; a pre-cap book). The unlock: each cycle record's 240 snapshot carries the **full hash-verified price history**, so ONE `build_crossfreq_system_fast` rebuild on the **latest** record yields multipliers and sleeve positions for every scored cycle — no live-store dependency, the journal is its own evidence.
- **The alignment keystone, proven on production data.** The row for a decision at cycle `T` is the index where `h4_ts[k] == T − 4h` (resolved from a `{ts: index}` map, never positionally). It is proven window-wide by a `final_targets` identity against the journaled values, which **VOIDs** on any shift — measured on the real 55-cycle journal at **worst |diff| = 0.0** across 54 cycles × 10 assets (bit-exact), with per-bar cap-breach agreeing with the builder's own diagnostic at **1318 == 1318**.
- **`n/a` now covers BOTH degeneracies (spec D8, found only by running for real).** The first live 7-metric run returned `governor_engagement` with band `[0.0, 1.0]` — the entire attainable range of a rate, so nothing could ever fall outside it — yet verdict "consistent" *and* counted in the multiplicity denominator. `metric_verdict` already escaped a **zero**-width band; a **full**-width band is the same failure inverted. An optional `domain` marks it `n/a` when the band covers **both** ends (a one-sided touch keeps its power, so `active_frac`'s real `[0.0074, 1.0]` stays discriminating), and the panel self-corrects to "0 of 6".
- **A correction the instrument made to its operator (spec D9).** Mid-iteration the constant realized multiplier (exactly 0.5 on all 54 cycles) was read against the null's *global* engagement rate (0.266) and flagged as a likely divergence. Judged correctly — against the distribution of same-length 9-day windows — the backtest reaches 100% engagement often enough that p95 = 1.0, so nine-of-nine engaged days is unremarkable. **Not a divergence.** Comparing a window statistic to a global mean is precisely the error the windowed null exists to prevent; it was caught before any verdict was registered.
- **Honesty hardening.** The vocabulary lock is now **structural** rather than conventional: free-form text reaching the rendered report (exception-derived reasons, void reasons) is scrubbed at the render boundary while the JSON keeps the raw diagnostic. A disclosure claiming a "partial-day downward bias" was **removed as false** — the multiplier is constant within a day by construction, so a partial day carries identical engagement information. And because `gross`/`net`/`active_frac`/`hhi` are all deterministic functions of one weight vector with `turnover` its first difference, the report now names that cluster (genuinely independent dimensions ≈ 2–3, not 6) and the footer states plainly that these are **structural-conformance checks — does the live book look like the backtest book — not evidence of edge**.
- **No regression, verified against a saved pre-change baseline:** all five original metrics byte-identical, provenance identical (L=54, span 8.83 d, `chain_ok`), `void_reasons: []`, exit 0. Constant realized series are judged, not suppressed — `n/a` stays reserved for an undiscriminating *instrument*, never a constant *observation*.
- **Ops-node enablement (first autonomous run here).** `data/engine-store` is absent by design on ops and is **not** a fetchable hot-hub dataset; `zcrypto engine seed` generates it from canonical + a Kraken **public** REST gap-fill (20/20 series seam-QA clean, 7700 bars appended, 0 divergent tail rows). The journal lives at the pull mirrors (`/mnt/zhao-crypto/engine-journal`), not `data/`. With both in place the soak-check produces real verdicts on ops.
- **Closeout.** [[T0072]] resolved + archived. [[T0073]] (secondary block-bootstrap null + `--null`/`--path`/regime context) remains open. No trial registered and no budget spent — this iteration builds instrument, not evidence.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-109: gate-export profiled — the super-linear hypothesis refuted, the real cause found ([[T0069]])

- **Measured, not assumed.** [[T0069]] suspected `zcrypto engine gate-export` of super-linear (repeated full-journal) work because ~8.5 min of CPU for a 96 MB journal "is far more than a linear scan should cost". Scaling the ops journal mirror 1→7 days shows per-cycle cost **flat** — 1.667 → 1.603 s/cycle across 6→39 cycles, slightly *decreasing* as fixed startup amortizes — and every step matches linear (6→12 cycles: ×1.97 measured vs ×2.00 linear, ×4.00 quadratic). **There is no O(n²) bug.**
- **But the root cause is worse than a slow scan, and better news for the fix.** `cProfile` attributes **81%** of the run to `build_crossfreq_system_fast`, called **once per journaled cycle** via `replay_cycle` (39 calls, 120.6 s cumulative) — each one a full ~28k-bar, 10-asset, 12-year strategy rebuild. Every hourly run re-verifies the *entire* append-only, hash-verified journal from scratch, redoing work the previous run already did. Snapshot hashing is a distant second at 14%.
- **Linear is not safe when the input grows without bound.** At the NAS Atom's ~10.9 s/cycle (derived from the topic's own 8.5 min / ~47-cycle datapoint; the Atom is ~6.8× slower than the ops i7), the topic's own "~20 min wall" trigger projects to fire **≈2026-07-29**, 50% of the 3600 s pull interval **≈2026-08-07**, and 100% **≈2026-09-04**. This **corrects the 2026-07-19 trigger check** that recorded both thresholds as "comfortably unfired" — true then, ~9 days out on the projection.
- **The remedy choice is now evidence-backed rather than a guess.** Incremental scoring (cache the verified prefix keyed on the journal's own content hashes; replay only new cycles) attacks the actual cause and bounds the hourly cost at roughly ONE cycle's rebuild (~11 s) **regardless of journal growth** — a ~47× cut that stays flat. Relocation to ops (spec `00054` D6's deferred option) buys a constant ~6.8× and decouples the single-threaded pull loop, but postpones rather than bounds. **Recommendation: both, incremental first.** [[T0069]] → `partial`, its `ripe_when` rewritten from a threshold to "ripe NOW".
- **No code changed.** This iteration is measurement + a corrected remedy decision; incremental scoring caches *gate evidence*, so it gets its own TDD iteration (cache-hit-equals-fresh-replay, tampered-journal misses the cache, streak arithmetic unchanged). The relocation is attended and stays parked for a maintenance window.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-110: gate-export incremental scoring — the cost stops growing with the journal ([[T0069]], spec 00060)

- **The structural fix for iter-109's finding.** `gate-export` re-replayed every journaled cycle every hour, each replay a full ~28k-bar, 12-year rebuild (81% of runtime). It now caches per-cycle outcomes and replays only what is new: **opt-in `--cache PATH`**, with no flag meaning today's behaviour byte-for-byte and `report` never cached (it is a human-facing *verification* tool and must genuinely re-verify).
- **Verified on real production evidence, not fixtures.** On the ops journal mirror (39 cycles): no-cache 63.11 s / cold 62.77 s (39 replayed) / **warm 0.30 s (0 replayed, 39 hits)** — and **the gate metrics were identical across all three runs**, which is the load-bearing property: a cache hit must be indistinguishable from a fresh replay. Cold matches no-cache, so enabling it costs nothing. Steady state (one new cycle/hour) is ~2 s here and ~13 s on the Atom vs ~510 s today; the point is that the cost is now **flat** rather than climbing toward the 3600 s pull interval — precisely, the *expensive* per-cycle rebuild is bounded to what's new, while `_journal_artifacts` and the evidence fingerprint still scan/hash every record each run, a small O(n) constant (the 0.30 s warm run over 39 cycles is that term) that stays seconds even at the projected 332-cycle horizon.
- **The safety property is the whole design, and the review found it broken twice before merge.** The cache invalidates wholesale whenever anything that could change a verdict changes. The first implementation hashed four modules; tracing `replay_cycle`'s actual call graph found two **live** gaps — `cli/risk/governor.py` (whose multipliers fold straight into `final_targets`) and `cli/engine/journal.py` (whose `validate_record`/`snapshot_content_hash` decide pass vs `validation_failed` vs `HashMismatchError`) — plus four latent verified-route modules and the unhashed replay *path*. Separately, the per-cycle key digested only `content_hash`, so tampering `n_bars` would make the real replay fail validation while the cache served the stale **pass**. Both closed; each fix pinned by a test the reviewer confirmed fails when the fix is reverted.
- **Deliberately over-sensitive.** A comment-only edit to any of the ten covered modules invalidates the whole cache and costs one full rebuild. Over-invalidation is cheap; under-invalidation silently corrupts the evidence that decides whether the strategy trades real money.
- **Fail open, never fail trusting.** Any cache problem — absent, unreadable, truncated, wrong schema, fingerprint mismatch — degrades to a full replay and rewrites; it never aborts and never serves a partially-trusted entry. The exception nets were narrowed to `(OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError)` so a future refactor's `AttributeError` surfaces in tests instead of silently disabling the cache forever.
- **Observability so a dead cache can't look healthy:** `zcrypto_gate_cache_replayed_total`, `_hits_total`, `_invalidated` emit **always** (zero when caching is off), so a fingerprint that changes every run shows up as `hits 0 / replayed n` rather than as a working optimisation.
- **A counting trap avoided:** `JournalCounts` is now derived from each resulting `CycleOutcome` in one place rather than incremented inside the replay branches — otherwise cached cycles would stop being counted and a report would state "0 mismatches" because it never counted them, while the gate itself still looked right.
- **Closeout.** [[T0069]] updated (incremental scoring landed; the **attended** relocation and enabling `--cache` in the deployment remain, both needing a maintenance window — the code ships inert). [[T0074]] registered: the fingerprint covers the replay *code* but not the execution *environment*, so a numpy/Python bump could serve stale outcomes if the drift exceeds the 1e-6 `compare_targets` budget.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-111: soak-check's second null — a verdict that depends on how the null was built is no longer reported as a finding ([[T0073]], spec 00061)

- **The descoped half of spec `00058` D3, now wired.** `soak-check` judged every metric against a single **windowed** null (all overlapping L-bar windows). Overlapping windows share observations, so that construction understates sampling variance — a known, one-directional bias with no second opinion. The **stationary block bootstrap** (Politis–Romano) resamples independent paths and carries a different, largely uncorrelated bias. Both now run by default (`--null both`), and each metric's two verdicts are reconciled by an explicit severity rule: identical ⇒ that label; adjacent ⇒ the **milder**, disclosed; opposite extremes ⇒ **`indeterminate (instrument-fragile)`**; exactly one `n/a` ⇒ the discriminating null's label, disclosed. `--path [fast|verified]` also landed, so a suspicious result can be re-read on the oracle builder without editing code.
- **The second null earned its place on its first real run.** On the ops journal mirror (54 cycles), `gross` and `net` come back **windowed `consistent`, bootstrap `weakly-consistent`** — an adjacent disagreement, reconciled to the milder and disclosed verbatim. The magnitude is the point: on `gross` the windowed band is `[0.0005, 0.2904]` (width 0.2899) against the bootstrap's `[0.0411, 0.1699]` (width 0.1288), so **the primary null's band is 2.25× wider**, and the same live value sits at the 35.6th percentile under one construction and the 6.5th under the other. The overlapping-window variance understatement is no longer a theoretical caveat in a docstring — it is measured, on this journal, and it moves a verdict.
- **Structural honesty, not a heuristic.** The gating verdicts and the panel count the **reconciled** label; the numeric columns stay the primary's (D2), so existing readings remain comparable. The bootstrap is seeded and the seed is deliberately **not** a CLI option — a user-tunable seed on gate-adjacent evidence invites shopping for a friendlier null.
- **Eleven defects were found before merge, and every one was the same class: the instrument describing its output falsely, never miscomputing it.** The table merged two columns into `consistentweakly-consistent`; the summary line counted raw verdicts while the table rendered reconciled ones, so a row could read `inconsistent` beside "0 of 2 outside band"; the JSON asserted the windowed null was the reference even under `--null block-bootstrap`; a disclosure could claim "no discriminating power" next to a rendered `inconsistent`; a docstring promised a `SoakError` never raised; the README claimed "a disagreement is never rendered as two identical strings", which is false on the adjacent branch. Three were caught only by **reading rendered output**, and the sharpest only by a reviewer **enumerating reconciliation branches the live data never exercises**.
- **The two that mattered most.** (i) `reconcile_verdicts` validated its vocabulary *below* the equality and `n/a` short-circuits, so `("probably-fine", "n/a")` returned `probably-fine` **as a rendered verdict** — the guard existed and the defect walked around it; validation is now an unconditional **precondition**, independent of branch order. (ii) The table rendered only `verdict` (reconciled) and `secondary` (raw), which **collide on 3 of D1's 5 branches**: `primary='inconsistent', secondary='weakly-consistent'` displayed as `weakly-consistent | weakly-consistent`, a suppressed `inconsistent` invisible to the reader, with the primary's label appearing nowhere. The table now carries **three** columns (`verdict | primary | secondary`), which makes concealment structurally impossible rather than depending on the labels happening to differ. The same shape was then found and fixed on the P&L line.
- **A test that fails pre-fix is not the same as a test that pins the claim.** Mutation testing on the fix wave's own tests found the central claim — *which column reports which null construction* — pinned by nothing: swapping `primary`/`secondary`, or attributing a verdict to a construction **that never ran**, left 128/128 green because the assertions were order-agnostic membership checks. Beware also that `"inconsistent"` contains `"consistent"` as a substring, so the obvious assertion passes against the broken output. The column→construction attribution is now pinned **positionally**, verified by applying each mutation and watching it fail.
- **Regression guard.** `--null windows` reproduces the pre-change verdicts, numerics, panel line and disclosures **exactly** (confirmed by a side-by-side `develop`-vs-`HEAD` run on the real journal), so the new construction is strictly additive. `--null both` also never raises where `--null windows` degrades — which holds only because `effective_n` is computed once and shared across both nulls, now documented at the sharing site as load-bearing.
- **Closeout.** [[T0073]] → `partial`: the dual null, the reconciliation rule, `--null`/`--path` and their tests landed; the report's **regime context** section (spec `00058` report-shape item 9) stays deferred, since `00058` named it without defining what it should contain and inventing a definition here would be worse than leaving it open. Its `ripe_when:` was rewritten accordingly — the original trigger has now fired and been consumed. Spec `00061` D2's wording was refined from "one new column" to the three-column form, with the reason recorded. No trial registered and no budget spent — this iteration builds instrument, not evidence.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-112: the gate cache no longer trusts a cycle forever ([[T0077]], spec 00062)

- **The finding this fixes was not a bug report — it was a design gap nobody had modelled.** `gate-export --cache` (iter-110) skips `replay_cycle` on a fingerprint hit, and that replay is the **only** place the journal's parquet bytes are re-hashed (`concordance.py:116`). The NAS pull delegates to it explicitly (`--no-verify`: *"no .sha256 sidecars, Role B verifies it via replay"*), rsync has no `--checksum`, and `evidence_fingerprint` digests the `content_hash` **recorded in the record** rather than a fresh read. Enabling `--cache` would therefore have converted *"every parquet re-verified ~24×/day"* into *"verified once, then trusted forever"* — and a snapshot altered in place after its first replay would be served as a **PASS**, silently, with `mismatch_total` at 0 and the streak still climbing. Spec `00060` D2 had scoped its fingerprint to "everything a replay's verdict depends on **from the journal side**", so the parquet-bytes axis was never argued; the design's governing asymmetry (*over-invalidation is safe, under-invalidation silently corrupts*) was never applied to the axis that actually breaks.
- **This also reframes iter-109.** That iteration characterised the per-cycle full rebuild as pure waste — *"redoing work the previous run already did."* It is **also the continuous integrity monitor**. Any optimisation that removes redundancy must replace the monitoring the redundancy was silently providing; that is the transferable lesson, not the specific fix.
- **The mitigation: bounded rotating re-verification, always on when the cache is active** — not a flag, because a safety property that can be switched off will be. Each run force-replays a deterministic slice of otherwise cache-eligible cycles. Keyed on `sha256(cycle_ts) % 24` so a cycle's slice is a **fixed property of the cycle** — an index-keyed slice would move as the journal grows, making coverage neither uniform nor provable — and `sha256` rather than the builtin `hash()`, which is not guaranteed stable across processes. The current slice is `now.hour % 24`: stateless, no cursor to persist, corrupt, or reset.
- **A forced re-verification failure moves the gate** (D4): the same `CycleOutcome(mismatch=True)` any replay produces, into `JournalCounts`/`evaluate_gate` unchanged, counted as `replayed` and never `from_cache`. Detecting corruption must change the verdict, not merely log.
- **Verified on the real journal against the MODIFIED predicate** (57 cycles) — iter-110's "identical gate metrics" evidence covers the old eligibility rule and does not carry over: no-cache 94.52 s / cold 93.70 s / warm 2.45 s / warm-again 2.48 s, all exit 0, **gate metrics identical across all four**. Warm replays **1** with 56 hits — the counters, not the runtime, are what prove rotation engaged, since a fast warm run is exactly what a silently-disabled rotation would also produce. A 24-hour sweep re-verified **57/57 cycles, each in exactly one slice**; worst hour 5 replays ≈ 54 s on the NAS Atom, ~1.5% of the 3600 s interval against ~510 s for a full replay.
- **The guarantee was attacked, not merely tested.** Review confirmed eligibility depends only on `cycle_ts` and the run clock, reading nothing from the cache file — so an attacker with full control of that file can suppress the staleness metric but not the rotation replay, and a failed `save_cache` cannot stop it either.
- **Two latent traps closed on the way, both of the run's recurring shape.** The new metrics' *values* were unpinned end-to-end: hardcoding `oldest_verification_age` to `0.0` passed 72/72 until a test pinned the exact value — and that number is D5's own declared backstop, the sole way a stopped rotation is distinguishable from a healthy cache, so a constant `0.0` would have read as *"everything verified seconds ago, forever."* Separately, `_ROTATION_SLICES > 24` would have left high slices permanently unreachable (since `now.hour % N` only spans `[0,23]`) — **silently**, the exact failure class the iteration exists to prevent; now an assert.
- **Also corrects [[T0069]] on two counts**, established by reading the deployed loop rather than trusting its own projections: the *"loop can no longer keep up"* cliff **does not exist** (`pull-entrypoint.sh` sleeps *after* the work, so the period is `3600 + work` and the loop drifts rather than falling behind — the same file records it already running at ~103 min), and consequently **relocation is the safer remedy, not the redundant one**, since it preserves the integrity check `--cache` removes. The constraint that actually binds is the 18000 s lag alert, whose own comment concedes the value was copied from a CLI default rather than derived.
- **Closeout.** [[T0077]] → `resolved` and archived, but only after its residuals were actually worked down rather than filed away. It briefly stood at `partial` with three: the >3-day staleness alert rule, the cache-file siting decision, and the owner's threat-model judgement. All three closed in-iteration — the rule was written, committed and **pushed live** (read-back verified); siting was decided as spec `00062` D9 (ephemeral, never on a shared path, since the NAS and ops run different polars runtimes and `replay_fingerprint` digests neither); and the owner confirmed in-place alteration is worth defending against, so daily rotation stands. Worth recording that the plan's Task 3 had pre-written "T0077 → resolved" before any of that was true: closing with live deferred sub-items is exactly what the `open-topics` rule merged earlier the same day forbids, and review caught it. The difference between that and this closure is that the sub-items were *done*, not re-labelled.
- **A guard came out of the deploy, not the design.** The first live push failed with a bare `curl: (22) ... error: 400`: the new rule's uid was 41 characters against Grafana's 40-char column. It bit only on that push because every existing rule updates via `PUT` while a new one is created via `POST`, and the limit applies only on create — so a too-long uid breaks exactly once, on the run that introduces it, and `grafana-push.sh` discards the response body. Since that is discoverable only during an attended push behind a vaulted token and a GPG pinentry prompt, `tests/test_infra_alert_rules.py` now pins uid length and uniqueness, the `noDataState`/`execErrState` enums, the required-field set (including that `condition` names a refId present in `data`), and that datasource UIDs stay templated. The longest surviving uid is exactly 40 — the ceiling was already being brushed, without a guard.
- [[T0069]]'s `--cache` sub-item is unblocked: enabling the flag no longer trades away the integrity check. It remains an attended deploy. No trial registered and no budget spent — instrument, not evidence.

## 2026-07-20 — iter-113: the gate's arithmetic guarantees now fail when broken ([[T0076]], spec 00063)

- **Test-only, and that is the finding.** A 62-mutation audit of `evaluate_gate` / `replay_cycle` / `compare_targets` found **no live defect** — the gate has been computing the right thing throughout. What it found was nine guarantees the code honours and nothing enforces. Two were pinned in PR #162 (the streak threshold, the dead-engine reset); this iteration pins the rest. `git diff -- cli/` is empty across the whole branch.
- **The keystone: the daily no-peek guarantee reopened on a *same-length* peek.** `concordance.py:118` is `len(ts) != n_bars or ts[0] != first_ts or ts[-1] != last_ts`, and the existing peek fixture tripped **two** terms at once — so deleting any single conjunct left the suite green. Swap the trailing settled bar for the in-progress candle at equal length and only `last_ts` differs: absent that check the builder runs on **lookahead data while the targets still compare equal**. Of the nine findings this is the only one whose failure mode was silent contamination of the verdict rather than a mis-set threshold or a loud error. Review confirmed it must use the *daily* grid — on h4 the metadata-equality backstop masks the mutation.
- **The three conjuncts are provably independent**, not three spellings of one test: deleting each in turn fails *exactly* its own case and leaves the other two green. That cross-check is the difference between three pins and one pin with extra names.
- **Two findings decomposed further than their own wording suggested**, and both were caught only by looking again after "done" — which is the transferable lesson, not the individual fixes. Finding 7 read "the day-cutoff anchor **and window**"; the anchor landed first, and deleting `+ _FRESHNESS_WINDOW` from the day cutoff still left all 30 tests green, because the anchor test's `now=20:35` sits past both the true 20:30 cutoff and a window-dropped 20:00 one. `now=20:15` discriminates. (The two `_FRESHNESS_WINDOW` usages are not both bare — the per-cycle bound was already pinned; only the day cutoff was uncovered.) Finding 2's remainder turned out to be a **name**: `test_gate_export_stale_journal_pings_fail` read as though it covered the dead-engine reset while exercising only `--lag-fail-seconds`, and it sat green beside an entirely unpinned reset. **A name that implies a guarantee it does not provide is worse than a missing test, because it stops anyone looking.**
- **Every pin is mutation-proven, and the method is now the deliverable.** For each: apply the mutation, confirm the edit **on disk** (the original text can be a substring of the replacement, silently no-opping), purge `__pycache__`, run under `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, watch it fail, restore. That substring trap fired for real during the final review — the reviewer's own mutation was rejected by its assert, and without it a survivor would have been reported as killed.
- **A do-not-clean-up, recorded deliberately:** the metadata-equality guard is a *provably equivalent* mutant — deleting it leaves all 31 tests passing — so the next reader will be tempted to remove it as dead code. It is the backstop that makes the keystone work on the h4 grid. Spec `00063` D8 says so; review verified the equivalence rather than taking it on faith.
- **The asymmetry worth carrying forward:** every mutation that opened the gate EARLIER survived the audit; every one that opened it later was caught or errs harmlessly stricter. The existing tests were written by someone checking the gate was not too strict. Nobody checked it was not too loose.
- **Closeout.** [[T0076]] → `resolved` and archived — all nine findings pinned, the finding-2 remainder fixed in the same branch rather than deferred, and no live sub-item left. [[T0075]] (the gate-cache guarantees) remains open and is the next package; its own audit needs re-running under bytecode control first, since its 44 "detected" verdicts were produced without it and stale bytecode makes a mutant look detected when the test never saw it. No trial registered and no budget spent — instrument, not evidence.

## 2026-07-20 — gate-cache evidence guarantees pinned, and fingerprint coverage made measurable ([[T0075]], [[T0080]])

- **All 19 [[T0075]] gate-cache guarantees are pinned** (spec `00064`), each mutation-verified under bytecode control. No live defect anywhere — `gate_cache.py` invalidates correctly today; what was missing was any test that notices when it stops. The whole-branch review re-derived coverage independently, writing its own 19 mutations from the audit's mutant definitions rather than reading the task mapping, and every one was killed by exactly the test claiming it.
- **The scope was 19, not the recorded 8 — and the reason was not the one predicted.** [[T0075]] expected its count understated because Audit A ran without bytecode control. Re-audited: **zero false detections**, so all 44 of Audit A's clean verdicts stand. The undercount came from **triage error** — `pair`/`grid` were ruled equivalent because the entry sort key masks the tamper, which holds only for an *order-changing* tamper; an order-preserving rename (`ETH`→`FTH`) defeats it and yields a stale PASS. **An equivalent-mutant ruling is a universal claim, and one non-distinguishing input is not evidence for it.** Why bytecode bit the concordance audit but not this one: Audit A's mutations were mostly line *deletions*, which change file size and invalidate the `.pyc` regardless of mtime.
- **The sharpest pin:** an unparseable `cycle-*.json` scored as a clean pass flips a 14-day journal from `gate_met=False` to `gate_met=True` with `last_failure=None` — a corrupt file becoming a PASS on the artifact authorising real-money trading. Asserted at journal level, since the per-cycle classification passing proves nothing about the gate the operator reads.
- **`replay_fingerprint`'s coverage was a false invariant, closed in two steps.** Spec `00060` D3 called `_REPLAY_CODE_PATHS` "the modules that determine a replay's result". It never was. Spec `00064` D9 added `command.py` + `dataset.py` (`_snapshot_reader` reads every price series a replay sees; switching `close`→`open` changed every verdict at a **byte-identical** fingerprint). Spec `00065` then replaced enumeration entirely with the transitive `cli.*` import closure — measured first: the hand-list covered **12 of 61** executing modules (~20%), so three rounds of "add one more module" were converging on nothing.
- **Adversarial verification caught the closure shipping the same hole one level up.** `_resolve_module` mapped a dotted name to its leaf file only, but importing `cli.engine.command` *executes* `cli/__init__.py` and `cli/engine/__init__.py` first. **Seven** modules ran unhashed on every replay (four ancestor `__init__.py` files plus three `cli/ohlc` submodules reachable only through one of them) — exploitable identically, at a byte-identical fingerprint with all 64 tests green. Fixed (`00065` D10); ancestors are now resolved explicitly.
- **The durable artifact is one test, not the closure.** `test_closure_covers_every_module_the_replay_roots_actually_execute` (`00065` D11) imports the roots in a clean subprocess, reads `sys.modules`, and asserts the closure is a **superset of what actually ran**. It enumerates nothing, so it cannot go stale — and it **failed on the first implementation**, which is what makes it the right pin. Now: 61 covered, 60 executed, **0 executed-but-uncovered**. A subprocess is required; pytest has already imported far more of `cli` than a replay does, so an in-process read passes by over-counting.
- **The pattern this iteration actually taught.** Over twenty defects were found across the work and **every single one was an artifact asserting something untrue** — a spec decision, a test comment, a commit subject, a docstring — never a computation returning a wrong number. **Four separately claimed coverage was complete when it was not**: spec `00060` D3 itself, the D9 commit, spec `00065`'s own goal statement, and `replay_fingerprint`'s docstring. Several originated in the orchestrator's specs and dispatch prompts and were propagated into contexts that falsified them. Prose asserting completeness is unfalsifiable and invites every later reader to assume a check exists; D11 is the first thing here that checks it automatically. Spec `00060` D3 was amended at its root to point at that test, with the instruction that if the two disagree, believe the test.
- **Fix-the-instance, twice more.** Finding 13's pin caught the guard it had been told about; its sibling in the same expression (dropping `not o.mismatch` from `replayed_ok`) survived all 65 tests until pinned. And the D7 label correction landed in the code and spec but left the [[T0075]] bullet *instructing a future worker to restore the wrong label* — a correction that leaves the instruction to undo it is not a correction.
- **Closeout.** [[T0075]] → `resolved` and archived (all 19 pinned; D9 ruled by the owner and implemented rather than deferred; the residual split into [[T0080]] rather than buried). [[T0080]] → `resolved` and archived (option 2 implemented, ancestor gap found and closed). Ratified spec `00060` D3 amended. **Operational note:** the fingerprint change invalidates the whole cache by design — the fleet pays **one** cold rebuild (~627 s vs 55 s warm) on the next NAS pull, once for both changes since they ship together. No trial registered and no budget spent — instrument, not evidence.

## 2026-07-22 — iter-116: the app ships its own logs, and docker.sock leaves the fleet ([[T0089]], [[T0042]], spec 00068)

- **The CLI ships its own logs.** New `cli/logging/ship.py`: an opt-in `--ship-logs` sink that POSTs the existing `JsonLineFormatter` line straight to Grafana Cloud Loki with labels `{host, container, level}` — `level` from `record.levelname`, so **no regex exists anywhere in this path**, at ingest or at query. stdlib only (urllib/threading/collections), no new dependency on the capture image. Env: `ZCRYPTO_LOKI_URL`/`_USERNAME`/`_PASSWORD` + `ZCRYPTO_LOG_HOST`/`_SERVICE`; the flag with any of them unset **or empty** is a hard startup error naming every missing one.
- **stdout is untouched and remains the local ground truth.** The switch is additive: plain-text console exactly as before, docker's json-file driver still capturing it under the existing caps. A crash loses at most the in-memory ring; the last lines survive locally.
- **Bounded, non-blocking, self-announcing.** `emit()` is a deque append under an O(1) lock — all network I/O lives in a daemon worker. Ring 4096, drop **oldest** and count; batches ≤500 or 1 s; one in-flight batch; backoff 1 s → 30 s; exit flush with a 2 s hard deadline. A dead Loki costs one bounded ring and nothing else. **Non-429 4xx drops the batch rather than retrying** — after an outage longer than Loki's out-of-order window the held batch's honest timestamps are rejected forever, so retry-forever would wedge shipping permanently and silently.
- **docker.sock is mounted into nothing, fleet-wide.** All four Alloys lose the socket bind-mount, the docker-group `group_add`, and their `discovery.docker`/`loki.source.docker` components. Alloy's own logs now reach Loki through the **journald log driver** + each host's journal pipeline (a first-time build on the NAS). The T0042 socket residual closes; the T0089 wedge class becomes unrepresentable, because no *foreign* process reads docker's log files any more.
- **The parse stage survives where it is still fed.** Deleted on the capture hosts only; **kept on ops and the NAS**, where the oneshot units and the `archive-pull` wrapper still ship Python-shaped lines through the journal. The honest principle is `direct-ship path regex-free; journal path keeps its one ingest parse` — an earlier draft said "delete it everywhere" and would have permanently blinded `NAS · archive-pull ERROR logs`.
- **Label continuity, so no rule needed re-keying.** `ZCRYPTO_LOG_HOST` renders today's values: `{{ base_hostname }}` on the capture hosts, the literal `ops`/`nas` elsewhere (those Alloys apply static relabels, and the ops rules hardcode `host="ops"`).
- **Alerting changed where the transport changed.** `zcrypto-ops-log-pipeline-dead` was re-scoped to `{container="alloy"}`: the poller now direct-ships lines carrying `host="ops"` and a level, which would have pinned that critical dead-man **green forever** — worse than NoData, because NoData is visible. Two rules added: `zcrypto-ops-poller-log-dead` (nothing watched the new direct-ship path) and `zcrypto-ops-unit-parse-dead` (restores T0060 level-regression coverage at 26 h). `zcrypto-alloy-docker-sd-wedged` retires with its subject, and its `prometheus_sd_*` series leave every keep-list — a keep-list entry for a series that cannot exist is the T0051 admitted-but-unpublished trap.
- **Rollout has NOT happened.** Everything above is repo state. Spec D7's attended steps (vault token in place, per-host converges, the canary bake, the post-gate engine converge, per-step positive-trace verification in Loki, and the `grafana-push.sh` step — which prunes orphaned rules, so it must wait until ops and NAS are actually converged) are the remainder, and the T0089/T0042/T0020 topic updates land with them.
- **Credential.** `logship_loki_token` vaulted in `group_vars/observed/vault.yml` — Grafana Cloud access policy `zcrypto-app-logship`, scope `logs:write` only, distinct from Alloy's token so the app's push can be revoked without blinding fleet telemetry. Only the token is new; the push URL and username are stack properties, reused from the existing `grafana_loki_*`.

## 2026-07-26 — iter-117: every daemon serves `/metrics`, and the rollout completes ([[T0020]], [[T0089]], [[T0042]], [[T0048]], spec 00069)

- **Four daemons expose a Prometheus endpoint.** `cli/obs/metrics.py` builds a registry carrying six `process_*` families and no `python_gc_*`/`python_info`, started only when `ZCRYPTO_METRICS_PORT` is set — absent means exporter-off, so the workstation soak and any ad-hoc run stay silent. The server is loopback-published (`127.0.0.1:<port>:<port>`), and a bind failure logs one ERROR and returns rather than raising: a broken exporter must never take a capture or trade daemon down with it.
- **Per-daemon taps, each with its own hazard closed.** Capture (9101) publishes additive counters plus the disk-watermark gauge, reading snapshot copies so a mid-scrape mutation cannot tear; engine (9102) publishes cycle gauges seeded from the newest journal artifact at startup, so a restart does not fake a fresh cycle; the ops liquidations poller (9103) and the logship sink expose their own counters, absent rather than zero when no handler is configured — absence is honest, zero is a claim.
- **The file-based metrics approach is retired**, and cadvisor is not adopted: the NAS SIGSEGVs, and admitting the whole container-metrics surface for four hosts was rejected on posture and series budget. Alloy's own self-scrape carries the same six `process_*` names instead.
- **Rollout executed and verified, Steps 0–8** (Steps 0–6 on 07-22/23; Step 7 the engine on 07-26 after the Stage-6a gate; Step 8 the same day). The engine step was an image upgrade, not a flag flip — the running image predated `cli.obs` entirely (`ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'cli.obs'`) — and it was deliberately held until the gate measured `streak_days 15`, `status 1`, `mismatch_total 0`.
- **Restart-safe seeding proved on live data, not only in tests**: after the engine converge, `zcrypto_engine_cycle_completed_at_seconds` decoded to 12:01:48 UTC — the preceding 12:00 cycle's completion — rather than process start.
- **The "may publish a different subset" hedge is now measured.** All four hosts publish **nine** `process_*` families on Alloy's self-endpoint; exactly **six** ship. The three the keep-list drops are `process_network_receive_bytes_total`, `process_network_transmit_bytes_total`, `process_virtual_memory_max_bytes`; the shipped `__name__` set is exactly the admitted six, extras leaked: none.
- **Series accounting: 135 new against D9's ≤150 budget** (`capture_app` 82, `engine_app` 16, `liquidations_app` 13, Alloy self-scrape `process_*` 24), versus the ~138 predicted. The budget governs the increment, not per-host totals — a per-host total of 308 on the ops node is not a breach, and the increment was measured directly rather than derived as (total − base), because the sets do not nest (00068 retired the docker-sd metrics).
- **Step 8 retired the last orphan rule.** `zcrypto-alloy-docker-sd-wedged` deleted via `GRAFANA_PRUNE=1` after a dry run confirmed it was the only one; the re-run reports no orphans. That was [[T0048]]'s archive gate.
- **[[T0089]] closed on its own criterion** — all six wedged containers confirmed shipping post-recreation *in Loki*, not inferred from the restarts; the engine was the sixth, cleared by this rollout's Step 7. One measurement trap is recorded there: the engine's post-recreation lines are stamped 13:35:18/13:35:20 while the converge *returned* at 13:35:22, so cutting the query at the deploy command's finish time reads as a failure — the container's own `StartedAt` (13:35:16.873) is the correct marker.
- **[[T0042]] and [[T0048]] closed and archived**; [[T0020]]'s host-label-asymmetry section was rewritten in place (past tense) now that the NAS carries `host="nas"` like the other three hosts, and the archived [[T0079]]'s index clause with it.
- **Operational finding, recorded rather than fixed here:** `primary` and `secondary` appear as `host` label values carrying one series each alongside `zcrypto`/`zcrypto-red` — a second labelling convention somewhere in the fleet. Harmless for the budget; it means `host=` is not uniform.

## 2026-07-26 — the engine journal stops growing forever, and the guard that matters is not about disk ([[T0021]], spec 00070)

- **A daily 01:23 UTC oneshot bounds the VPS engine journal at a 60-day tail** — `zcrypto-engine-journal-prune`, installed by the `engine` role with `ProtectSystem=strict` and `ReadWritePaths` limited to the journal dir (plus, since spec `00071`, the textfile dir it publishes into), so a bug in its own globs *structurally* cannot reach the trade key or the capture spool. Timer enabled, service left `static` (enabling it too would prune on every boot).
- **Retention was never a capacity problem, and saying so out loud changed the design.** 13 MB/day against 51 GB free is ~11 years of headroom. With no deadline forcing a cheap choice, the design could pay any amount of caution — which is how the real hazard got found instead of a `find -mtime +14 -delete` shipping.
- **The load-bearing guard protects order generation, not disk.** `cli/engine/cycle.py` derives each cycle's orders as a delta against the most recent journaled cycle; with no prior record `prev_targets is None`, every delta becomes the full target, and the engine emits orders to rebuild the entire book ("the shadow book starts flat"). An age-only prune reaches that state whenever the engine stops for longer than the retention window — every day ages out at once. So deletion requires **two** conditions: older than the cutoff **and** not among the newest N day-dirs. They coincide in healthy operation and come apart exactly when the floor is the only thing preventing a spurious whole-book rebuild. Recorded caveat: the floor counts day-*dirs*, so 60 consecutive days of all-failed cycles could still strand the lookup — not guarded, because any guard would key on the records the outage is failing to produce.
- **"Prune after verified pull" resolved to margin-plus-monitoring, not a handshake.** The architecture is pull-only (`rrsync -ro`, spec `00051` D10), so a NAS→VPS acknowledgement would mean opening a write-capable path *into the trade-key host*. Rejected: disk hygiene is not worth weakening that boundary. A day instead survives ~1,440 hourly pull opportunities under a 6 h lag alert — and the precondition was **verified rather than argued**: the oldest day on the host (`2026-07-11`) measured 127 files / 12,553,096 bytes on the VPS and byte-identically on the NAS.
- **The first review found the tests green and hollow.** Fixtures planted enough recent days that the keep-newest floor satisfied every assertion on its own — so the age cutoff could be *deleted outright* and all 21 tests still passed, as could either regex anchor and `-type d`. Four cases were added and the suite is now mutation-checked: all five mutations fail it. The lesson is the general one — a passing suite is evidence only about the fixtures it was given.
- **Observability was got wrong here, and is corrected in spec `00071` ([[T0100]]).** The reasoning recorded at the time — app metrics come from `/metrics` endpoints Alloy scrapes, a oneshot has no process to scrape, these hosts run no textfile collector, therefore publish nothing — was true at every step and did not follow. *The correct response to "no reader exists" is to add the reader.* The fallback claimed instead was false too: the journal keep-regex admitted only `zcrypto-capture-prune.service`, so the log line never reached Loki and the prune spent its first day observable through **nothing**. Superseded text follows, kept only because the reasoning is instructive: App metrics are now scraped from live `/metrics` endpoints; a oneshot that runs for a second has nothing to scrape. The fleet's mechanism for ephemeral timers is the textfile collector, which the ops node runs and the capture/engine hosts deliberately do not. Stated honestly rather than papered over: **nothing detects a timer that silently stops** — the spool-disk alert covers this filesystem but sits ~9 years away at 13 MB/day, so it backstops disk exhaustion, not the timer. Accepted because over-deletion is the dangerous failure and the keep-newest floor, not an alert, is what guards it. That alert's summary now names both prune timers.
- **Retention is 60 days.** The owner raised it from 14 during implementation, *between* the two converges — the first deploy ran at 14. The design is value-independent — the cutoff and the floor read the same parameter — so only numbers moved: steady state ~780 MB (1.5% of free space), ~1,440 pull opportunities of margin. A test now pins the deployed default against the spec, so the next drive-by edit has to change the ruling too.
- **Deployed attended and verified by outcome**, converged at 14 and re-converged at 60: `changed=4` then `changed=1`, with compose and the engine systemd unit `ok` in both, so no restart handler ever fired — confirmed by the container's `StartedAt` being byte-identical across both converges with `RestartCount` 0. The deployed command was read back from systemd's own resolved `argv[]`, not just the unit file.
- **The prune was proven on real data before the value changed**, which is the more informative record. At 14 a host `--dry-run` returned exactly the predicted `deleted=1 kept=15 cutoff="2026-07-12 UTC"` — one eligible day, the day sitting exactly *on* the cutoff correctly spared. At 60 it returns `deleted=0 kept=16 cutoff="2026-05-27 UTC"`, so **the timer is a verified no-op until ~2026-09-10**, when the oldest day first ages out.

## 2026-07-26 — the capture VPSes stop rebooting themselves, and one-off timers get a metrics path ([[T0027]], [[T0100]], spec 00071)

- **`Automatic-Reboot "false"` on both capture VPSes.** Patches still auto-install; the reboot is a human act. `zcrypto-ops` still reads `"true"` at 02:25 — untouched, because the role default preserves today's behaviour and only `group_vars/capture_host` overrides it. Three lines, and one footgun worth recording: a bare YAML `false` renders through Jinja as Python's `False`, emitting `Automatic-Reboot "False"` — accepted by apt as not-true *by accident*, invisible to lint, to `--check --diff`, and to any on-host grep phrased as "is it false". Both values are quoted strings and a test proves the unquoted form breaks. Nothing in `tests/` covered `roles/base` before this.
- **The recorded guideline's transport did not exist.** T0027's 2026-07-23 design called for the detector to write to "the same `integrations/unix` transport that already carries the NAS `gate.prom` — no regex, no new plumbing". The two hosts it targets ran **no textfile collector**, and `integrations/unix` is static-mode Grafana Agent vocabulary this flow-mode fleet does not use. Building the transport became its own defect, [[T0100]].
- **The collector had been dropped for a contingent reason that expired.** Spec `00069` moved long-lived services to `/metrics` endpoints, correctly; the capture-host Alloy shed its textfile collector alongside, stating why: *"there is no gate/replay/archive timer on them."* A one-off runs for a second and has no process to scrape, so textfile is the right transport for that class — which is exactly why the ops node kept its collector. The premise died the day a one-off timer landed. The rule is now written into the config: long-lived → `/metrics`, one-off → `.prom`.
- **It had already cost [[T0021]] everything.** That prune shipped publishing nothing, reasoning that no collector would read it — true, and the wrong conclusion; the fix is to add the reader. The fallback claimed in its place was false too: the journal keep-regex admitted only `zcrypto-capture-prune.service`, so its log line never reached Loki. For its first day the prune was observable through **nothing**. Both halves fixed; its metrics now reach Grafana Cloud.
- **No compose edit and no volume change** — the material simplification. Alloy already bind-mounts `/:/host/root:ro`, and the collector's `directory` is just a path it globs, unrelated to `rootfs_path`. But it *does* need a container recreate, which the spec originally got wrong: `config.alloy` is a **single-file** bind mount, so `copy:` writes a new inode the running container never sees, and no role in the fleet restarts Alloy — deliberately. The converge lands the config; a human recreates.
- **Three ways a `.prom` fails, and each defeats the rule that catches the others.** *Stale* — the collector serves the last values forever and `node_textfile_scrape_error` does not fire (it covers malformed input only). *Unreadable* — it does. *Absent entirely* — neither can, because a staleness rule cannot fire on a series that does not exist and `noDataState: OK` renders that silence green. So one rule became four: per-publisher `node_textfile_mtime_seconds` (1 h for the 15-minute probe, 26 h for the daily prune — one shared window would have let the attended-reboot net sit dead for a day), `scrape_error`, and `count(node_reboot_required) < 2`.
- **The review caught the defect reproducing itself one layer down, before deploy.** `mktemp` creates `0600` and `mv` *preserves* it, so the retro-fixed prune published root-only while Alloy reads as non-root — measured `600` against the new detector's `644`, because the `chmod` was written into the new script, with a comment explaining exactly why, and not into the one being retro-fixed. It would have shipped inert, and the staleness rule could not have surfaced it: the collector skips an unreadable file *before* stamping its mtime, so there was no series to go stale. The allow-list was also admitting three of the prune's four series; `oldest_day_age_seconds` was being dropped at remote-write — the exact trap the spec claims to close. It is now derived from what the script actually emits, not a hand-kept list.
- **Four tests passed for the wrong reason**, all found by the same review. The atomicity test asserted only that no temp file was left behind — which a truncate-in-place rewrite satisfies trivially, so it passed *because* the safety mechanism was removed; now asserted by inode. The producer/reader test passed with `"textfile"` removed from `set_collectors`. The timer-enable test split on the last of three occurrences and never inspected the task it named. The `.timer` file had no content coverage at all.
- **Ordering was load-bearing, not cosmetic.** T0027's own verification step — touch and remove the flag — would have rebooted the live capture + engine primary had it run while `Automatic-Reboot` was still `"true"`, since a present `/run/reboot-required` is precisely what unattended-upgrades acts on. The flip landed and was verified first, and the precondition was re-checked on-host in the same command that touched the flag.
- **Deployed attended in three scoped converges, verified by outcome.** `--tags base` (`changed=1` per host, one line), `--tags capture` with the running digests (`changed=6`), `--tags engine` (`changed=2`) — capture `StartedAt` and engine `StartedAt` byte-identical across all three, `RestartCount` 0. Then read back from Grafana Cloud rather than inferred: `zcrypto_engine_journal_prune_kept_days{zcrypto}=16`, `oldest_day_age_seconds=1377233`, `node_reboot_required` 0 on both hosts flipping to 1 and back under a real flag touch, `node_textfile_mtime_seconds` carrying the `file` label all three staleness rules key on, and `node_textfile_scrape_error` 0 on all four hosts.

## 2026-07-26 — the fault signals nothing was watching ([[T0008]], [[T0100]])

- **Six alert rules, for metrics that had been live and unwatched.** [[T0008]]'s own trigger was the starting point — spec `00069` shipped `zcrypto_capture_book_desynced` and `zcrypto_capture_resubscribes_total` on both hosts and `alerts.yaml` queried neither — but writing the guard test first turned up four more, and the worst of them was not on anyone's list.
- **A breached disk watermark discards data, and only a dead-man knew.** `cli/capture/command.py`'s book and trade handlers `continue`/`return` past the writers while `watermark.breached` holds, so incoming L2 and trades are **dropped**, unbackfillable. It also withholds the healthcheck ping — so hc.io pages *"this host went quiet"*, not *"capture is discarding"*, and nothing could tell those apart. Now `critical` at 2 min.
- **`min_over_time`, not the `max_over_time` the topic proposed.** `max` fires on any desync inside the window including the transient the daemon self-heals 234/234 times; `min` requires the pair desynced for the *whole* window — the stuck shape, which is what "past its single resubscribe" means. Recovery is one fire-and-forget resubscribe fired only on the `not was_desynced` transition, so a failed attempt is never retried.
- **Measurement changed a decision, which is the point of taking it first.** `zcrypto_capture_reconnects_total` looked like an obvious fourth rule until it measured **32–35 per host per week** — baseline, not a fault; a naive threshold would have paged ~5×/day. Excluded with the number written down. [[T0035]]'s trigger is a counter *reset* correlated with a `process_start_time_seconds` jump, which needs the correlation rather than a count.
- **Review caught a rule that would have misfired during the absence.** `increase()` extrapolates to the window edges, so `increase(counter[1d]) > 1` returns ~1.0007 for a **single** increment at any nonzero sample offset — one genuine self-healed venue desync would have paged, asserted a re-elevating rate that was not happening, and stayed firing a full day. Reproduced numerically; threshold is 1.5. The same alarm fatigue this file already killed once by deleting the `Gate · not MET` rule.
- **The guard is derived from the keep-regex, not hand-listed** — 46 admitted series, 38 excluded each with a written reason, 8 requiring a rule. A hand-list cannot catch the *next* unwatched metric, which is precisely the mechanism that let these sit for two months. Matching is word-boundary: `node_load1` is a strict prefix of `node_load15` and both are admitted, so a substring test let one satisfy the other.
- **One rule exists to keep the others honest.** `node_scrape_collector_success` = 0 means a collector errored and its entire family goes *absent* — and NoData maps to `OK` on this fleet, so a failed `filesystem` collector silently disarms the disk-low rule and a failed `textfile` collector disarms every one-off timer alert.

## 2026-07-26 — internal vocabulary off the operator's screen, enforced by a test ([[T0096]])

- **The rule is `.claude/rules/operator-facing-text.md`**: `Phase <N>`, `T<NNNN>`, `iter-<NNN>` and `spec <NNNNN>` never reach a surface visible **without opening the repo**. Source comments, docstrings, `docs/` and commit messages stay untouched, where those tokens are load-bearing.
- **A test, not a sweep.** A sweep has to be re-run by whoever remembers it exists; `tests/test_internal_terms_not_operator_visible.py` runs on every PR. That also settles the topic's open question about a pre-commit guard: a hook would add noise for a check the suite already performs.
- **Two rounds, and the second was three times the first.** The initial pass found **23** leaks against an estimated 14. Adversarial review then showed the *detector* had blind spots, and widening it found **25 more** — 48 sites, 62 token instances, across eight surfaces. The method was the work; the sweep was mechanical.
- **The three blind spots are each a false-all-clear waiting to happen.** *Indirection*: a message built into a variable and echoed later is invisible to any call-site walk, and chasing it statically is dataflow analysis — so the detector checks every **non-docstring literal** instead, which costs four findings and no false positives. *Line wrapping*: Rich breaks the `--help` column at ~46 chars, so `spec 00052` split across a line was invisible to a regex expecting the space. *A too-greedy path carve-out*: `spec 00054/T0058` is two tokens joined by a slash, not a path — and that exact string was in this repo, in a `Description=` that only failed on its **other** token.
