- **Closeout.** [[T0112]] resolved and archived, both sub-items closed — detection (the ratio gate) and the tail-depth diagnostic — with all three options the topic had suggested explicitly disposed of rather than left open: raising `MIN_POOL` to the k=2 bound and the robust-quantile estimator are both spec `00079` Non-goals (the first refuses more and catches less; the second stays declined because a refusal is explainable and an iterative estimator is not), and annotating-instead-of-refusing was rejected in D4. **The D6 residual is a recorded conscious drop, not a deferral, and no successor topic exists**: at k ≥ ~0.01·n the p99 itself is contaminated, both ratios read ≈1 and the gate is blind — constructed, not estimated, blindness begins at exactly k=115 for n = 11,389 (k=114 is still caught, second ratio 53.4). It is accepted on the absurdity of the regime, which requires the measured window to be **77–97 % outage** by wall time, with the on-screen `n` beside the window's span as the eyeball check. The truncated-hours count is explicitly **not** a backstop — it reads `secs > thresh`, so the same contaminated threshold blinds it too — and the drop is recorded at the gate in the source as well as in the topic. No decisions-log entry owed: this iteration's decisions are engineering, not subject-matter research.

## 2026-08-02 — reach-review residuals closed, with two of the topic's own premises refuted (spec 00080, T0098 resolved)

- **The proposed detection signature was false for 2 of the 7 rebuild targets, so the shipped cleanup uses no signature at all.** [[T0098]] asserted that a missing `manifest.json` reliably marks a partial run. Measured against `REBUILDABLE`'s **seven** keys — by import, not by grep, after a grep-based first pass at this very question missed three targets and invented a fourth: true for **five** (the three `ohlc-*` targets plus `derivatives-funding` and `derivatives-oi`), and **false for `snapshots`** (whose builder writes only `kraken-refdata-<UTC stamp>.json`) and **false for `universe`** (which writes only `point-in-time-universe.json` and merely *reads* the OHLC set's manifest — the read is what makes a naive grep call it a writer). Both fixes the topic proposed — clean up a manifest-less sibling, or make the retry replace one — would therefore have classified those two targets' **completed** output as partial garbage and deleted it. What shipped instead: on any builder failure `rebuild_sets` deletes the whole just-minted sibling (`shutil.rmtree`), uniformly for all seven targets, needing no per-target machinery and resting on no signature. It is sound for a reason the old empty-only `rmdir` obscured — the exists-guard raises *before* `mkdir` when the directory pre-exists, so every byte under `out_root` was written by the invocation that just raised, and the "never delete builder output" caveat was protecting a directory this function does not mint, which is unreachable past that guard. The handler catches `BaseException` and re-raises, so an operator's Ctrl-C during a ~90 s paced round cleans up exactly like a builder error, pinned by a test so nobody narrows it back. The strand was reproduced against unmodified code before the fix existed: a builder that writes then raises stranded the date-stamped sibling and the same-day retry hit `sibling already exists` forever.
- **The hard-kill residual is an accepted conscious drop, and the guard message is the whole mitigation.** SIGKILL or power loss skips every handler, so a sibling can still strand. Rather than a per-builder completion-witness protocol — machinery whose only consumer is the case `rmtree` already covers — the `sibling already exists` refusal now tells the operator that the directory is either a completed sibling from earlier today or the leavings of a run killed mid-build, and to inspect and remove it to retry. Plain language, no internal tokens, since it is runtime output of `cli/`. Recorded in the code comment beside the handler and in the archived topic; **no successor topic exists and none is owed.**
- **`PAIR_KEYS` has a single binding, and it is now `cli/ohlc/fetch.py`.** It lived in `cli/engine/store.py` only because that was its first consumer, which forced `cli/ohlc/reach.py` to import upward into `cli.engine`. It is Kraken-REST reference data (display asset → REST pair key) consumed to call `fetch_ohlc`, and both users already import that module, so the move added no module and no import edge. The store imports and re-exports it, leaving all four downstream import sites and `cli/engine/__init__.py`'s re-export untouched with zero test changes. Importing `cli.ohlc.reach` no longer loads `cli.engine` at all, which **dissolves** the "`cli/ohlc/__init__.py` must never import `reach`" half-initialised-package trap instead of documenting it — the inversion comment that existed only to describe the problem is deleted with it.
- **`cli/ohlc/seam.py` is now the seam definition's single home, and the guard policies are deliberately NOT there.** The topic proposed extracting a helper and deleting both duplicate copies; deleting them is **not a pure refactor**, which measurement showed rather than argument: the two guard blocks raise different exception types (`OHLCError` vs `EngineError`), their message prefixes are test-pinned on both sides (`seam too thin` / `seam mismatch` in `tests/test_ohlc_reach.py`, `shortfall` / `mismatch` in `tests/test_engine_store.py`), reach's mismatch message carries both close values where store's carries neither, reach has a detached branch store lacks, and store has an `allow_replace` poisoned-tail path reach lacks — so unifying them would either change operator-visible test-pinned messages or drop the close-values diagnostic, a behaviour change wearing a refactor's clothes. Extracted instead is exactly what was identical: `MIN_SEAM_OVERLAP = 6` (one binding; `_SEED_MIN_OVERLAP` deleted), `drop_in_progress` (the two bodies were byte-identical), and `seam_overlap(left, right) -> (overlap_bars, mismatches)` — the join-on-`ts` and close-comparison both guards open with, which is the surface a future safety fix must hit in both callers and therefore the exact drift risk the topic registered. Each guard keeps a docstring cross-pointer to its sibling so the divergence cannot later be mistaken for an oversight. Mutation-proved rather than assumed: corrupting `seam_overlap`'s comparison fails the pinned mismatch tests in **both** test files, proving both callers really route through the shared definition.
- **Behaviour-neutrality was proven at the wiring level, not asserted.** The full suite is green at **2755 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped** with zero edits to existing test assertions, and `zcrypto engine soak-check` produced **byte-identical stdout and exit code 0** before and after the branch on the same local inputs (diffed against a baseline captured at `develop`'s tip before the first commit — 17 lines, the deterministic `NO VERDICT -- no journaled cycles found` path). That probe exists for the CLI → soak → store *wiring* the refactor moves through; the arithmetic is pinned separately by `tests/test_engine_soak.py`'s fixtures.
- **No deploy in this iteration, and the next attended engine converge ships this refactor.** Merging to `develop` cannot reach production: the engine runs pinned at `e5a44e1cb138`, its container unrestarted since 2026-07-26. But the change touches `cli/engine/store.py` and `cli/engine/soak.py`'s import path — the instrument that reads the ~40-day soak at the go/no-go — so the converge session must know it carries this change (spec `00080` D6). The freeze that parked two of the three sub-items had genuinely lifted: Stage 6a CLOSED 2026-07-30, verdict PASS. No dataset introduced and no decisions-log entry owed — this iteration's decisions are engineering, not subject-matter research.

## 2026-08-02 — the two Stage-6b feeder measurements, answered (spec 00081, T0117 + T0118 resolved)

- **Where the book's ~5 % gross goes is now decomposed rather than observed, and the topic's framing was a false binary whose neither branch was the answer.** Measured by `zcrypto engine decompose` over **136 journaled cycles, 2026-07-11 → 2026-08-02** on the NAS replica — medians of per-cycle values, every consecutive-stage ratio itself a median of per-cycle ratios rather than a ratio of stage medians, since the two diverge the moment the multiplier varies and a ratio of medians divides two numbers that need not come from the same cycle. The chain: A2 sleeve gross **32.224 %** of NAV (min 7.423, max 54.853) → **÷3 by the fixed ⅓ combination weights** → **10.741 %** combined → position caps bind on **0 of 136** cycles, a pure no-op → **×0.5** governor, constant across the whole window → **5.371 %** final gross. [[T0117]] asked whether ~5 % was the vol-target pipeline's intended output or in-window governor derating; the governor *is* derating, but that is a factor of 2 out of a factor of ~6, and the missing factor was in neither branch. **No residual is left over** — spec `00081`'s Risks anticipated one and there is none. The "naive 15–20 %" was a per-sleeve vol target mistaken for a book gross target: A2 alone runs *above* it, and the entire gap to the observed figure is the ⅓ combination times the ×0.5 governor, both exactly accounted.
- **The spec's own D3 hypothesis is refuted, and it was the iteration's own prediction.** D3 made the cancellation term first-class on the reasoning that ⅓-weighting nets opposing sleeve positions away asset by asset, and called it *"the likeliest answer"*. The measured cancellation ratio is **1.000** on every one of the 136 cycles — nothing cancels, because **B and A1 hold float-exactly zero in all 136**, so there is nothing to cancel against. The exactness belongs to the zero, not to the ratio: the ratio's numerator sums per-element `⅓·x` while its denominator divides a summed gross by 3, two float paths that differ by an ulp on roughly a third of books, so it is 1.000 at reporting precision rather than bit-exactly 1.0. An earlier draft of this entry claimed the ratio itself was exact — checkably false, and corrected here rather than left to read as a measurement. Recorded rather than quietly dropped, because the report still carries the term and a later reader would otherwise read 1.000 as a finding about sleeve *agreement* instead of about sleeve *dormancy*. A separate ratio in the same table IS bit-exact and should not be confused with this one: `capped_ratio` sums numerator and denominator off the same builder floats, pinned by an `== 1.0` assert an approximate comparison would have let through. And a flat cycle's `0/0` stays NaN, filtered out of the median rather than counted as agreement it never showed.
- **The measurement found what it was not looking for, and it is registered as [[T0124]] rather than folded away.** The dormancy is not a defect — both sleeves were live for much of the backtest (B non-zero on **51.4 %** of 28,079 rows, last non-zero ≈ **2025-10-30**; A1 on **57.0 %**, ≈ **2025-11-09**), and they are long-only sleeves correctly sitting out a sustained downtrend, with B behind the 200-day SMA gate `13.phase5-holdout-ledger.md` already records as off. What no document recorded is the **compound consequence**: with fixed ⅓ weights a single live sleeve contributes one third of its own gross and the governor then halves that, so what Stage 6b would take live is structurally a **one-sleeve book at ⅙ of A2's gross**, not the diversified three-sleeve system whose Sharpe 1.5609 / maxDD 13.57 % / stress figures record 44 registered. It is also the mechanism behind the placeability problem previously attributed to sizing alone, and **nothing observes it** — no metric states how many sleeves contribute, so a re-arming would be as silent as the onset was. It is an owner call on rung 3's premise, not a research question, which is why it is a topic and not a paragraph. *(Ruled the same day: **proceed knowingly**, three conditions — the go/no-go baseline becomes the conditional live-state band (~0.5–0.75, CI spanning zero; the first-cut 0.47 was diluted by fully-flat bars), the amendment states the divergence, and the sleeve-occupancy gauge is a rung-3 precondition. The ruling, its quantification and the rejected alternatives are in T0124's `Done so far` and the `[iter-118]` decisions-log entry.)*
- **The pre-registered tracking band is confirmed structurally unmeetable at rung 3's size — with numbers now, not an argument.** `zcrypto engine accum-replay` simulates the accumulate-until-placeable policy over the same 136 cycles at five NAV rungs. Drift floor per cycle, in bps of NAV: €500 → median **147.0** / p95 209.2 (placed on 14/136 cycles); **€1,000 → 51.2 / 115.7** (29/136); €2,500 → 11.6 / 30.9 (40/136); €5,000 → 6.2 / 14.2 (50/136); €10,000 → 4.3 / 8.9 (64/136). Against §12's `weekly |live − sim| ≤ 10 bps of NAV`, rung 3's €1,000 sits at **5.1× the band at the median and 11.6× at p95**; the band only becomes meetable around **€5,000 (median 6.2) / €10,000 (p95 8.9)** — the far end of the ramp, not the tiny-live size the stage specifies. 10 bps is not a wrong number, it is a number that presupposes a book roughly an order of magnitude larger than the one rung 3 funds. The four weekly means at €1,000 are 94.5 (W28, partial) / 50.3 (W29) / 68.0 (W30) / 32.4 (W31, partial).
- **No weekly p95 is reported, and that omission is a deliverable rather than a gap.** The window is exactly four ISO weeks, two of them partial; a p95 over four points is the maximum wearing a percentile's name, and this number is destined for a live-trading gate band. Every week prints with its cycle count and a partial flag; the per-cycle median and p95 over all 136 points carry the statistical weight. Two more properties are load-bearing and were chosen against plausible alternatives: held state is carried in **base units**, never EUR — `ordermin` is natively a quantity so the floor comparison is direct, and a EUR-denominated held state would compare a price-stale "held" against a freshly priced "target" and report zero drift across a pure price move (pinned by a fixture whose held position is **nonzero**, since at `held_qty = 0` the close cancels and both designs agree byte for byte) — and `ordermin`/`costmin` are **independent gates, not a max over mixed units**, so a delta clearing 50 DOGE but worth €0.10 is refused where a max would have placed it.
- **Reading the minimums correctly was itself a measured trap, twice, and both forms produce a wrong floor rather than an error.** They come from the snapshot's `universe` block, not `raw.assetpairs` — there is **no `DOGE/EUR` wsname** there at all (Kraken lists `XDG/EUR`, and BTC as `XBT/EUR`), so an `<ASSET>/EUR` match silently loses assets — and the `quote == "EUR"` filter is load-bearing, because `universe` also carries `ETH/BTC` and `SOL/BTC` at `costmin 0.00002`, so keying by `base` alone overwrites their EUR floors with a BTC number read as euros. Both are tests. Every figure above rests on `kraken-refdata-20260707T032900Z.json`, **`fetched_at 2026-07-07T03:29:00+00:00`**, printed beside the drift table on every run — Kraken owns these values and moves them without notice, and a band quoted at the go/no-go from a stale table is exactly the silent-staleness failure [[T0113]] exists to prevent.
- **Both identity guards were proven to bite on real data, with distinct messages.** The internal identity (`multiplier × capped_recomputed[a] == final_targets[a]`, exactly, per asset) catches the builder's combination or caps changing under the harness; the journal identity (rebuilt forming-row targets equal the **journaled** `final_targets`) catches what the internal one structurally cannot — a rebuild that is self-consistent but describes a book the engine never traded. They fail with different text (`stage identity broken` vs `replay disagrees with the journal`), so the two failure modes are distinguishable at the point of reading, and the clean 136-cycle run exits 0. A record that fails to replay is **named and counted in the output**, never skipped, and one that fails to *parse* aborts rather than being dropped, since it cannot even be named per cycle downstream — either way no aggregate is ever computed over a quietly smaller window.
- **Two read-only subcommands over one module, no builder change and no live-path change.** `cli/engine/feeders.py` holds `decompose_report` + `accumulation_report`, exposed as `zcrypto engine decompose` and `zcrypto engine accum-replay`, both `--journal-dir`-driven and mirroring `soak-check`'s config-defaulted `(text, payload)` shape; README Usage landed in the same commit. `--json` sanitizes before dumping — non-finite floats become `null`, mapping keys become strings, with `allow_nan=False` as the self-check — because NaN is *legitimate* here (a flat cycle's `0/0` cancellation ratio must not masquerade as 1.0) and `json.dumps` would otherwise emit the bare `NaN` token that most non-Python parsers reject. The accumulation policy is **simulated**, not implemented: building it is [[T0119]]'s work inside [[T0018]]'s iteration.
- **Closeout.** [[T0117]] and [[T0118]] resolved and archived, all six sub-items closed, each topic's Resolution carrying the numbers themselves rather than a pointer to them — T0118's including the `fetched_at` stamp, for the reason above. [[T0116]] gains both parameters in its `## Findings so far`: **5.371 %** median final gross with the chain that produces it as the expected-gross parameter for rungs 2–3, the full NAV curve as the re-registered band's basis, the note that [[T0124]] now bears on rung 3's premise, and — carried out of spec prose into the place where the gate is actually written — the **re-derivation trigger**: the floor is derived from *shadow* targets, so if rung 1/2 execution changes the target series materially the band wants re-deriving before rung 3's gate reads it. T0118's third sub-item asked for a sensitivity check against T0117's gross answer on the premise that a 3× larger book would shrink the relative floor ~3×; **no correction is owed** — T0117 measured that there is no hidden larger book, and the replay consumed the very same journaled `final_targets` T0117 decomposed. No deploy owed (both commands are read-only over a pulled journal replica and ship in no timer), no dataset introduced, and no decisions-log entry owed: this iteration **measured** rather than decided, and the subject-matter decisions these numbers feed — the re-registered band, the ladder amendment, and the one-sleeve go/no-go call — are T0116's and the owner's, to be logged when they are taken.

## 2026-08-02 — the §10 whole-book limits get a caller, and two pre-live settings get their real values (iter-119, T0120 + T0121 + T0122 partial)

- **Three of §10's four whole-book limits stopped being dead code.** `apply_gross_leverage_cap`, `apply_net_exposure_band`, and `apply_margin_floor` were implemented, unit-tested, and called by nothing outside their own test file — the risk layer every design document treats as standing between the book and a margin blow-up was enforcing one quarter of itself. They now run in both target builders (the verified path and the bit-identical fast path) through one shared helper, at the ruled order `combined → apply_position_caps → gross_leverage_cap → net_exposure_band → margin_floor → × governor`. **The governor stays outermost, and that placement is the load-bearing part**: it is a pure returns overlay by construction, so clamping after it would silently re-scale the multiplier's own effect and partially undo a de-levering governor. Costing moved to the limited book in the same change, so the governor is fed the returns of the book that would actually trade rather than of one the limits would have rejected.
- **Order among the three is deliberately unpinned, and the tests say so.** All three are proportional whole-book scalings, so on a breaching fixture the ruled order and its reverse produce the same book. What is pinned is the observable part — the stack sits after the (non-proportional) per-asset caps and inside the governor. No order-sensitivity property was asserted, because none holds; asserting one would have manufactured a test that passes for the wrong reason.
- **Verdict-neutrality was measured, and then separately proven not to be vacuous** — the two are different claims and only the second distinguishes correct-and-inert wiring from inert code. Neutrality: the committed frozen figures are unmoved (Sharpe 1.5609, maxDD 0.1357/0.1866, `cap_breach_bars` 1318, `governor_engaged_bars` 7302), and all **137** journaled cycles replay with no stage-identity break, **0 of 137** cap-bound. Nothing can bind because the **capped** book — the operand the whole-book limits actually see, since they run before the governor multiply — has a median gross of **10.74 %** against a 150 % soft cap. (The familiar ~5.4 % is the *post-governor* final gross, a different book: 10.740 × 0.500 = 5.370 on the median cycle. Quoting the post-governor figure as the limits' operand is an easy and wrong shortcut — it makes the choke prediction below irreproducible.) Non-vacuity: choking `soft_cap` to 0.01 then fails *every* replayed cycle, and on the cycle checked in full the scale factor lands where predicted — 0.13724 measured against 0.13725 predicted, that cycle's capped gross being 7.286 %. The factor is per-cycle, not one constant across the 137.
- **The wiring exposed a worse defect than the one it was sent to fix, and it is the reason this iteration matters beyond bookkeeping.** Three recomputations *outside* the builder — `cli/engine/feeders.py`'s stage-identity check and two sites in `cli/engine/soak.py` — rebuilt the book with `apply_position_caps` alone. They agreed with the builder only while nothing bound. The first binding limit would have raised `stage identity broken` and dropped `reconcile_ok` to False: **a tooling false-positive firing precisely when the risk layer does its job**, accusing the engine at the moment it is correct. That inversion is worse than a silent clamp, because it trains an operator to distrust the alarm. All book-rebuilding sites now share the one public helper.
- **Three cap-breach mirrors were deliberately NOT moved to the limited book, and the reasoning is the non-obvious half.** `cap_bound` in `cli/engine/feeders.py`, `cap_breach` in `_net_live_from_result`, and the `breach` term in `realized_internals` all mirror `cap_breach_bars`, which the builder computes *before* the limits run — so applying the stack to them would have **introduced** a divergence rather than removed one. Only one of the three is a whole call site left untouched (`realized_internals`); the other two are the breach term inside a site whose *other* quantities did move to the limited book, which is why the split had to be made per-quantity rather than per-file. The implementer pushed back on the brief on exactly this point and was right; review then re-traced every site in both directions against the specific builder variable it mirrors, rather than accepting either party's wording.
- **`spot_positions_quote_currency` is `"ZEUR"`, and the near-miss is the durable lesson**: the wrong value fails *silently*. The adapter compares this string literally against the instrument's quote-currency `code`. Measured against the real loaded Kraken spot universe — 1,592 instruments — **546 carry code `ZEUR`, 0 carry `EUR`**, 31 carry `XXBT`, 814 `ZUSD`. The trap is that the instrument *ID* renders as `ADA/EUR.KRAKEN`, because `normalize_spot_symbol` renames the **symbol**, while the quote `Currency` keeps Kraken's legacy `Z` prefix. So the plausible `"EUR"` matches nothing — an empty position report indistinguishable from a flat book, the same failure as the `"USDT"` default it replaces. A first implementation did set `"EUR"`, inferring from `normalize_currency_code`'s existence in the adapter without checking whether that code path reaches the quote currency; it does not. Caught by review, then reproduced independently by two further agents before the value changed. The pinning test cites the measured histogram rather than any normalization claim, so a future plausible-looking "correction" back to `"EUR"` fails.
- **`default_leverage` is left unset on purpose — a ruling, not an omission.** The adapter treats `None` as cash (no leverage sent), and the node already runs `spot_account_type=AccountType.MARGIN`, which is what enables per-order leverage via `SubmitOrder.params={"leverage": N}`. A global default would silently lever **every** ordinary rebalance of a book §10 caps at 1.5×/2.0× *gross*; the master plan rules the book's gross cap and does not rule a per-order default. Rung 1's T2 tax probe still gets its margin long and margin short by passing leverage per order. Recorded because "unset" and "not yet decided" look identical in a config file.
- **Known limitations recorded rather than built for**: `spot_positions_quote_currency` takes a *single* quote currency, so the EUR book and the `XXBT`-quoted `ETH/BTC` / `SOL/BTC` pairs cannot both be covered by it; and under `spot_account_type=MARGIN` the adapter takes the `OpenPositions` branch and never reads the field at all — it becomes live only via a CASH fallback or `use_spot_position_reports`. Set anyway, precisely because the failure mode when it does become live is silent.
- **The production kill criteria got their first ratified number, and the second criterion changed shape instead.** The DD floor is **20 % governed drawdown** — retire, not de-lever — derived from the deployed ladder `((0.075, 0.5), (0.11, 0.25), (0.15, 0.0))` against record 44's registered governed maxDD of 0.1357: 1.47× that maxDD and five points past a terminal rung that *re-arms*, so reaching it means the governor already went flat and the book lost five further points, a state the validated system never entered. 18 % was rejected as inside the ladder's working range — one failed re-arm cycle reaches it, so the criterion would retire the system for the ladder working as designed.
- **A kill on live Sharpe provably cannot fire, which is why §12's "Ongoing" register was never merely missing a threshold.** With the IID standard error of an annualized Sharpe over `T` years at `sqrt((1 + S²/2)/T)`, the T0124 live-state baseline (governed Sharpe ≈ 0.63 extended / 0.75 registered-window) yields a 95 % half-width of **±2.15 after a full year**, and needs **8.2 years** one-sided or **11.6 years** two-sided to separate from zero; even the 1.5609 headline needs 2.5–3.5 years. The statistic is therefore the **paired per-cycle live-minus-simulation difference**: same signal, same bars, so the common market factor cancels and the residual is execution error alone — decidable in weeks where the standalone quantity is not. This is the faithful reading of §12's existing "beyond CI vs its own simulation" wording, so **no master-plan amendment is owed**. The bound itself is registered with its trigger rather than guessed, because it cannot be estimated from shadow data, where nothing fills and the paired difference is identically zero by construction.
- **The recurring routines register on their topic, not in `infra/runbooks/README.md`.** That file's scope is procedures for a signal that fires at an operator, and every section owes a checkable `Retire when`; a monthly routine with no live signal behind it would be the backlog the runbook bans by name. `T0049` gained the bullet that makes its runbook build carry them, exactly as `T0113`'s monthly ⏱ sweep is registered.
- **Still open on these topics, and deliberately so**: a limit-breach counter (joins T0018's order/PnL `/metrics` families rather than minting its own — today a bind is invisible in the result type, since `cap_breach_bars` counts per-asset breaches only); the venue discrepancy-polling intervals (their values are a share of a REST budget only the executor's call pattern defines, and the go/no-go's "zero unreconciled states" leans on this poll, so a number invented against an unmeasured budget would make the gate read clean by not looking); and `zcrypto engine decompose`'s table still labelling the governor column `capped → final`, which on a binding cycle would attribute the limits' share to the governor's row.

## 2026-08-03 — iter-119 shipped: the sleeve gauge is live and reads 1 (T0124 resolved)

- **One image discharged three payloads.** `c7ed09020fe1` (built from the iter-119 merge `f67afb80`) carries iter-119's §10 whole-book limits and `"ZEUR"` spot-position reporting, [[T0124]]'s sleeve-occupancy gauges, and spec `00080`'s refactor — the last of which finally discharges that spec's standing D6 flag, since the engine now runs an image built after `b86dcdbf`. Each payload was verified **in the pulled image from its own surface** (`apply_whole_book_limits` present, the gauge name in the command module, `"ZEUR"` in the node config, `PAIR_KEYS` relocated) rather than inferred from the tag.
- **The bake was real, and its most useful reading was the one that nearly went wrong.** Canary leg on `zcrypto-red` 2026-08-02 21:47:13Z; prune fired at the converge took the **strong** form (`deleted=720`, `Result=success`), so no degraded-gate acceptance was owed; 36 of 36 book streams — 12 pairs × hours 22/23/00 — began at `:00:00.0x`, read from a **pulled** copy because the capture hosts carry no parquet reader. The RSS row is the cautionary one. The cold baseline was **107.1 MB**, taken one minute after start; three hours warm the host scrape read **138.5 MB** (**+29.3 %**) and the Cloud scrape **136.4 MB** (+27.3 %) — the two differ only because they are separate scrapes moments apart, and either one read against that cold baseline looks like a climb. Against the host's **own** history it is a **decrease**: 136.4 MB versus **150.0 MB on the old image** six hours earlier, i.e. −9.1 %. The primary independently showed a +0.69 MB/h 3-hour slope while its 6-hour net was *down* 4.3 MB, which is the rotation sawtooth the three-hour window exists to separate out. A two-point comparison against a cold baseline would have rolled back a healthy image.
- **The engine converged 2026-08-03 04:45:41Z**, read from the container's `.State.StartedAt` rather than the converge command's return time. It landed in the 04:00→08:00 inter-cycle gap, 44 minutes after `cycle-04.json` completed at 04:01:48.791Z — so the 04:00 boundary was served cleanly by the old image and the gate streak was never at risk. **Stated plainly because it was luck rather than design: the backgrounded converge sat queued and executed ~3.5 h after it was launched**, at a moment the gap was not re-verified immediately beforehand.
- **The tag set was changed from the plan, deliberately, and the reason generalizes.** The Alloy **keep-list lives in the capture role**, not the engine role — so `--tags engine` alone would have published the new gauge families while leaving them unadmitted at Alloy, and the by-value check would have had nothing to read in Grafana Cloud. The converge therefore ran `--tags capture,engine` with **capture's digest held at its current value**, so the capture compose never changed and live capture never restarted. The preview confirmed exactly that shape: keep-list renders, engine pin moves, engine restarts, capture compose untouched. **The primary's capture deliberately stays on `99faf16514e3`** — the payload is engine-side, and restarting live capture would spend risk on the unbackfillable path for nothing.
- **The verification that mattered, read BY VALUE on two independent surfaces.** After the first cycle under the new image (`cycle-08.json` completed 08:01:40.845Z, inside `[08:00, 08:30]`, engine unrestarted since the converge): `zcrypto_engine_active_sleeves` = **1.0** on the host `/metrics` and **1** in Grafana Cloud, with `zcrypto_engine_sleeve_gross` reading B **0.0**, A1 **0.0**, A2 **0.10002169357501955**. Exactly the predicted one-sleeve state, confirmed live rather than inferred from the 136 journaled shadow cycles. Treating "the series appeared" as success would have repeated precisely the failure this gauge was built to catch, which is why the topic's own closing criterion was written as a value and not a presence check.
- **The silence before that first cycle was correct, and worth recording so a future operator does not read it as breakage.** Between the 04:45 converge and the 08:00 cycle both families published **nothing** — by construction: the labelled family stays silent until `.labels()` and the unlabelled one registers lazily, because a fresh Gauge's `0.0` would *claim* "no sleeves active" rather than be absent. An engine that has not yet completed a cycle has no sleeve state to report, and saying so by omission is the honest encoding.
- **`zcrypto-engine-sleeve-count-changed` is live and additive.** Pushed with `grafana-push.sh` (no `GRAFANA_PRUNE`; the run reported *no orphaned rules*), then **read back** — uid present, `noDataState: OK`, `for: 5m`, severity warning, and the query on **`grafanacloud-prom`**. That read-back is not ceremony: the provisioning API accepts a wrong datasource uid happily and still reports `health=ok`, which is how [[T0034]] silently repointed seven rules at the wrong data.
- **The gate was re-read immediately before the converge rather than assumed from the clock.** All six items were quoted fresh — red's digest and unchanged `StartedAt`, the rotation hours, the abort signals, the RSS slope, `cycle-04`'s completion inside its window, and the primary pre-flight. That last one caught a real gap: the candidate digest was **not resident on the primary**, so it was pulled before the converge rather than inside the stop→start window, which is the whole point of pre-staging.
- **A measurement discipline failure caught and corrected mid-flight, twice, both of the same shape.** The first `--check --diff` was piped through `tail -45`, which silently dropped the compose-pin task — the one change the converge exists to make — leaving the recap's `changed=3` disagreeing with the two changes visible in the evidence; re-run untruncated it showed the pin moving explicitly. Then `docker logs --since 21:47:00` **failed to parse** (it needs a duration, not a timestamp), so a grep over its empty output returned "0 abort signals" — a clean bill from a command that had errored. Redone with `--since 10m` and the input line count printed first, the zero became a zero over 601 real lines. Both are the same lesson the rules already carry: a truncated sweep is not a sweep, and an empty filtered query is not an absent event.
- **No decisions-log entry is owed.** This iteration executed and verified an already-ruled item ([[T0124]]'s PROCEED-KNOWINGLY, logged at iter-118); nothing here is a new subject-matter decision, and the tag-set and capture-hold choices are engineering, which the logging gate excludes by name.

## 2026-08-03 — the deployable's cost basis, ruled and re-quoted (iter-120, T0090 partial)

- **The execution style is ruled: maker-first with a taker fallback** (owner, 2026-08-03). Post-only inside the spread with a time-box, crossing only if still unfilled, and the realized blend **measured at rung 1** rather than assumed. What carried it was checkable rather than arguable: resting post-only orders are **free until filled** (verified in `kraken-fee-schedule.md` against Kraken's own article), so attempting maker costs nothing but latency; the master plan's thesis was already maker-first; and [[T0119]]'s `delta = target − actually held` **is** the mechanism that absorbs an unfilled delta — so maker fill risk is exactly the risk that design already exists to handle, and choosing maker spends no new risk budget. Maker-only-never-cross was rejected as unsound (an unfilled delta would never place, turning accumulation into an unbounded journal-vs-venue gap); taker-only was rejected as paying ~44 % of the conditional baseline for executor simplicity.
- **The record re-quoted across the execution range, instrument validated first.** All three registered anchors reproduced exactly on the committed builder (×1.0 → 1.5609, ×1.5 → 1.3029, ×2.0 → 1.2106) before any new point counted. Headline: **1.6304** maker@€1k → **1.5609** registered → **1.3641** taker@€10k. Conditional live-state: **0.9072 → 0.7509 → 0.5066**. Every **headline** point still clears every benchmark — the worst leads the like-for-like 4h decisive 1.2447 by 0.119 — so the topic's "no verdict flips at either end" survives measurement. The conditional column is **not** benchmark-comparable and must not be read that way: it is the expectation a perfectly-executing 6b should deliver, not a figure competing with 1.2447.
- **The finding worth carrying forward: the execution decision matters ~2.7× more for the live book than the headline shows.** Across the range the headline falls **16.3 %** while the conditional live-state baseline falls **44.2 %**. The mechanism is structural rather than incidental — the one-sleeve book runs at ⅙ gross and carries more turnover per unit of return, so cost bites hardest in precisely the state the live book occupies. Anyone reading only the headline curve underestimates the stake by nearly three times. The conditional arm was validated against [[T0124]]'s registered figures on the same 27,337-bar window (0.7509 vs 0.75, 1.9424 vs 1.94, headline 1.5609), with the state split nesting exactly: live 6,885 + fully-flat 3,680 = 10,565 = **38.6 %**, T0124's own number, the 25.2 % live subset being what remains after excluding the A2-flat bars the live book never occupies.
- **A numerical coincidence recorded so nobody trips over it.** [[T0116]]'s amendment registered the go/no-go band as "roughly 0.5–0.75" to express **estimation** uncertainty (IID SE ≈ 0.44, CI spanning zero). That interval is almost exactly the **cost-basis** range from taker up to ×1.0 — two unrelated uncertainties sharing one interval. At the taker end the baseline would sit at the band's floor with no room left for the statistical uncertainty the band was written for, so the honest quote is two-dimensional: conditional **and** cost-basis-dependent. Registered as a carried decision; §12 was not edited from this iteration.
- **The cost model is split into `fee_per_side` + `spread_per_side`, and it is deliberately representational.** The old `spot_fee_per_side = 0.006` fused a 40 bps venue fee to a 20 bps spread **guess** that the Phase-5 runbook always said would be replaced by calibration; [[T0014]] measured the real spread at 2.11 bps/side, making the guess 5–10× too large. The defaults keep the pre-measurement 20 bps so every registered figure reproduces — `0.0040 + 0.0020 == 0.006` at **0 ulp** — and re-pricing to the measured value is a separate deliberate step at rung 1.
- **Neutrality was proven past the tolerance that would have hidden a defect.** The pinned regressions are `approx(abs=0.005 / 0.0005)`, which a 1-ulp shift survives, so passing them proves nothing about bit-neutrality. The proof instead is sha256 over the **raw IEEE-754 bytes** of `governed_net`, `ungoverned_net`, `multipliers` and `final_targets` on both builders, before and after: byte-identical. The sum is evaluated **once** into a local rather than re-added at each charge site, because this same iteration's sibling work had already lost a proof to a 1-ulp difference between `sum()` and chained `+`.
- **Known and stated rather than papered over: the two cost terms are not independently observable.** Only their sum is ever charged — confirmed by a control that swaps the defaults (same sum → identical output). The split buys traceability, not new behavior, and the guards pin exactly that: each field *feeds the sum*. Both guards were proven by defect injection to fail per-field rather than wholesale, and a reviewer re-injected the same defects independently rather than take the claim.
- **One of the topic's own sub-items turned out to be impossible, and it is the most consequential thing this iteration found.** T0090 listed "re-read the 43-vs-44 ordering at ×1.40" as *cheap, autonomous, spends no budget* — "the criterion the record rests on, and the one thing still unread". It cannot be done: `git log --all -S"adaptive" -- cli/` returns **nothing**, both trials' `run_ref` name **scratchpad** scripts that no longer exist, and `_inverse_vol_weights` survives only for *asset-basket* weighting inside sleeves, never for the **sleeve-level** weighting trial 43 used (`weight_warmup_bars = 180`, `weight_zero_vol_fallback_bars = 10638`). Re-reading that ordering would mean reconstructing an unversioned instrument from a registry variant string and trusting it to reproduce a number nobody can check. **Trial 44 is reproducible from committed code; trial 43 is not — so the ADOPT decision that made 44 the deployable can never be re-examined at a different cost basis.** Same failure class as [[T0065]]'s reproducibility round, now reaching the deployable record's own adoption criterion.
- **Fee axis verified rather than inherited**: Kraken tier 1 is **0.40 % maker / 0.80 % taker** under the schedule effective 2026-07-09; our 30-day volume is $0.00 and tier 4's 0.20 %/0.35 % needs $25k, unreachable at a €1,000 book. Margin legs pay the spot fee on **both** open and close.
- **Carried, not resolved**: `spread_per_side` inherits a `> 0` validator from the blended constant, where it was obviously right; on a split term a **zero spread is legitimate** (a maker fill at mid). Deliberately not loosened inside a commit whose contract was bit-identical neutrality — registered in T0090 against the executor iteration, and until then a zero-spread config fails loud with a named error rather than producing a silent wrong number.
- **The band collision was ruled and §12 amended the same day** *(added 2026-08-03, after the ruling)*. §12 said the go/no-go reads the conditional expectation "quoted as a band with its confidence interval" — naming **one** uncertainty while two are in play, the second measured here as comparable in size. Rather than widen one interval to hold both — which passes execution it should fail, the same failure §12 already warns about for a *stale* band, and which destroys the diagnostic besides — the two are now **separate terms**: the statistical CI, and the cost basis. They behave differently and the amendment says so: **the cost term resolves to a point once rung 1 measures the realized blend; the statistical one never resolves.** The amendment also splits the **failure diagnosis**, which the merged form could not express — divergence attributable to the estimate means the backtest or its conditioning is wrong ⇒ return to Phase 5, while divergence attributable to the cost basis is an **execution finding** that re-prices the basis without invalidating the backtest. Both misattributions are costly in opposite directions (sending a sound system back a phase, or ramping capital onto a broken one), which is why the diagnosis is written into the gate rather than left to judgement under drawdown stress. Concrete figures stay **out** of §12 per T0116's discipline; the re-derivation trigger list gains "whenever the realized maker/taker blend moves".
- **Spec `00080`'s D6 converge-exposure flag is discharged, and the discharge was checked rather than asserted.** The engine now runs `c7ed09020fe1`, built from `f67afb80` — and `git merge-base --is-ancestor b86dcdbf f67afb80` confirms it, with the counter-check that `b86dcdbf` is *not* an ancestor of `3540b0bb`, which is exactly why the 08-02 12:13 re-pin did not discharge it. That flag had been wrong in **both** directions across its life: a premature "discharged" the 08-02 re-pin had not earned, then an "unshipped" warning that outlived its truth. Its carriers are updated in place — `14.phase6b-orientation.md` (which had pointed at the memo as the flag's only home) and the memo entry itself, now marked purgeable.

## 2026-08-03 — the registry can no longer record a trial nobody can rebuild (T0125 partial)

- **The guard exists because the failure it prevents already cost something irreversible.** Registry record 44 is the deployable, and its registered verdict is ADOPT *vs incumbent trial 43* — a trial that cannot be rebuilt, since its construction was never committed and its `run_ref` names scratchpad scripts that are gone. The criterion that selected the live system can never be re-examined at any cost basis. That is unfixable; this makes the next occurrence impossible.
- **The measurement reframed the topic.** Of 46 records, **14 name scratchpads — trial_ids 33–46, contiguous, every trial from 33 onward** — and the other 32 name a `docs/research/*.md` **write-up** rather than the code that produced the run. **Zero records have ever named a committed `.py` runner.** The convention never required one, so this was never about trial 43 specifically; trial 43 is only where it first cost something.
- **Two layers, split deliberately.** Append time (`validate_caller_fields`, which `TrialRegistry.append` calls *before* opening the file): `run_ref` is now **required** — a `None` run_ref is strictly worse than a scratchpad one — and must name at least one repo-relative path that **exists**, with absolute paths and `..` escapes rejected because provenance means a path *inside* the repo. A `scratchpad` marker raises its **own** error, since it is the self-declared form of the defect and deserves a precise diagnosis rather than a generic "no path resolved". No git, no subprocess: this runs on every append. Repo level (`tests/test_trial_registry_provenance.py`, where git is guaranteed): every `run_ref` must name a **git-tracked** path — the check that actually means *committed*.
- **The legacy exemption is frozen and asserted in BOTH directions**, which is the guard's weakest point by construction and therefore the part worth stating. Trials 33–46 are exempt because the registry is append-only and hash-chained, so those records can never be repaired. The test asserts that no record *outside* the set fails **and** that every member genuinely fails today — so the pin cannot decorate a pass and absorb a regression behind it. Widening the frozenset makes a test fail rather than quietly pass, and that was verified by widening it.
- **The stored-record path stays lenient on purpose, and review found the real reason is stronger than the one first written.** `validate_stored_record` calls `validate_caller_fields`, so a strict load path would not merely have broken reads — `append` re-validates every stored record under lock, so it would have **bricked all future appends** to the live registry. Provenance over history is asserted by the repo-level test instead, which operates on the file rather than the API, so even a hand-appended record is caught. A dedicated test pins the split so nobody "tightens" the load path later.
- **A permanent trap was found in review and closed before it could fire.** The two layers resolved paths differently — the filesystem normalizes `./cli/x.py`, `git ls-files` does not — so a record naming a genuinely **committed** file in a non-canonical spelling would have passed the append guard and then failed the provenance test **forever**: the registry is append-only, so the offending record could never be edited out and the only remedy would have been weakening the test. Extraction now canonicalizes, and a test pins that both layers accept every spelling of the same path; removing the canonicalization makes that test fail.
- **Deliberately not marked a breaking change, and the reasoning is recorded because the default would have been wrong.** `TrialRegistry.append`'s `run_ref` lost its `None` default, which is a genuine in-repo signature change — but the only callers are two test helpers, updated in the same commit, and there is no out-of-repo consumer. At `version = 0.0.0` with `major_version_zero = false`, a `!` marker would have minted **v1.0.0**, announcing a stable public API this repo has never released, on the strength of a test-helper signature.

## 2026-08-03 — the go/no-go now says what it certifies, and a named-but-undefined report section is dropped (iter-121, T0064 + T0073 resolved)

- **The ruling: accept the missing out-of-time evidence, and narrow the gate's claim to match** (owner, 2026-08-03). §12 now states that passing the go/no-go certifies **execution** — the live book reproduces the intended one, orders place and reconcile, costs and drift sit inside the measured band, ops paths hold — and explicitly does **not** certify **edge**. It also names the two limitations the decision inherits: no out-of-time validation of returns, and [[T0125]]'s adoption criterion that cannot be re-examined. Both are stated at the gate because that is where they bind; a criteria-met reading that did not mention them would overclaim.
- **Mitigation was not rejected as too costly — it was measured as unavailable**, which is what made the ruling decidable rather than a judgement call. The only window that exists is 2026-04-01 → 2026-08-03: **124 days = 0.339 years**. A Sharpe estimate over it returns 95 % CIs of **[−3.45, +6.57]** at the registered 1.5609, **[−3.06, +4.56]** at 0.75 and **[−3.05, +4.31]** at 0.63, needing **3.5 / 8.8 / 11.6 years** to separate from zero. A ratified budget expansion would therefore buy a test that provably cannot discriminate, repeat the degenerate-window failure that wasted the first look, and burn the option for a window that might one day be long enough. Accepting forecloses nothing — the expansion stays available.
- **The decision-support read was run at full window first, and it is honest about its own limits.** `zcrypto engine soak-check` over the live journal: **L = 140 scored bars / 23.17 days**, all three self-tests `ok`, chain consistent; **1 of 7 outside band, 2 of 7 indeterminate**; realized cumulative net **−0.4559 %**, non-gating and near-vacuous at this length. Both disagreements trace to **already-registered conditions rather than new findings** — `governor_engagement` reads inconsistent because the multiplier is 0.5 on all 140 cycles with zero variance ([[T0018]]'s recorded entering state), and `gross`/`net` sit at 0.0501 against a null median of 0.0829 — a level consistent with the one-sleeve book [[T0124]] measured, since a single live sleeve at fixed ⅓ weights carries less gross than the diversified combination the null samples. **The *level* is attributed; the *verdict* is not.** "Indeterminate" is a severity label meaning the two null constructions disagree — instrument fragility — and nothing here demonstrates that composition causes that disagreement. Attributing it would be explaining away a result rather than reading it. The instrument's own closing line is the operative one: structural-conformance checks, not evidence of edge.
- **The read had to be repaired before it could be believed, and this is the iteration's real finding.** Run against the configured store, `soak-check` scored **54 bars ending 2026-07-19** and silently dropped **87 of 141 cycles** — because iter-103 retired the workstation soak, which was what kept that store warm, and nothing replaced it while the default `--store-dir` still pointed there. The report printed `dropped_tail` and the window bounds, so it was not dishonest; but nothing said the window was bounded by the **store** rather than the journal, so `L=54, 0 of 6 outside band` read as the current state of the evidence when it was a read on 38 % of it.
- **The stale window did not merely shrink the sample — it changed the verdict.** At 54 bars: 0 of 6 outside band, with `governor_engagement`'s null band spanning the full [0,1] and therefore no discriminating power at all. At 140: 1 of 7 outside, 2 of 7 indeterminate, and `governor_engagement` finally discriminating. Ruling on the stale read would have been ruling on 38 % of the evidence while believing it was current — and the metric that mattered most was precisely the one that had no power.
- **`soak-check` now states what bounded its window** (`de708d7d`). The classification is structural rather than a count threshold: of the candidate cycles *after* the last scored one, take the gate that skipped them — store-closes-absent ⇒ `store`, successor-postdates-now ⇒ `clock`, else `journal`. Review verified the load-bearing property independently: `journal` is reported **iff** the trailing set is empty, so the store can never run out inside the scoreable frontier and still read benign. A store-bound window prints a warning naming both bounds and the cycle cost, placed inside the window block and before the fingerprint table, since a warning after the table is a warning nobody reads. It deliberately does **not** refuse — a partial pulled store is legitimate; the failure was silence.
- **The benign case is pinned as hard as the firing one.** `dropped_tail == 1` from the last-cycle-never-scores rule must not warn, and the naive `dropped_tail > 1` rule was shown to fail on two distinct false-positive shapes (an interior store hole, and a clock-bound tail) — so both directions are held, not just the one the fix was aimed at.
- **The default `--store-dir` is deliberately left pointing at the cold workstation store.** Re-pointing it would be wrong: the configured store is the engine's own, correct on the VPS where the engine runs. A workstation run is therefore store-bound by construction and now says so; the remedy is to pass a pulled VPS store, which is recorded in the archived topic.
- **T0073's remainder was one definition, and it is DROPPED on a measurement** (owner, 2026-08-03). Spec `00058` *named* a "regime context" section without specifying its content, which is why it was deferred rather than built — inventing a definition inside an implementation iteration would have baked an arbitrary choice into a go/no-go instrument. The open question was which regime variable is worth conditioning a soak verdict on, and the test it had to pass was *what would a reader do differently*, because on this instrument unactionable context reads as evidence.
- **The experiment is the one any regime split performs anyway: halve the window.** On the 23.17-day realized window (L = 140), split into contiguous halves of **L = 71 and L = 68** — both the **same regime** by every system-internal measure (multiplier 0.5 with zero variance, cap-breach 0, one active sleeve throughout). Two results: **conditioning destroys the only discrimination the instrument makes** — `governor_engagement` reads `inconsistent` at full window and **`n/a`, no discriminating power, on both halves** — and **two windows in the same regime already disagree with each other**, `gross`/`net` landing consistent vs indeterminate and realized net at **+0.0302 %** vs **−0.5514 %**. So a regime section could not separate a regime effect from sampling variation at any window this instrument will plausibly read; it would present exactly that split and invite a reader to see structure in noise.
- **Nothing is lost, because the honest residue already ships.** `soak-check` already states regime state without conditioning any verdict on it — "realized multiplier was 0.5 on all 140 scored cycles (no variance)", the same for cap-breach — and sleeve occupancy now has its own gauge and alert. That is regime context in the only form that adds information rather than width. The drop is recorded **in spec `00058` itself**, with the measurement, so the spec no longer reads as owing a section.
- **Deferring a third time was rejected — on the non-alternating leg, not on the power arithmetic.** Rejected — but on the right leg. The measurement only **brackets** the power threshold in (≈70, 140]; it does not show that the ~90-bar cells a ≥30-day window would give are powerless. The rejection rests instead on the regimes **not alternating**, which window length cannot fix: the governor has been ×0.5 for the entire soak (carried from the 2025 drawdown) and both dormant sleeves have been flat ~9 months, so a split on either leaves **one cell empty at any horizon** — and an empty cell is not a comparison at any L. This topic had already been re-pointed **twice** for triggers that could not fire.

## 2026-08-03 — the deployable stops resting on a comparison nobody can rebuild (iter-122, T0125 resolved)

- **The ruling turned a one-option acceptance into a re-grounding, because a second basis existed underneath.** T0125's remainder was framed as bare acceptance: record 44's verdict is ADOPT *vs incumbent trial 43*, trial 43 is unrebuildable, so accept it. But record 44 also registers **benchmark-relative** legs — SPA, DSR, worst-slice-relative, cost-stress — entirely independent of trial 43. The 4h benchmark reproduced **exactly** (1.2128 full / 1.2447 decisive, union 27,338 bars matching the phase-4 record), so accepting a hole would have discarded a reproducible foundation that was already there. §12 now states the gate rests on it.
- **The whole kill bar is measured rather than registry-asserted**, re-derived from committed code with the instrument validated before any leg counted: SPA `spa_p_full` 0.001999000499750125, `spa_p_decisive` 0.004497751124437781 and all five `spa_grid_*` cells **bit-identical**; `var_trials_4h` 1.3058096692086857e-05 and `per_period_sharpe_4h` 0.03335455562889141 **bit-identical**; `dsr` 0.9999994822040257 against a registry that stored it rounded to 1.0; `worst_slice_relative_pass` 1; `cap_breach_bars` 1318 / `governor_engaged_bars` 7302 exact. Margin over the reproduced benchmark: **+0.3481 full / +0.3136 decisive**.
- **The line between this re-derivation and rebuilding trial 43 is the substance of the ruling, not a caveat on it.** Every primitive here is **committed** (`cli/validation/spa.py`, `dsr.py`, `bootstrap.py`, `cli/alpha/killbar.py`, the record-44 builder), and the benchmark's construction was **specified in advance** in `iterations-history-phase4.md` — read before running. A first attempt using the *wrong* construction (the daily benchmark expanded to 4h, rather than the time-preserving re-parameterization at 180/1200/ppy 2190) missed by **0.06 Sharpe** and was **discarded rather than adjusted**. Trial 43 has neither property: no committed code, no specification to reproduce against. A rebuild of it could only be tuned until it matched, which proves nothing — so reconstruction stays explicitly rejected.
- **The re-derivation is committed, not a scratchpad** — `cli/portfolio/record44_legs.py` with tests, runnable by anyone. A topic that exists *because* a scratchpad vanished cannot honestly deposit its answer in another one.
- **Two details spec `00038` never pinned are now written there, recovered rather than chosen.** The SPA grid's headline cell is `(mean_block 30, seed 42)`: its full-window reading is stored as `spa_p_full` and its decisive reading as `spa_p_decisive`, which is why the registry carries no `spa_grid_b30_s42` key — a gap that otherwise reads as a missing cell. And the benchmark-relative worst-slice test runs on the **governed net over full history**, year taken from each bar's **close** stamp; on the decisive window the book's worst slice moves from 2022 to 2014, contradicting the registered note, which is what makes the choice discriminating rather than a fit.
- **A measurement-harness bug of the same class the session kept finding, caught by bisecting rather than retrying.** My first re-derivation attempt hung twice and timed out at 15 minutes with an empty output file. It was not a slow primitive: a `dict(zip(...))` had been left inside a per-timestamp comprehension, rebuilding a 17,192-entry map 273,380 times. The tell was that the first timing line printed and the next did not — had the input been slightly smaller it would have produced a slow, correct-looking answer and the conclusion would have been a false finding about the repo rather than a true one about the harness.
- **The closeout broke a verification chain and review caught it — worth recording precisely because of what it was a closeout about.** The two recovered conventions were first appended to spec `00038`, and registry records 43/44 store `spec_hash a25d7102…`, which is the **sha256 of that file**. The append moved it to `914c9fc4…`, breaking the pin that verifies the ratified record. Reverted, hash restored and re-measured, conventions relocated into the committed re-derivation module. The rule it yields: **a spec named by a registry `spec_hash` is immutable** — record a recovered convention in runnable code, never in the pinned document. Corrected alongside it: §12 had listed the cost-stress rungs and drawdowns among the re-derived legs, which the module itself disclaims; they remain covered by the frozen-figure regression at its own 4-dp tolerance, and the earlier "byte-exactly" phrasing was wrong.
- **What stays permanently lost, and the record says so.** Nobody can re-read the 43-vs-44 ordering, including at the taker end where the registry itself discloses the ordering **reverses** at ×2.0 (1.2106 vs 1.2400). The re-grounding does not recover that counterfactual — it makes the gate not depend on it.

## 2026-08-03 — refine-rules round 3: eight lessons placed, and the round catches its own weakening (iter-123)

- **Eight items graduated and the memory inbox is empty for the first time** — five harvested from the session, three standing. The routing matters more than the count: **only four grew the always-loaded corpus**. The vault token-extraction recipe went to `infra/scripts/grafana-push.sh`'s own env documentation, where someone hits the wall; the tmux session-suicide warning to `fleet.md`, read on demand; the backgrounded-converge lesson to a role-assert registered against the mechanization topic rather than prose on the heaviest rule; and the `docker logs --since` trap was **folded into an existing bullet** instead of added beside it, because it is an instance of a rule already there.
- **The `spec_hash` failure turned out to be recurring, not novel — which is why it became a rule.** The sweep found the phase-5 history recording the same break: records 34–35 pin the pre-amendment sha256 of their spec, edited after both appends, so the pin can never be recomputed. It was caught by review, written up as a narrated provenance note, and **no rule was made** — so it happened again on 2026-08-03 against a different spec. A narrated note is precisely what failed to prevent the recurrence, so this round added the rule **and** a check: a record's `spec_hash` must equal the sha256 of some committed spec, with 34–35 frozen and the exemption asserted in both directions. Measured before pinning — of six distinct hash groups across 46 records, exactly one dangles, and the deployable's own pin is asserted live rather than exempted.
- **The round's largest edit weakened something, and its own cold review caught it.** Widening the spec/plan ceremony's "trivial" branch to **design-settled** (file count is not the test — a spec written after the ruling is transcription) silently dropped a **model floor**: the cold spec+plan review mandates Opus, and **Fable** for the unbackfillable capture path, the live trade path and canonical data. That floor rode on the ceremony being removed, while the surviving pre-push review mandates a *different agent* but no floor — so a multi-file live-trade change could have been reviewed by any model where Fable was mandatory. The floor is now attached to the design-settled path explicitly. **An escape hatch widened without noticing what was riding on it is the characteristic failure of a refinement round**, and it is the reason the cold review reads every changed line rather than only removals.
- **The same review found the widening self-certifying in unattended mode** — topics open autonomously and the loop pre-satisfies approval gates, so a run could author the deciding topic and claim design-settled in the same pass. Closed in both places it could be exploited: the rule now requires the ruling to **predate the branch**, and the unattended skill says so at the step where a loop would make that call.
- **The history-entry skip was deliberately decoupled from the widening.** The old rule bundled "skip the spec" and "skip the history entry" into one branch, so widening would have licensed dropping entries for multi-file work — which practice never did. The two now turn on different tests: spec/plan ceremony on whether design is settled, the entry on whether the change is trivial **in substance**.
- **Measured, not asserted, at every gate**: modal-floor lines **82 → 86** (no decrease, so nothing softened); always-loaded corpus **41,870 → 43,992 bytes, +2,122 (+5.1%)**, accepted explicitly by the owner; **8 of 8** graduations verified present at their named landing paths before a single staged memory file was deleted; operand check across `CLAUDE.md` and every rule returned ~70 OK with one drift (a `data/` claim, corrected) and two unverifiable host-side items; every `references/` file confirmed named at a loading step.

## 2026-08-03 — converge discipline becomes refusable: thirteen guards, mutate-probe, and capture-deploys rewritten to mechanism pointers (iter-124, T0111 partial)

- **Thirteen converge guards landed across the capture/engine/ops/docker roles, `site.yml`, and `bootstrap.yml`** (spec `00082`, T0111 wave 1 + both M-asserts): digest-resident preflights on all three tiers — on ops, where every runner is `--pull never`, an unpulled digest exits every timer 125; on capture/engine, where the unit's own `ExecStartPre` pulls at start, it instead extends the stop→start window a converge must keep to stop → migration-if-any → start — the un-tagged-primary refusal, the engine inter-cycle-window re-assert read at task execution time (the 2026-08-03 backgrounded-converge lesson — launch-time checks don't survive a 3.5 h delay), the engine secrets/port preflight, pair-add order (delegated read of the primary's deployed pairs, fail-closed on unreachable), canary digest-parity (capture re-pins — the engine mirror is a registered wave-2 candidate), fleet-pins recording (controller-tree read) on all three tiers, the liquidations repin decision, the panel-timer hold, the `daemon.json` change-ack (probe-render → show diff → ask → act), and the re-bootstrap refusal.
- **Override semantics are uniform and reason-required** (spec `00082` D1): `-e <name>_override="<reason>"` — empty, boolean-like, or <9-char values refused; the accepted reason is echoed into the play log so bypass and justification land in the same artifact. `canary_override`, `pins_override`, `engine_window_override`; the conscious-choice flags `daemon_json_ack`, `liquidations_decision=roll-after|defer`, `ops_panel_timer_hold`, `rebootstrap` follow the same refuse-by-default shape.
- **Every guard was proven by constructing its named defect and reading which failure fired** — 82 tests evaluate the committed YAML's real `that:`/`when:` expressions through Ansible's own Templar against violation/pass fixtures. The substrate lesson: ansible-core 2.21.2 Data-Tagging makes `Templar.template()` return a plain string UNRENDERED — `trust_as_template()` is mandatory, else every fixture is vacuously truthy (caught by the cold spec+plan review, reproduced before folding).
- **`infra/scripts/mutate-probe.sh`** (11 tests) collapses four `agent-ops.md` mutation-discipline bullets into one enforced script: dirty-tree refusal, `git archive` sandbox seeding, pytest-in-sandbox refusal, stale-`.pyc` purge, mandatory failing control, no-op-sed abort, `cmp`-verified pristine restore, signal-safe cleanup (restore-before-clean, green 30/30 then 15/15 across two campaigns under SIGTERM/SIGINT after a reviewer-reproduced signal hole was fixed — 2 failures in 20 before it).
- **`.claude/hooks/git-mv-guard.sh`** (PostToolUse on Bash, 12 tests) warns the moment `git mv` forms the `RM` porcelain trap — staged rename of the INDEX version with edits left unstaged — which bit twice on 2026-08-03 and which repo-side pre-commit structurally cannot see (it stashes unstaged changes first). The considered network-timeout hook was dropped (owner ruling; the timeout-prefix rule already covers it at lower complexity).
- **`fleet-deploys.md` was rewritten from incident narratives to mechanism pointers**: twelve per-edit owner-signed edits (the narratives now live in the asserts' fail messages, delivered at the moment of the mistake). Net size grew slightly rather than shrinking — 9987 → 10374 bytes (+387), 64 lines unchanged: what each pointer costs in naming the refusing mechanism and its bypass, the deleted narrative roughly gave back. `agent-ops.md`'s four mutation bullets became one `mutate-probe.sh` pointer.
- Wave-2 remainder and two guard-tightening candidates recorded in T0111 (`partial`): the scripts (`converge.sh`, `zcrypto-panel-regenerate`, `ops-postverify.sh`, genesis annotation, `vault-pass.sh` ancestor check), the window guard's early-cycle floor, guard 4's any-non-empty `--skip-tags` acceptance.

## 2026-08-04 — the converge path itself becomes refusable: wave-2 scripts, the engine parity mirror, and the gap closures (iter-125, T0111 partial)

- **`infra/ansible/scripts/converge.sh` is the documented converge path** (spec `00083` D1): requires `--limit` (a bare `site.yml` still runs every play), always runs and displays the `--check --diff` preview first (a failed preview refuses, rc 4), then takes a typed confirm of the `--limit` value itself — byte-exact, read from `/dev/tty` only, so a pipe or heredoc cannot drive it — before `exec`ing the real pass. Preview-only invocations (`--check`) stop clean after the preview.
- **`zcrypto-panel-regenerate`** (ops role installs it) turns the panel delete-and-rebuild checklist into one refusing flow: timer stopped first, window sized from the input tree (~2.1 s/MB ×1.2) and refused when the ETA crosses the 02:25 UTC auto-reboot (reason-required override), the healthchecks.io pause taken as a typed `paused` gate before anything is deleted, the whole ops-side panel root deleted (out-of-scope subtrees included), the rebuild run **inside** the materialize unit so a stray timer tick collides instead of double-writing, the timer restarted only on success, and the NAS-deletion + un-pause checklist printed for the halves the host cannot reach. Review hardening: a failing `du` on the NAS mount refuses instead of silently neutralizing the ETA guard (timer deliberately left stopped — the dead-man is still armed at that step), and an empty-rendered panel subdir refuses before any call rather than pointing `rm -rf` at the whole ops data dir.
- **`infra/scripts/ops-postverify.sh`** runs the ops verify-by-outcome checklist as one command over `grafana-query.py`: six checks, `(no series)` and query errors read FAIL, never a clean bill; value extraction is anchored to series lines so a future PromQL containing `== ` cannot mint a phantom PASS from the query echo (caught in review, red-first regression test).
- **`vault-pass.sh` now refuses the ancestries that decrypt the whole vault to stdout** — `ansible-inventory --host`, `--list`, and (review catch) `--graph --vars` — by walking `/proc` from itself to init; the refusal names the safe alternatives. The red phase of its own tests decrypted the real vault once into the implementer subagent's local transcript (nothing committed, branch not on remote, independently entropy-swept); rotation was offered to the owner.
- **The engine canary-parity mirror closes wave-1's registered live-trade-path gap**: the engine role now refuses an engine re-pin whose digest the secondary is not running as capture (there is no engine secondary — that bake IS the engine's gate), sharing `canary_override` and the fail-closed unreachable semantics with the capture assert; the mirror was re-derived structurally in review with zero unsanctioned divergence, and a second review round pinned the when-side mechanics after a constructed mutation showed an unreachable secondary could have been made to pass silently with every test green.
- **The engine window floor follows the journal**: when the boundary's `cycle-HH.json` carries `completed_at`, the floor becomes that completion + 5 min in place of the fixed B+30 min — usually earlier (a cycle finishing at B+2 min opens the window at B+7), but later when the cycle itself ran long, and that late refusal is the point rather than a regression: the 5 min of post-completion clearance is unconditional, so a cycle completing at B+28 keeps the converge out until B+33. Probed on the engine host at task execution time; every failure path (missing file, parse error, garbage stdout, short numbers) keeps the conservative B+30 floor, with the 10-digit epoch anchor pinned by its own test after the implementer measured that `"99"` would otherwise evaporate the floor to a 1970 timestamp.
- **Three smaller closures**: guard 4 now accepts only `--skip-tags` forms that actually exclude the engine; the liquidations pin probe stat-splits absent (stand-down) from unreadable (refuse), resolving symlinks so a dangling compose link reads absent rather than prescribing chmod; `mutate-probe.sh` gained hermetic rc 8 (seeding) / rc 9 (cleanup-restore failure, pristine kept and named) and phase-named no-op aborts; the git-mv hook resolves `git -C` and `cd`-prefixed (spaced or not) forms to the right repo and NOTEs unresolvable dirs instead of judging the wrong one.
- **The registered genesis-annotation item was found already shipped and booked, not rebuilt** — measured before design (spec-00079-era `genesis_skipped` + row mark), recorded in T0111.
- Verification: 376 tests across the eight wave files (each refusal proven against its constructed violation; pty gates 10×-looped; signal semantics re-driven live in review), full suite 3034 green including the data-dependent regression tests, `pre-commit run -a` clean, both playbooks syntax-clean. T0111's sole remainder is the attended ops-host drills (registered with its `ripe_when`).

## 2026-08-04 — a mandated routine that nothing ran, and the stale source it was reading (iter-126, T0113 resolved)

- **The complaint was never that the sweep was hard — it was that nothing would run it.** The master plan mandates re-confirming its externally-owned ⏱ facts "at Phase 0 and again at go-live"; Phase 0 did it (register stamped `2026-07-07`) and the second sweep lived only as plan prose. It now exists in four places — three of which fail loudly if removed, while the fourth is the one to watch: the **`/zcrypto-refdata-sweep` skill** (procedure), a **scheduled `#zcrypto` reminder** (trigger — the mechanism T0103/T0105 use, because a `ripe_when:` date is read by nobody at the moment it fires), the **`refdata-sweep-due` runbook section** (response), and the register itself relocated to `docs/reference/` as the living artifact it is. **The reminder is the weak link by construction**: a scheduled Slack message fires once and its absence is silent, which is why re-arming the next one is step 4 of the runbook procedure rather than an afterthought — the runbook says so in its own words. The runbook's own scope forbids backlog entries and admits only "a procedure for a signal that fires at an operator" — the reminder is what makes the section legitimate rather than smuggled in.
- **Sweep #1's verdict: UNCHANGED**, and re-derived rather than asserted. All twelve §3 candidates still online and margin-enabled with identical leverage bands, `ordermin`, `costmin` and aliases; review reproduced it independently, including a **fresh live fetch an hour later** that also matched. Kraken's own universe churned underneath — **13 pairs added, 93 removed, 15 assets added** — without touching the basket, which is why the register now states that **a changed `raw_sha256` is not a changed fact**: the hash moves nearly every sweep, and a routine that alarms on it is ignored by its third run.
- **The register was re-confirming the wrong things, and the review measured it.** `derive_universe` extracted status/margin/leverage/minimums and nothing else, while the master plan's ⏱ list names **fees and borrow-rollover rates** — both public and never captured. Review measured `margin_rate` on AVAX and `short_position_limit` on four pairs moving inside **one hour**, invisible to the table and the hash alike. Now captured: the volume-tiered fee ladders, `margin_call`/`margin_stop`, position limits, and per-asset **`margin_rate`**, which *is* the rollover rate and lives on the **asset** — reading it off the pair would have yielded `None` for every row and read as "no borrow data" rather than as a bug.
- **Both borrow sides render, because they price opposite trades.** A short sells the borrowed base; **a long buys with borrowed quote currency**, so the base-side rate never prices it. Rendering only the base side left the book's long leg unpriced while the table looked complete — caught at review, and pinned by a test spanning a EUR-quoted and a BTC-quoted pair where the two genuinely differ.
- **The sharpest finding came from the owner, and it was a correction to me.** Asked whether folding fees into the register duplicated `docs/reference/kraken-fee-schedule.md`, the answer was worse than duplication: the two **contradicted**, and the register was wrong. Kraken's public `AssetPairs` still serves the **pre-2026-07-09** schedule (0.25 % maker / 0.40 % taker, breaks at \$10k/\$50k) a month after it was superseded by 0.40 %/0.80 % with breaks at \$2.5k/\$10k/\$25k — exactly what the fee doc predicted when it was written from the logged-in account ("the public fee-schedule page still showed the old schedule when this was captured"). I had already drawn a false finding from it: that `fee_per_side = 0.0040` was mislabelled "tier-1 MAKER". Under the schedule in force, **0.40 % *is* tier-1 maker** — the comment was right and my source was stale. Retracted; `kraken-fee-schedule.md` is now named the fee source of truth in both files and the skill, and the register's fee columns are a **drift detector on the endpoint** — read the level from the account, the change from here.
- **A source of truth with no re-read mechanism is a stale fact waiting to happen.** `kraken-fee-schedule.md` was written during **T0000**'s Phase-0 account actions, carries a "⏱ reconfirm on the logged-in Fee tab" marker, and feeds `cli/costs/fees.py` verbatim — but T0000 was resolved and archived, so its trigger died with it and nothing had re-read it in a month. It becomes the **attended half** of the monthly sweep, with three outcomes routed differently: unchanged is logged, changed corrects the doc *and names what re-prices*, and owner-unavailable records **"not re-read"** — never inherited. The register's log carries an account-tier column whose first two rows demonstrate the distinction: sweep #0 holds the real 2026-07-07 read, sweep #1 holds *not re-read*, because at \$0 volume the tier **cannot** have moved but "cannot have" is not "was checked".
- **Nothing was archived away with the topic.** Its three deferrals were re-homed first: the monthly cadence into skill+runbook+reminder; the **pre-go/no-go run into T0085**, where the decision is taken and where both halves are mandatory plus the human-only re-reads (MiCA, tax rules, data pricing) that no endpoint covers; and the orphaned account-tier re-read into the sweep's attended half.

## 2026-08-05 — the two drills that closed the mechanization topic, and the render bug only a real converge could find (iter-125 closeout, T0111 resolved)
