- **Both mechanized converge paths are now proven on the live fleet rather than only against their tests.** `converge.sh` was drilled at the 2026-08-04 secondary re-pin, where its typed-confirm gate **refused two agent-driven attempts with rc 3** — agent-harness commands carry pipes and no controlling terminal in every shape, foreground or backgrounded — and landed only when the operator ran it in a plain terminal. `zcrypto-panel-regenerate` was drilled the same day as a full delete-and-rebuild of the ops panel tree: 08:39:46 → 16:45:31 UTC, `hours_written=6370 hours_skipped=0 rows=22827108 errors=0`, run inside the materialize unit exactly so a stray timer tick is a no-op.
- **The rebuild's generation was proven by value, not by presence** — the distinction this project keeps having to relearn. The tick the timer had queued behind the 8-hour rebuild ran immediately after and reported `hours_written=80 hours_skipped=6370 errors=0`: it read the new tree and skipped *precisely* the hours the rebuild wrote. A mismatched generation would have refused; a corrupt tree would have rewritten rather than skipped. "The unit exited 0" would have shown none of that.
- **A completed `Type=oneshot` unit retains the PREVIOUS run's `Result`, and that nearly produced a false all-clear.** At the moment the rebuild ended, `systemctl show` reported `Result=success` with `ExecMainStartTimestamp` **16:45:31** — a 4-minute run, not the 8-hour one. That green belonged to the queued tick, not to the drill. `ExecMainExitTimestamp` and the journal's own `Starting`/`Finished` pairs are the discriminator; `is-active` is worse still, exiting **3** for `activating`, which had already appended a spurious "probe-failed" earlier in the same drill.
- **The drill's real yield was two render bugs no offline test could have caught.** The first attempt installed a script that never rendered: bash's string-length expansion `${#var}` opens with a brace-hash pair that **Jinja reads as a comment tag**, so the override guard silently vanished. The fix for *that* then shipped a second broken script, because Ansible renders with `trim_blocks=True` while a bare `jinja2.Environment()` defaults it False — the test that cleared the fix was lying about the engine it modelled. The guard is now written as offset expansion (`"${override:8}"`, inert to Jinja entirely), and `tests/test_infra_shell_templates_render.py` walks **every** `roles/*/templates/*.sh.j2` through Ansible's own Templar plus `bash -n`, with a registry that fails on an unregistered template. The generalisable lesson, recorded in the test's own docstring: a template test that does not use the renderer the fleet uses proves nothing — and `bash -n` only catches welds that produce syntax errors, so it is a floor, not a ceiling.
- **T0111 closes with nothing deferred out of it.** Its accepted residuals (mutate-probe's bare rc 1 on a missing `--file`, several unpinned inner rules of the git-mv hook, subshell-paren `cd` judged from the process cwd, `mv`+`git add` deliberately unwatched) are recorded as measured, fail-safe and consciously unfixed — none is a live deferral, so none needed re-homing before archiving.

## 2026-08-05 — the presentation layer: 18 charted families against 40 that could page you (iter-127, T0020 built)

- **The gap was measured before anything was designed, and it was the whole argument.** The committed dashboards charted **18** metric families; the alert rules fired on **40**. Roughly 32 families could page an operator with **no graph anywhere** — including all 13 capture families, all 8 engine families, and the entire continuity verify-replay sweep (11 families, four of them paging critical). Three of the four `NAS ·` panels were not NAS panels at all: they carried no `host` matcher, so each had silently become a five-host panel under a one-host title as the fleet grew. An operator paged at 03:00 had nowhere to look, and the board that existed was lying about which machine it showed.
- **Four boards, split by who is asking**: `Fleet health`, `Data integrity`, `Engine`, and a reworked `Logs`. The alert layer went **58 → 63 rules** — two capture liveness rules, two engine liveness rules, and an engine ERROR-log rule. That last one closed a hole nobody had noticed: `container="engine"` was matched by **no rule at all**, so an ERROR line from the component that moves money paged nobody. Every rule now carries `__dashboardUid__`/`__panelId__`, so a page lands on the panel plotting that rule's own expression rather than merely one showing the same family.
- **The governing principle came from the owner and is two-sided**: coverage is *family*-level, never rule-level — two rules on one family are served by one timeseries showing both thresholds — **and** a firing alert must point at the panel that shows it. `tests/test_dashboards_cover_metrics.py` makes the first half mechanical; the pointer annotations make the second.
- **The most instructive defect was in that guard itself, and it was invisible until a reviewer attacked it.** The test existed *because* `test_infra_alloy_series.py` checks keep-list admission from a hand-curated per-host list, and is therefore blind to a family missing from that list — which is exactly how the bridgehead's collector-failure hole survived it. The new test was to derive its expectations from `alerts.yaml` instead. It derived them **from the keep-regexes it audits**. Removing one family fired it correctly; removing *two* made the host drop silently out of the derived set, and all 15 tests passed — the hole reopened one line wider, caught only by the hand-curated list it was replacing. A guard whose self-description is false is worse than a missing one, because everything downstream rests on it. It now uses the topology already pinned in its own constants.
- **Reasoning that looked right on paper and failed against the live system, five times.** A `for: 2m` that detected its motivating 209-second blackout in **12.1 %** of scrape/eval alignments, where both reviewing lenses had estimated ~47 % and the "obvious" fix of `for: 1m` still missed it 13 % of the time. A `for: 35m` premised on a restart re-running a failed boundary — which `startup_action` refuses outright once an artifact exists, so the outcome was final the instant the sidecar landed. A log-line hoist that worked, parsed, and returned the wrong 200 characters, because `__line__` is the *raw* line and the direct-ship daemons emit JSON, so ~150 characters were `{"ts":…,"level":…}` and the message truncated mid-sentence. A panel painting absence **green** on the one engine signal whose rule pages critical on no-data. And a Go template branch that was silently unreachable, because `eq .Labels.msg ""` behaves as documented only under `missingkey=zero` — under Go's default a missing key is *invalid* and `eq` returns false without erroring, so the dead-canary sentence never rendered on precisely the seven unlabelled alerts it exists for.
- **Verification kept moving from the headline to the thing the defect actually moves.** Every guard was proven to trip against a constructed defect through `mutate-probe.sh`, never asserted. The `__line__` and container enumerations were run against **live Loki**, with a negative control proving the `drop` stage is load-bearing (removing it returns a real `HTTP 400 JSONParserErr`). Both the implementer and the reviewer of the notification templates found a Go toolchain on the box and **executed** them against Grafana-shaped payloads under both `missingkey` options rather than reasoning about them. The 61-kept-days retention finding was settled by running the actual prune script against 200 fabricated day-dirs.
- **The same failure shape recurred seven times: a number corrected in one place and left stale in another.** A rule's `for:` moved and the panel holding its pointer kept quoting the old one; a rollout checklist expected 62 rules against a file carrying 63, so a push dropping exactly one rule would have read back as correct and passed. The final sweep found two more unprompted after it stopped being asked. This is the argument for the coverage guard existing at all — the drift is not carelessness, it is what happens to any invariant a machine does not check.
- **Two spec claims of mine were measurably false and are corrected in place**, not annotated below themselves: that a summary could carry the host vocabulary (Grafana bakes annotations at evaluation time, before any template runs, so summaries now never interpolate `$labels.host` — enforced by a test), and the `missingkey` claim above. A third — "60 and 63 days" of journal retention — was drift: the prune's cutoff is a strict comparison against each day-dir's own ISO name, so steady state is **61** kept dirs and the oldest is 60 days *plus the hours since midnight*.
- **Deployed 2026-08-05/06, verified by outcome at every tier** (the rollout half, branch `feat/t0020-rollout`; this bullet was written "Not deployed" at build closeout and is rewritten in place now that the rollout is real). Grafana live: 4 boards, 63 rules with 63 pointers, templates byte-identical on read-back and live-fired into `#zcrypto` — the live fire caught the error branch keyed on a label Grafana never sets, fixed and re-pushed. Converge chain: secondary capture bake PASSED (3 rotation hours, all abort signals clear, prune `deleted=402`, zero truncated hours on the pulled copy), ops (the carried [[T0044]] gauge live at 73 on its first tick), bridgehead (the collector alert now spans 5 hosts), engine inside the 00→04 gap (`cycle-04.json` completed 04:01:40.93Z; the lazy `cycle_duration` gauge appeared exactly at the first cycle reading 10.932155, and `target_weight` holds exactly 10 labels — both fixes proving themselves live). The deprecated `zcrypto-main` board: deleted by hand in the UI, 404 verified, and its JSON removed from the repo in the same change because `grafana-push.sh` would silently have recreated it at the next push. **Series budget, measured at closeout**: 819 active — ops 330, zcrypto 175, nas 144, zcrypto-red 129, zaccess 39, 2 label-only strays — against spec `00043`'s <1k target. The `00069` baseline (2026-07-26) was recorded per host, never as a total: its counts sum to 694 before the 2 label-only strays this measurement includes, so like-for-like growth is ~123 series in ten days — this iteration added one family on one host, so most of that predates it and is flagged unattributed. [[T0020]] is `resolved` and archived; the rollout's own follow-ups are [[T0128]], [[T0129]] and [[T0130]] (the engine journal's constant `code_version`, surfaced by this rollout's own verification).

## 2026-08-06 — the panel regeneration told an operator to delete something it never named (iter-128, T0111 follow-up)

- **The defect surfaced as a question, not an alert.** The 2026-08-04 regeneration drill printed a closing checklist whose first item was "Delete the OLD panel copy on the NAS share" — no host, no path, no command, and nothing anywhere in the repo supplying any of them. A day later the owner could not find what to delete, which is the only reason it was looked at. `grafana-push.sh`-style tooling would have caught a missing file; nothing catches an instruction that names no operand.
- **The obvious procedure was the dangerous one.** Diff the two trees, delete what only the NAS has — which is exactly wrong, because the materialize sweep skips every hour at or below its watermark. An hour a rebuild fails to anchor is therefore absent on ops and present *only* on the NAS, indistinguishable by path from a stale orphan; deleting it destroys the last copy, and the pull runs outward from ops so nothing restores it. An adversarial review refuted the first synthesis on precisely this, after that synthesis had already been written out as a recommendation.
- **The answer turned out to be that nothing was owed at all, and it was measured rather than argued.** With the generation unchanged the two copies were bit-identical: 20071 files each side, zero paths either side lacked, all 6690 parquet `.sha256` sidecars equal — with both walks exiting 0 and a control proving the comparison could still detect a one-line change. No deletion was performed.
- **The trigger the checklist now states is narrower than the one it used to imply, and the correction went the opposite way to intuition.** The panel path carries no generation component, so a schema, notional-ladder or level-count change *forces* a regeneration but rewrites identical paths and orphans nothing. Only a grid-directory rename or a pair leaving the archive/quote scope orphans a path — and that second case is the one nothing else catches, since the generation guard runs against a tree step 4 has just emptied.
- **Two silent failure modes in the fix itself, both found by review rather than by testing.** The completion-line capture sat *after* the timer restart, and the timer is `Persistent=true` — so a queued catch-up tick could fire immediately and `tail -1` would return its numbers instead of the rebuild's, the exact shape the drill recorded (`hours_written=80 hours_skipped=6370`: plausible, reassuring, wrong). And `comm` was left unpinned while both `sort`s carried `LC_ALL=C`; under a UTF-8 locale glibc case-folds the leading letter, `comm` rejects C-sorted input as unsorted and exits 1 — three errors at the moment the operator decides whether to delete, and `ssh` forwards `LANG`.
- **The test that should have caught the rewrite asserted nothing.** It checked that `"NAS"`, `"Un-pause"` and `"ops_panel_timer_hold"` appeared — all three of which the *old* text satisfied verbatim, so the suite was byte-identically green before and after and a revert would have shipped unnoticed. The harness also stubbed only `date`/`systemctl`/`du`, so the completion block's success path had never executed anywhere: every run silently took its fallback. Both are addressed — though the superseded assertion is left in place beside the new one rather than deleted — and every new assertion is proven to bite through `mutate-probe.sh`. The first round of that proving was itself insufficient: the ordering guard was anchored to a printed banner, and `systemctl start …timer` prints nothing, so hoisting the restart above the journal read while leaving the banner alone survived it. Review constructed exactly that and the test passed. The ordering is now asserted on an ordered call log covering both commands, which is the only surface where it is observable.
- **Ships at the next ops converge**, since it is an Ansible template; nothing regresses meanwhile, the old checklist simply stays on the host. `fleet-deploys.md` was corrected in the same change — it promised an unconditional NAS-deletion and is the always-loaded text an agent reads while *planning* a regeneration, so leaving it would have kept reproducing the expectation the script change exists to kill. The spec, its plan, the earlier changelog entry and the archived topic deliberately keep the old wording: they record what was true when written.

## 2026-08-06 — the alert stack's first live event, and the decision it unblocked (iter-129, resolves T0105)

- **A real Kraken maintenance window (07:01–07:18Z) validated the whole layered design on its first firing.** The 120 s fleet-silence rule caught the blackout at 07:04, the 300 s per-pair rule named all 24 stuck streams at 07:07, the venue rule attributed the cause (`system=maintenance`) in the same minute, and both silence rules auto-resolved when the feed returned — zero human action needed, zero taken, and the venue rule's own "do not restart or converge on this signal alone" held. **~15 minutes of L2 across all 12 pairs is permanently gone, and this entry originally said the opposite.** The reconciler booked hour 07 as `both_streams_silent`, `residual_seconds = 10588.382751`, plus twelve `unwitnessed` records whose gaps run 07:01:01.1Z–07:16:19.1Z. The OPENING boundary tracks the venue's status line to the second (`maintenance` 07:01:02Z); the closing one does not — book flow resumed 9.6 s after `cancel_only` (first book 07:15:07.607301Z), 67 s before `post_only` and 190 s before `online`. The claim written here on the day ("nothing was lost… the residual-gap counter is byte-identical") was measured at ~07:20Z, two minutes after recovery, when hour 07 had not yet ended and could not be booked for another 1 h 52 m: the reconciler runs twice-hourly (`*:12,42`) and `SETTLE_HOURS = 2` made the hour ineligible until 09:00, booked 09:12. The counter was unchanged because no answer could exist yet, not because the hour was clean. An unprocessed interval is not a verified-empty one. The `Reconciler · residual gap increased` page fired correctly on 2026-08-06 09:24Z and the counter now reads 15636.019483. What survives of the original claim is the part about blame, not about loss: the venue emitted nothing, so neither host missed anything and no fleet component failed — the data never existed to capture. Neither daemon restarted (`RestartCount` 0 on both).
- **The event delivered the number T0105's parked pre-drain decision was waiting on, and it is zero**: `effective_time=None` on all five state transitions, both hosts — Kraken gave no advance notice, and the status message itself (~60 s ahead of feed silence) is the earliest signal that exists. The pre-drain is **consciously dropped** (owner ruling, decisions log): nothing exists to key it on, and building against the still-unobserved planned-maintenance shape is the mechanism-nobody-proves-runs pattern the topic's own findings warn against. The right-sized remnant — the 6b executor checking venue state at cycle entry before placing orders — is recorded as an input to T0018's order-submission item, where the order path is designed anyway.
- **T0105 is resolved and archived** with the full measured event record (the runbook's procedure sent it there); the runbook's venue procedure was rewritten per its own retire-when instruction, and the counter-presence alert silenced time-boxed to 08-13 on its rule uid — it stays firing until the next capture daemon restart clears the counter, which is days-to-weeks away and gated on a bake.
- **One instrument correction the event exposed**: `ALERTS{alertstate="firing"}` is structurally empty for Grafana-managed rules — all of ours — so its `(no series)` reads as "nothing firing" regardless of reality; `grafana-query.py`'s docstring now names the API endpoint that actually answers the question.

## 2026-08-07 — the permanent-loss page was right, and the entry above it was wrong (iter-130)

- **A CRITICAL `Reconciler · residual gap increased` page fired on 2026-08-06 09:24Z and re-notified every four hours until investigated on 08-07.** It was correct. The residual counter moved 5047.636732 → 15636.019483, and the ledger names the cause exactly: one `both_streams_silent` record for hour 2026-08-06 07:00Z carrying `residual_seconds = 10588.382751`, which ties to the counter delta to six decimals, plus twelve `unwitnessed` records whose gaps run 07:01:01.1Z–07:16:19.1Z. The opening boundary tracks the venue's own status line to the second (`maintenance` logged 07:01:02Z vs earliest gap 07:01:01.107346Z); the closing one does **not** — the latest gap ends 07:16:19.077883Z, **119 s before** `online` at 07:18:18Z, nearest the `post_only` transition at 07:16:15Z. Book flow resumed at the ladder's **`cancel_only`** step: the first book message after the halt was 07:15:07.607301Z (BTC/EUR), ~9.6 s after `cancel_only` at 07:14:58Z and **68 s before** `post_only` at 07:16:15Z, with ten more streams resuming 07:15:39–07:15:40Z. The draft of this entry attributed it to `post_only` — the same species of ladder mis-attribution it had just corrected one clause earlier. **~15 minutes of L2 book data across all 12 pairs is permanently gone.**
- **The failure worth recording is not the loss — it is how confidently it was denied.** The iter-129 entry above, and T0105's resolution, both stated "nothing was lost" on the strength of the residual counter reading identically before and after. That reading was taken at ~07:20Z — and it was not merely early, it was structurally meaningless. Hour 07 had **not yet ended**; the reconcile timer runs **twice-hourly** (`*:12,42`), not hourly; and `SETTLE_HOURS = 2` puts the hour's first possible booking at 09:12, ~1 h 50 m after the read. The counter was unchanged because no answer could exist yet, not because the hour was clean and not because a job was running late. Both records are corrected in place rather than annotated, since the original sentences read first and more confidently than any note beneath them.
- **The generalisable rule: an unprocessed interval is not a verified-empty one — and freshness is not coverage.** This is "an empty filtered query is not an absent event" one level up: the filter was valid, the query was right, and the *producer of the answer had not run for that interval yet*. The first draft of this entry named `zcrypto_reconcile_last_success_timestamp_seconds` as the one-query check that would have caught it. **It would not have.** Measured: at 07:20Z that gauge read 06:42Z — perfectly fresh for a timer that runs `*:12,42` — and at 08:42Z it read 08:42Z, maximally fresh, while hour 07 was still unbooked. It is run liveness, not a coverage watermark, and it answers reassuringly in exactly the situation that misleads you. The sound check is arithmetic, not a gauge: `SETTLE_HOURS = 2` (`cli/archive/settle.py`) means **hour H is first bookable at H+2 h, at the next `:12`/`:42` tick**. Hour 07 was therefore unbookable until 09:12, which is when it was in fact booked. Before reading any derived counter as evidence of absence, compute when its producer could first have covered the interval — and prefer that arithmetic over any freshness signal, because a freshness signal is designed to look good.
- **The correction itself was first written too narrowly — against a rule that already existed.** `agent-ops.md` already says: *"Sweep for the CLAIM, not the phrase you wrote — enumerate its variants before grepping, re-sweep after each fix round, and name the point-in-time records deliberately left carrying the old wording."* The draft violated a standing instruction rather than exposing a missing one, so no new rule is owed and this bullet is a record, not a proposal. It fixed the two records written as prose — this changelog and T0105's resolution — and left `infra/runbooks/README.md`, which is the **operator-facing** one: it stated that a venue-side halt "books no loss", instructed the responder to read the residual counter while pre-supplying the answer, and generalised the mistake into a standing rule for every future halt. Correcting the records one authored while leaving the record one operates by makes a correction *look* complete while the failure mode stays armed. The runbook now states the loss, and carries the H+2 h arithmetic in place of the counter read.
- **No fleet component failed, and that half of the original claim stands**: the venue emitted nothing, so neither capture host missed anything that existed, `RestartCount` remained 0 on both, and no restart or converge was warranted — the venue rule's "do not act on this signal alone" was correct guidance throughout. What was wrong was the leap from *"we did nothing wrong"* to *"nothing was lost"*.
- **The 178.272850 s between the two ledger measures is fully accounted for, and the first draft of this entry explained it wrongly.** The twelve `unwitnessed` gaps sum to 10766.655601 against 10588.382751 booked. `unwitnessed` is ledgered for visibility and counted **nowhere** — counting it beside `both_streams_silent` would double-book the same seconds. That part was right. The mechanism was not: the draft said `fleet_dark_windows` "bounds each stream's window by whichever stream returned first", which describes the **superseded** pre-`containing_dark_window` booking. The landed code books each stream's **own** window, and only when that window *contains* a fleet-wide intersection — deliberately, to keep thin-market false positives out of a counter that can never be walked back. The difference decomposes exactly: **177.633976 s** is five streams' *second* dark window, which opened at or after the intersection closed and so contains none (ADA 36.318063, LTC 39.191033, ETH/BTC 35.851998, XRP 35.928438, LINK 30.344444); **0.638874 s** is three streams whose own window starts fractionally later than the primary-only gap because `pair_stamps` carries secondary stamps too. Nothing is unexplained.
- **The precedent the draft leaned on was not comparable, and the honest figure is smaller.** The 1.27 % recorded at `cli/archive/command.py` measures own-window versus intersection×count — a defect that was *fixed*. Its like-for-like successor here is **62.494755 s = 0.59 %**, not the 1.66 % the draft quoted as "same order". Borrowing a number that measures something else is how a plausible explanation survives review.
- **State the substance plainly: the true loss is ~10766.66 s, about 178 s MORE than the counter reports.** The code calls this direction "in the reassuring direction" — a caution, not a reassurance — and the draft rewrote it as "the safe way for it to be wrong". Under-reporting is defensible here, because the counter is monotone and can never be walked back, so the design prefers to omit seconds it cannot attribute rather than book seconds it might have to retract. But in an entry about a reassuring reading producing a false record, restating a caution as a comfort is precisely the reflex under examination.

## 2026-08-08 — the notional ladder learns about quotes, and the calibration covers all twelve legs (iter-131, spec 00085, resolves T0092)

- **The panel's fill ladder is per-quote.** `NOTIONALS_EUR` became `NOTIONALS_BY_QUOTE` (`cli/panel/primitives.py`): EUR pairs keep 100/1k/10k EUR, and BTC-quoted pairs use the BTC quantities worth those EUR amounts at a pinned `BTC_EUR_REFERENCE = 55876.28413495087`, measured over its own fixed window. Column names are unchanged, so the grid point `fill_bps_bid_1k` names is the same on every pair and the values stay comparable across quotes. `PANEL_QUOTE` and the four EUR-scope guards are gone; the generation manifest carries `notionals_by_quote`, so any ladder change forces a regeneration on its own and `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays 2.
- **The FX reference is pinned to the TREE, not to a calibration window.** It defines what every BTC `fill_bps_*` column *means*, so a later recalibration must not move it — `calibrate()` returns 55888.13 over the new calibration window and that divergence is correct, not a defect. The guarantee is mechanical: the manifest holds the derived BTC rungs, so changing the constant makes `_check_generation` refuse every existing tree at the next sweep.
- **The whole panel tree was regenerated** (ops converged to `65402dc67701`, then `zcrypto-panel-regenerate` 2026-08-07 12:55:29→22:26:13Z, 9 h 30 m at 4.37 s/hour): `pairs=12 pairs_out_of_scope=0 hours_written=7836 hours_skipped=0 hours_unsettled=72 hours_unanchored=0 rows=28103636 errors=0`. Both `/BTC` subtrees exist for the first time, and their `fill_bps_*` columns read **100 % non-null** and monotonic in size (ETH/BTC 0.684→1.259 bps, SOL/BTC 0.910→2.672) where all six were previously null.
- **The regeneration orphaned nothing, measured rather than argued.** `LC_ALL=C comm -13` over the ops and NAS path sets returned **0** NAS-only paths, so no deletion was owed — the first real exercise of the checklist rule that a ladder bump rewrites identical paths.
- **The wrong-run trap fired for real and was avoided by construction.** The materialize timer is `Persistent=true`, so its catch-up tick ran 9 minutes after the rebuild and logged `hours_written=120 hours_skipped=7836` — plausible, reassuring, and the wrong run. Reading the completion line anchored to the rebuild (never `tail -1`) is what caught it; `7836 + 120 = 7956` reconciles with the tree's file count.
- **`SPREAD_CALIBRATION` is keyed by full symbol** with twelve rows, over one window shared by every row: `2026-07-23T14:00:00Z … 2026-08-07T19:00:00Z`, 365 h / 15.21 days, `min_rows == max_rows == 1,314,000` — zero missing seconds and perfect joint sampling, against 0.602 % shared gaps in the superseded window. A base-keyed table returned the EUR row for `"ETH"` while raising for `"ETH/BTC"`; the re-key converts a silently wrong value into a loud failure, and a mutation probe proves it (reverting to a base lookup is KILLED). `cli/data/rebuild.py`'s quote guard collapsed to plain membership — safe only after the ladder went per-quote *and* the table gained `/BTC` rows.
- **The EUR rows moved materially, and the spec's estimate was wrong.** 9 of 10 pairs move >2 % at some rung, worst −25.01 % (DOT @1k), against spec D4's "under 2 %, per T0091's precedent". The move is the window, not the pipeline: re-running the committed script over the OLD window against the REGENERATED tree still reproduces the OLD table exactly. That check is now a permanent control holding the superseded values as literals so it cannot follow the restamp it exists to verify. The standing lesson is that one window's calibration is a point estimate, not a constant.
- **A near-miss worth keeping: the exit bar was almost lost silently.** The window originally drafted for this restamp ended 2026-08-06T06:00Z and spans **13.67 days**, below Phase 2's "≥2 weeks of captured spreads" row that T0091 discharged at 14.68 days and a 13.1-day predecessor explicitly failed. Every other assertion — table, provenance stamp, row counts, the reproduction control — is blind to span, so it would have shipped green. There is now a test that reads the two timestamps and trips on exactly that window. Taking the longer window admits Kraken's 2026-08-06 07:01–07:18Z maintenance (0.078 % of the window, frozen book), which is disclosed in `captured-spread-calibration.md` rather than trimmed: a 17-minute gap is disclosable, a regressed criterion is invisible.

## 2026-08-08 — the engine's ERROR rule gets a dead canary, and the measurement nearly went the wrong way (iter-132, resolves T0128)

- **`zcrypto-engine-log-dead` added** (`infra/grafana/alerts.yaml`, group `zcrypto-gate`): a fires-on-silence dead-man for the trading engine's log plane, mirroring `zcrypto-capture-log-dead-primary` in shape. The `Engine · ERROR logs` rule is presence-fired, so it is silent in two states that are indistinguishable from outside — the engine is healthy, or its shipping path regressed and no line arrives at all. The capture daemon has had this cover since spec `00068`; the engine had none.
- **The engine is a BURST emitter, and that decides the window.** Measured over the **full 12.6 days** Loki serves for this selector (974 lines, bucketed locally): it emits ~11 lines within ~90 s of each 4-hourly boundary and nothing between, **no gap anywhere exceeds 4.5 h**, and every one of ~76 boundaries produced a burst. (The first read spanned 72 h — short of this topic's own "take the whole of the served window"; the full span was read at review and the characterisation is unchanged.) So a line-count canary is the right instrument, but only above 4 h; anything at or under it false-fires on every healthy inter-cycle gap.
- **Window 6 h = the measured 4.00 h period + the fleet's own 2 h margin**, the same shape its ops timer canaries use (26 h for a daily emitter). Minimum lines observed in any 6 h window: 11, max 34, so the `< 1` threshold fires only when a whole cycle burst goes missing.
- **The measurement trap — and the first explanation of it was itself wrong.** A `count_over_time` range query reads this stream back as "every hour has lines", i.e. a continuous emitter; that reading was taken, believed and written down before raw entries overturned it. The mechanism first recorded — "Loki forces ~240 min spacing regardless of the `step` requested" — is **false**, caught at review and tested directly: `step=600` returns 600 s spacing, so nothing is being forced. The real cause is that `count_over_time` emits **no sample for a step whose range window is empty**, so on a burst emitter only the burst hours survive and they sit one cycle apart. The cheap fix is the `or on() vector(0)` the rule already carries — with it, `step=1h` returns the zero hours. The wrong story would have sent the next reader to page raw entries and to distrust a tool that was working correctly; `alerts.yaml` now carries the corrected version.
- **Two things checked rather than assumed**: `level=~".+"` is present on the engine stream (values `INFO`) — an absent label would match nothing and park the rule permanently firing; and retention serves this selector back 7 days at full rate, so it was never the binding constraint.
- **The panel half landed with it.** `Logs` panel 103 gains the engine's own dead-man series (refId `I`) with the same right-axis + threshold-at-1 override its seven siblings carry — so the alert's `__panelId__` pointer leads to a chart that actually plots what the rule evaluates, rather than merely resolving. Its description now warns what the shared linear axis does: the engine sits at **11–34** where capture sits at **747–1348**, so a healthy engine renders near the floor — **read the threshold line at 1, not the height**.
- **The pointer is guarded by construction.** `test_every_rule_points_at_a_real_panel_or_a_runbook` was proven to bite on this specific rule via `mutate-probe.sh` — a dangling `__panelId__` is KILLED, with the control (a bad `__dashboardUid__`) proven to fail first, so the probe's verdict counts.
- **Live and verified the same night; T0128 `resolved` and archived.** Pushed via `grafana-push.sh`: folder **63 → 64 rules**, **no orphaned rules** (purely additive, nothing to prune). Verified **by value, not presence** — the rule's own query returned **32** against the measured 11–34 band, far above its `< 1` threshold — and confirmed evaluating rather than merely stored: `state=inactive health=ok lastError=none`, 0.12 s per evaluation. Presence alone would not have distinguished a healthy rule from one reading 0 through a wrong selector.

## 2026-08-09 — the dispose race reaches production after all (dependabot round; T0115 → partial)

- **T0115's central safety claim was false, and the correction is the whole point of this entry.** The topic held that the race "cannot bite production today: the live engine *runs* its node, so teardown takes `loop.stop()`, never `loop.close()`". Traced rather than assumed: `TradingNode.dispose()` branches on the **loop's state at dispose time**, not on whether the node ran — `if not loop.is_closed(): if loop.is_running(): loop.stop() else: loop.close()`. `zcrypto engine run` is a **synchronous** Typer command, so no loop is running when it calls `node.run()`; `run()` therefore takes `run_until_complete(run_async())`, which returns with the loop **not running and not closed** (measured directly), and nothing in `kernel.dispose()` closes it. The `finally: node.dispose()` therefore reaches `loop.close()` — the same branch that SIGABRTed CI 3 of 4 runs. **The live engine executes the aborting path on every shutdown.**
- **The error was a grep read as more than it measured.** The cheap "does anything else dispose a never-run node?" sweep found exactly one production site and was then read as establishing *which branch* that site takes. It never could: it counted sites. A first draft of this work stamped the inherited claim "confirmed by measurement" and was caught in review — the failure mode is quoting a measurement for a proposition it does not address, which is the same shape as the `count_over_time` misreading recorded on 2026-08-08.
- **What is still honestly unmeasured**: whether the Rust threads are mid-unwind at that moment on a *run* node, which is what decides whether the race actually fires in production rather than merely being reachable. A next step now reads the engine container's recorded exit codes for a `134`, since every past converge shut down through this path — history is the cheapest available evidence. The option of closing T0115 on "unreachable from production" is struck.
- **`infra/scripts/nautilus-dispose-probe.py` added** — the per-bump dispose probe as runnable code rather than a procedure described in prose; rebuilding it from the description had already picked the wrong `EngineConfig` import once. Probed **1.231.0 at 24/24 disposes clean** and **1.230.0 at 18/18 clean**; the second is why the first proves nothing, since 1.230.0 is the version whose defect is known. **The probe can escalate, never exonerate** — recorded on the instrument itself so a future clean run is not misread as a fix.
- **Proving the probe's own guard exposed a defect in it.** A stub child exiting 0 after one of three disposes is now caught as `INCONCLUSIVE`, and zero rounds is refused outright — but constructing that case showed every non-signal failure was being reported as "the race is locally reachable", i.e. an import error from the very bump under test would have manufactured a race finding. The verdicts are now split: only a **signal death** claims the race; anything else reports `NO VERDICT`.
- **The dependabot round that surfaced it**: coverage 7.15.3 (#271) and typer 0.27.1 (#274) merged; **nautilus-trader 1.231.0 (#270) stopped unmerged** by `tests/test_nautilus_adapter.py`'s deliberate version pin, which `docs/reference/adapter-verification/1.230.0.md` binds to an attended re-run of the order-semantics probes (~€0.20 of real zero-fill orders). The pin caught what the "escalate on major versions" heuristic would have waved through — 1.230.0 → 1.231.0 is a *minor* bump.

## 2026-08-09 — dataset provenance becomes what the run read, and the registry gets its first real door (iter-133, spec 00086, closes T0065's execution-reproducibility round)

- **The gap, restated because its shape drove everything**: the registry validated `dataset_hash` as "a non-empty str" and nothing more, so whatever an uncommitted scratchpad driver passed became permanent provenance. For **44 of 46** records it can no longer be resolved — `ba47e37e` (38 records) and `81dc9b44` (4) resisted ~226,000 candidate recipes, and `git log --all --diff-filter=D` confirms no driver was ever committed and deleted.
- **Two earlier designs failed nine cold-review rounds, and the third succeeded by removing the act both attached to.** A generic manifest reader died in four rounds (10→6→6→5 blocking) on one recorded root cause — *the manifest ecosystem has no contract*; an allowlist reshape ran five more (13→8→6→4→5) and was stopped by judgement, not convergence. Both derived identity from what manifests **declare**, to guard `append()` — a door with **no production caller**. This design computes identity from the **bytes a run reads** and gives the door a user. The wall is not handled better; it is unreachable. Round 2 of the new pair returned **SHIP + SHIP**, the first double-SHIP of the eleven rounds.
- **`cli/registry/observed.py`** — `ObservedReader` hashes each parquet's bytes at read time, applies any window **itself** (so rows-used cannot drift from rows-recorded), and accumulates `{files, rows, span}`. A new dataset backing a trial needs zero provenance code: no allowlist entry, no adapter. A windowed fit moves `rows`/`span` honestly, closing the sample-window inexpressibility the allowlist design had conceded.
- **Schema 4** — `dataset_hash = compute_hash(datasets)` through the registry's own hashing, so the derivation cannot be lost without breaking `record_hash`; `append()` offers no argument to supply it, and re-validates the finished record before writing (the file is append-only and hash-chained, so one bad line is permanent). Enforced at **load**: block present, shaped, digest re-derives — plus a floor requiring `schema_version >= 4` past **trial 46**, by id because ids cannot grow retroactively. Without that floor nothing compels a *new* record to say 4: the next writer templates off record 46, which says 3, and `{"schema_version": 3, "dataset_hash": "ba47e37e"}` would reproduce the original failure verbatim and load clean forever. All 46 committed records load untouched.
- **`zcrypto research eval` is the door** — the first production caller of `append()` in this project's history, with `record44-crossfreq` and `record33-combined` as committed subjects. Registration requires a committed subject, making the discipline record 44 already followed into the paved path rather than an unwritten norm.
- **The legacy ruling is executed, not narrated.** `docs/reference/legacy-dataset-pins.jsonl` carries the epistemics **in the referent value**, so a careless grep cannot return a bare path reading as verified fact: `81dc9b44` unrecoverable with a null referent, the two inferred rows marked INFERRED inline, and `cccb8d17` reproduced by a test that **executes** its recipe and names its unrecoverable operand.
- **A cold review corrected the spec, not the code** — D6 claimed trials 33/35 "also read 4h"; the registry shows both ran from scratchpad drivers with daily-cadence notes, so committed state establishes no such thing. The pins row stated the honest negative and the spec was amended to match. The design's own rule — claim only what is measured — biting its author is the intended behaviour.
- **The instrument-blindness lesson, third instance this week and the sharpest.** Round 1 caught the loader comparing **file-byte** hashes against manifests that vouch **frame-content** hashes (`dataset_hash` over canonical CSV): it would have refused every healthy read of `ohlc-full`/`ohlc-15m` with the false diagnosis "the file changed since the freeze", killing both production subjects — **while CI stayed green**, because the suite had mismatch cases and no true-positive. Probe 9 now reconstructs that mutation verbatim and confirms the trap: under it, the mismatch and absence cases still pass and only the true-positive fails.
- **All nine mutation probes KILLED**, and two harness problems were reported rather than papered over: probe 6's first control was *rejected* by `mutate-probe.sh` because the **test** was weak (`match="nothing"` is rescued by a second "nothing" in the same message — since tightened and proven to discriminate), and probe 8's killing assertion turned out to be the exit-code one, so a probe 8b was added to fire the no-write half directly. Recorded for whoever re-verifies: probe 7's guard exits non-zero on **both** sides of its defect — only the plurality-of-named-files assertion discriminates, never `$?`.
- **Verification runs where it can**: the record-44 control drives the runner's own loading path and reproduces the registered `governor_engaged_bars` 7302 / `cap_breach_bars` 1318 plus the frozen 20-file / 202,405-row slice (workstation, skip-with-reason elsewhere); the full capture→append→load→re-derive pipeline runs on **bare-checkout CI** over synthetic parquets — coverage the manifest-reading designs could not have had. The disk conformance pass re-hashes what records cite, with `absent-here` legal only for dirs in a committed, empty-at-birth exceptions list, so an unresolvable citation cannot be laundered as merely-elsewhere; it is gated on the data root's presence, without which the first schema-4 record would turn every CI run permanently red.
- **T0065's execution-reproducibility round is closed whole** — recipe, command and ruling in one round, nothing parked. REACH remains its only open work, and its two halves have different gates: the live-trades→bars materializer is autonomous and **ripe now**, while the Q2/Q3 OHLCVT ingest waits on Kraken publishing those dumps.

## 2026-08-10 — the trade tape becomes a bar dataset, and a clock stops being trusted (iter-134, spec 00087, closes T0065's REACH autonomous half)

- **`tape-bars` ships** (`cli/tick/materialize.py`, `zcrypto tick materialize`): 15m OHLCV daily finals built from the captured trade tape, read **reconciled-first**, with 60/240/1440 *derived* from the base rather than materialized. It exists because below 4h nothing else reaches the present — REST's window recedes (the 2026-07-23 reach round proved it: 1h came back detached across an 83.7-day gap, 15m could not reach at all) and the OHLCVT dumps are quarterly, while the captured tape accrues. **Q2 2026 was verified unpublished on 2026-08-10**, so the ingest half stays externally blocked and this half is what could be done.
- **The derivation is exact, and the spec names why the tempting form is wrong.** `ticks_to_bars` computes a *true tick-weighted* vwap, so `Σ(vwapᵢ·volᵢ)` telescopes to `Σ(price·volume)` and the coarse vwap re-derives as `Σ(vwapᵢ·volᵢ)/Σ(volᵢ)`. A plain mean of sub-bar vwaps is what a reimplementation reaches for and is wrong on any non-uniform volume; a probe seds exactly that formula, keeping the alias so it fails **by value rather than by crash** — a crash would prove the line load-bearing without proving the test catches a silently wrong number. Measured honestly: exact in arithmetic, *not* bit-identical in floats (~5.8e-16 relative on a lopsided fixture).
- **The load-bearing decision was reversed by review, and the reversal is the lesson.** Publishing was first gated on wall-clock — 26 h past day end, derived from the healer's nominal cadence. That is a **proxy** for "the backfill has run", and the healer's *designed* delay modes break it by an order of magnitude: the fail-closed NAS gate skips a cycle, the UTC-day stamp is written *before* the run so one failure costs the whole day's attempt, and a leftover container after a dockerd crash extends an outage by up to 24 h. With no rewrite path, a proxy failure is silent, permanent, and indistinguishable from a quiet market. The direct check existed unused: `cli/trades/gaps.py::detect` proves `trade_id` contiguity from the bytes. **Heal-completeness is now measured**; the constant survives only as a cheap pre-filter.
- **A second premise fell the same way, and withdrawing it simplified the design.** A rule refusing any day missing one of its 24 hours would have made every day containing a *quiet* hour permanently unpublishable — the capture writer commits no final for an hour with no events (`if not parts: return`), and zero-print trades hours are production-measured (`settle.py` records LINK/EUR trading in hours 01 and 04 on 2026-07-14 with zero between). `trade_id` contiguity already tells a missing hour from a quiet one; file presence never could.
- **`detect`'s endpoint blindness needed a second fix.** It bounds its span by the first and last observed id and treats neither as a gap, so over exactly one day a boundary hole reads clean. The first repair extended by one adjacent hour — wrong for the same reason as above, since the adjacent hour is often legitimately absent, and review demonstrated a **permanent short publish** through it. The span now reaches the nearest *present* segment each side, with both real edges ruled: no successor means the live edge and is refused, no predecessor means the genesis day, where the endpoint rule is correct.
- **The verification problem, and the perishable answer.** The tape starts 2026-07-08; `ohlc-full` ends 2026-03-31 — they do **not** overlap, so there is no canonical to check against. Kraken REST at 15m does, for ~7.5 days. The control ran here and passed on real tape (BTC/EUR 2026-08-09, 96 bars; OHLC and trade count exact, volume to 6.6e-16), cannot pass vacuously, and skips *distinctly* on a network failure so downtime never indicts the data. It surfaced a durable venue fact: **Kraken publishes `vwap` truncated to the pair's price precision** — 96/96 bars match under ROUND_DOWN to 0.1, 53/96 under round-half-even — so the assertion is the exact half-open tick identity, not a widened tolerance.
- **Two counters exist because the failure mode is a green silence.** The unhealed path exits 0 *by design*, so a stalled healer would freeze the watermark while every surface reported success. `days_gap` counts settled, unpublished days that have fallen outside the re-scan window — computed from the calendar and the published set, so it keeps reporting *after* a hole becomes permanent, which is exactly when every other counter drops it. The staleness rule reads `last_publish`, never `last_success`, for the same reason. Both are wired to alert rules and a dashboard panel, with runbook sections written when the link guard caught the summaries citing anchors that did not exist.
- **All eleven mutation probes killed their named guard**, plus a supplementary 9b added because probe 9's control and mutation failed at the same assertion and so could not separate harness bite from guard bite — reported as measured rather than tuned until it looked convincing.
- **Two durability defects were found by review and fixed against an os-level trace**: `publish_day` fsynced the directory once after both renames, which persists bytes but not *order*, so a crash could leave a final visible with its sidecar unpersisted — permanent, since the `.exists()` skip means it is never re-minted; and `publish_day` sat outside the sweep's `try`, so an `ENOSPC` aborted every remaining pair for that tick.
- **DEPLOYED 2026-08-10 14:49:08Z** (this bullet was written "Not deployed" at closeout and is rewritten in place now that the rollout is real). The ops timer, its gauges, the two alert rules and the dashboard panel are live: `zcrypto-tape-bars.timer` enabled and active since that stamp, and the series that this bullet said read `(no series)` now read `zcrypto_tapebars_exit_code 0`, `zcrypto_tapebars_days_gap 0`, `zcrypto_tapebars_last_publish_timestamp_seconds` 2026-08-16T02:52:10Z. The converge was attended and passed `-e ops_alloy_digest=<currently-running>` as owed, because `config.alloy` was edited to admit the new series. The full suite caught that omission — the gauges matched no keep-regex and would have made the alert rules read `NoData` forever.
- **T0065's REACH round now has only its externally-gated half left**; nothing autonomous remains anywhere in that topic.

## 2026-08-11 — the execution safety envelope: two keys, a kill switch, a venue gate, and a ledger nothing can submit through yet (iter-135, spec/plan 00088, T0018 partial)

- **What shipped, none of it able to submit an order.** The first of T0018's risk-first five-spec decomposition (`00088`→`00092`, table in `docs/open-topics/T0018-phase6-build-sequence.md`): a two-key arming interlock (the `exec_armed` config key, rendered `false`, plus a host-side arm file — both must be true), a latching kill switch that overrides everything, a fail-closed venue-status gate reading Kraken's public `SystemStatus` at process start and after each cycle completes, with a 30 s snapshot bound, a restart-triggered reduce-only hold latched unconditionally at every `engine run` start and cleared only by a human, a per-cycle execution ledger (`exec-<HH>.json`, structurally disjoint from `cycle-*.json`/`failed-cycle-*.json` so a refusal to trade can never read as a broken research day to the Stage-6a concordance glob), six `zcrypto_exec_*` gauges wired through the existing metrics sink, an Engine-board dashboard row, three alert rules with runbook sections, and `zcrypto engine exec-status` for the operator's own view of every input the gate read. The deployed engine was already an authenticated trading client with zero submission call sites anywhere — nothing structurally distinguished "half-built" from "live" before this landed. Order submission itself is `00090`, not this spec.
- **The shipped `_present` in `cli/engine/execgate.py` diverges from the plan's Task 3 snippet, and the divergence is deliberate — the code is authoritative.** `_present` now splits its failure semantics by file: the arm file (`ARM_FILE`) fails closed by reading **absent** on any doubt (`Path.exists()`, `except OSError: return False`), while the kill file and restart-hold file fail closed by reading **present** on any doubt, via a direct `os.lstat()` where only `FileNotFoundError` maps to absent and every other `OSError` or `ValueError` maps to present-and-refuse. The two directions are opposite because absence is what *arms* the first key and what *permits* the other two — a fail direction that reads "can't tell" as "safe" is wrong for a file whose absence is safety.
- **Three review-driven fix rounds got the asymmetry right, each catching what the previous round left open.** Round 1 (`6530c28f`) found three fail-open paths inherited from the plan's own reference code: `Path.exists()`/`os.path.lexists()` swallow every `OSError` into `False` for the kill/hold files too, a naive `observed_at` raised `TypeError` out of `evaluate()` instead of refusing, and the venue-snapshot cache accepted a backwards clock step as still-fresh. Round 2 (`4ea3006c`) found that round 1's `os.path.lexists` swap left the `except OSError` branch on the kill/hold files unreachable — `lexists`, like `Path.exists`, swallows `OSError` internally before it ever reaches the caller's `try`, so the round-1 fix silently fixed nothing; replaced with a direct `os.lstat()`. Round 3 (`45ddf228`) caught that round 2's `os.lstat()` raises `ValueError`, not `OSError`, for a path containing an embedded NUL byte — a regression round 2 introduced by moving off the wrapper that used to swallow both.
- **Task 9's mutation sweep ended at 38 probe rows, 32 killed, 6 survived** (2 deliberate uniqueness cross-checks, 4 real gaps). The four real gaps (`ec7f9dad`) were the arm-file `except OSError` branch — unproven because this Python's `pathlib` already swallows every real-filesystem failure the suite can construct before the except clause is ever reached, forced to raise directly so it fails closed if genuinely hit; two unprobed `isinstance` guards in `venue.py` (a JSON array body, a body with no `"result"` key) whose deletion would raise `AttributeError` out of a function documented to never raise; and Task 9's row 23, the `status`-is-a-nonempty-string guard, whose miss was refusal-preserving — the gate still refused — but silently degraded the operator-facing status to `None` instead of `"unreadable"`, caught by pinning the status string (not just `ok`) on the existing missing-status-key test.
- **Making the metrics sink unconditional — so the execution ledger is not a side effect of telemetry being switched on — leaked a live-network gate across the whole unit suite.** `cycle._metrics_sink` is module-global process state; only `test_engine_metrics.py` reset it, so every `engine run()` test in `test_engine_command.py` left a live closure behind (a real `ExecutionGate` pointed at a torn-down `tmp_path`) that fired for every later test sharing the process — confirmed 8 blocked outbound connection attempts to `api.kraken.com` and 18 stray `write_exec_record` calls into a dead `journal_dir` when running two test files together, silent both times because `cycle.py`'s own sink guard swallows a raising sink by design. Fixed (`bb2693eb`) with a new autouse `tests/conftest.py` fixture resetting the sink after every test suite-wide, plus reordering `_sink` to write the ledger record before either gauge group is touched so a raising gauge update can no longer cost that cycle's ledger entry.
- **The plan specified a `zcrypto_exec_venue_snapshot_age_seconds` gauge; it shipped as `zcrypto_exec_last_evaluation_timestamp_seconds` instead**, replaced during the cold-review fold (`7c3ab7aa`) once the age gauge was shown to always read ~0 by construction — evaluations are hours apart, the snapshot bound is 30 s, so every read the gauge could ever publish is fresh, making it a permanently-reassuring number that answers nothing. The heartbeat timestamp answers the question nothing else could: whether the gate is still being evaluated at all, or every published value is frozen at whatever it last read while cycle telemetry stays green. Rendered on the dashboard as an age (`time() - zcrypto_exec_last_evaluation_timestamp_seconds`) and backs the third alert rule, `zcrypto-engine-exec-not-evaluated`.
- **The three alert rules** (`5de4b380`, runbook sections in `infra/runbooks/README.md`): `zcrypto-engine-exec-armed-too-long`, `zcrypto-engine-exec-kill-tripped`, `zcrypto-engine-exec-not-evaluated`. A review round (`b443efbc`) corrected the armed-too-long runbook's claimed six-hour self-resolve — `min_over_time` actually clears at the very next evaluation after a single disarmed sample — and registered a venue-divergence alert as owed follow-up on `T0018` rather than building it here: `zcrypto_exec_venue_ok` reading not-ok while the capture daemon's own venue reading says online can only happen when the engine's own REST read path is broken while the venue is genuinely online, a case the existing capture-side venue rule cannot see since it reads a different series — and alerting on it before an order-submission call site exists would just double-page every real venue outage.
- **T0018 updated in this closeout**: the `00088` row in the five-spec table flips from "in flight" to landed; the deferral-list intro's stale "Three deferrals" is corrected to seven, matching the bullets actually listed (it had drifted as bullets were added mid-branch without the count being revisited).
- **No dataset-catalog change is owed.** This iteration introduces, relocates and retires no dataset — the execution ledger is operational state under `data/engine-journal`, not a research dataset, and follows the existing journal's own (uncatalogued, decisions-log-recorded) retention treatment.
- **Five commits carry a `Reviewed-by:` finding recorded in the closeout commit body rather than on their own commits**: `51b48e28`, `6530c28f`, `4ea3006c`, `0d9666ab`, `5de4b380` were each reviewed by Claude Fable 5, but amending mid-chain needs an interactive rebase, unavailable in this environment — the closeout commit is this repo's designated fallback home for a trailer that could not land on its own commit. `c1f7550e` (spec) and `9bb80127` (plan) are deliberately exempt from that list: spec/plan commits whose content the owner approved in the producing flow carry no reviewer trailer by convention, so their absence is not the same debt.

## 2026-08-13 — venue truth lands: the instrument map, a frozen Cache snapshot, and the realized-state artifact (iter-138, spec/plan 00089, T0018 partial, T0130 + T0134 resolved)

- **What shipped, all of it read-only.** The second of [[T0018]]'s five-spec risk-first decomposition: the asset→`InstrumentId` map and the pure `size_order` constraint-sizing function (`cli/engine/instruments.py`); `VenueState` — a frozen dataclass snapshot of the Nautilus Cache (held positions, balances, per-instrument constraints, `snapshot_at`) plus `runtime_concordance`, the only new code touching Nautilus (`cli/engine/venuestate.py`); the `venue-<HH>.json` realized-state artifact beside `cycle-HH.json`, mirroring `execledger.py`'s shape and structurally invisible to the Stage-6a concordance glob the same way `exec-HH.json` is (`cli/engine/venueledger.py`); `run_cycle`'s new `venue_state` seam — journaled first, before any target computation, and never consulted for targets or orders; the strategy's boundary snapshot hook (`ShadowStrategy._snapshot_venue_state`, degrading to `None` on any read failure without costing the boundary); the repo-side basket-concordance test pinning the traded basket against the committed universe doc under a ruled-exceptions baseline owned by [[T0137]]; and four `zcrypto_venue_*` gauges, admitted through the capture Alloy keep-list and watched by two new alert rules (`zcrypto-venue-concordance-failed`, `zcrypto-venue-snapshot-stale`) in the same change. Nothing here places, sizes, or arms an order — `size_order` is pure and uncalled by any production path until `00090`.
- **The spec was falsified mid-build, and the correction is D5a.** Task 1's own probe, reused for Task 2, measured through a loopback HTTP server serving a canned Kraken `AssetPairs` response into the installed adapter's compiled Rust parse path (no packet left the host) that nautilus 1.230.0's Kraken adapter never maps Kraken's `costmin` onto `min_notional` — it reads `None` on every instrument, while `min_quantity`/`size_increment`/`price_increment` all populate correctly. A second measurement forced the mechanism: the engine host mounts only `/var/lib/zcrypto-engine` and the config file, no refdata snapshot, so a runtime file read was never available either. `costmin` therefore ships as a committed per-base constant (`COSTMIN_EUR`, all ten legs `0.45`), labelled `costmin_source: snapshot-constant` in the artifact so no reader mistakes it for a live venue read, and pinned against `load_minimums` over the newest refdata snapshot by `tests/test_costmin_drift.py` — the runtime concordance check covers only the three Cache-supplied constraints, since checking costmin there would fail all ten legs forever and hold the new alert red (the T0135 failure D2 exists to avoid).
- **The read-only claim is proven, not asserted.** Task 4's central pin — `cycle-HH.json` byte-identical with and without a `VenueState`, only `CycleResult.venue` (in-memory) differing — survived four adversarial mutation probes, all KILLED: filtering an order on costmin, netting positions into targets, a venue-presence leak that touched only the journaled bytes (proving the byte-identity clause independently of the targets/orders assertions), and moving the venue-record write to after target computation (proving the write-first ordering).
- **A defect no test could have caught.** Task 7's cold review found the `zcrypto_venue_snapshot_timestamp_seconds` gauge registered eagerly at `0.0` with no seed, so every engine converge — which lands deliberately inside the inter-cycle gap — would fire `zcrypto-venue-snapshot-stale` roughly six minutes post-restart and hold it red until the next boundary (up to 4h), with a summary claiming the writer had stopped when it had in fact just restarted. The gauge was correct; the operational behaviour was not. Fixed by seeding from the newest on-disk `status: ok` `venue-HH.json` at startup (`_seed_venue_state`), mirroring `_seed_cycle_state`'s precedent, guarded so a corrupt or absent artifact degrades rather than blocking engine start. Third occurrence of the same T0135 shape this session.
- **The T0134 sweep found a third instance the trigger never named.** Task 8 fixed T0134's own test plus its new sibling (`test_run_survives_an_unreadable_venue_record_at_metrics_seed_time`, born the same iteration with the identical order-dependence), then swept the defect class rather than stopping there and found `test_a_raising_startup_evaluation_never_prevents_the_engine_from_starting` — pre-existing, unrelated to this spec — failing standalone for the same reason. All three fixed in one commit by attaching `caplog`'s handler directly to the `zcrypto` logger for the duration of the assertion (mirroring the existing `test_archive_replay.py` fix) instead of relying on root-logger propagation `configure()` turns off; each proven non-tautological by mutation (silencing the guarded `logger.exception` call turns its test red).
- **T0130 resolved**: D8 measured the topic's own "packaging default" guess false — no release has ever been cut, so `0.0.0` was always the true installed version everywhere, and `importlib.metadata` reports it faithfully; a semver that has never moved cannot identify a build. CI's OCI revision label sits outside the container and is invisible to the process. Fixed by threading the build sha through as `GIT_REVISION`/`ZCRYPTO_BUILD_REVISION` (workflow build-arg → `ENV`) so `_code_version()` composes `<version>+<sha12>` in both `cycle-HH.json` and `venue-HH.json`. Acceptance is by value at the next converge, not at merge — recorded on the archived topic once the on-host reading matches the image's revision label.
- **T0134 resolved**: all three instances of the order-dependent `caplog` shape now pass standalone and in file order; the topic archives with the full account above.
- **Two loose ends, disposed rather than left in prose.** No dashboard panel exists yet for the `zcrypto_venue_*` family, so the two new alert rules carry no `__dashboardUid__`/`__panelId__` — registered as a ninth deferral on [[T0018]] rather than a standalone topic, since the family most naturally joins the Engine board alongside `00090`'s order/position/PnL panels rather than earning a one-off row. The plan's Task 1 Steps 5–6 describe `COSTMIN_EUR` and its drift test as landing in Task 1's commit; they actually landed in Task 2's (`82ef1419`), because the D5a ruling arrived mid-Task-2 and rewriting Task 1's already-Fable-reviewed commit to absorb them would have invalidated its review anchor — noted in place on the plan rather than restructured.
- **D5a's ruling reaches its operating surface in this change**, per `spec-plan-locations.md`: `tests/test_costmin_drift.py` is the committed constant's only guard and skips wherever `data/snapshots/` is absent (including CI, since the data root is gitignored), so the `zcrypto-refdata-sweep` skill now runs it immediately after minting a fresh snapshot — the one moment the guard can actually fire.
- **Review floor held throughout** (engine code on the trade-key host): every code/test commit reviewed by Claude Fable 5. Two commits carry a review this branch could not attach as their own trailer — `82ef1419` (Task 2's code commit, whose `Reviewed-by:` was lost when an unrelated trailer fix required a reset+recreate of that commit, tree hash verified identical before/after) and `b1909607` (Task 8's first of two commits) — recorded here per this repo's fallback-home convention for a trailer that cannot land on its own commit; interior commits in a chain cannot be amended without an interactive rebase, unavailable in this environment.
- **No dataset-catalog change is owed.** This iteration introduces, relocates and retires no dataset — the venue ledger is operational state under `data/engine-journal`, following the same uncatalogued treatment as the execution ledger it sits beside.

## 2026-08-15 — the twelve-leg pipeline: full-symbol keys, the /BTC legs at structural zero, and record 47 (iter-139, spec/plan 00094 + spec 00095, T0137 resolved, T0018 partial)

- **What shipped: the engine's whole pipeline re-keys from bases to full symbols, and the traded basket widens from ten legs to twelve.** `cli/engine/store.py::BASKET` becomes the single committed source of truth (twelve sorted symbols; `PAIR_KEYS` derived from it against the fetch map, symbol-keyed, failing loud at import if a member is missing) and `_store_path` splits the symbol so `/BTC` legs land under their own quote directory while the ten `/EUR` paths stay byte-identical. `INSTRUMENT_IDS` derives from `BASKET`; `COSTMIN_EUR` is deleted and replaced by `COSTMIN`, symbol-keyed with an explicit `(value, quote_currency)` pair in the refdata snapshot's own vocabulary (`"EUR"`/`"BTC"`, never the adapter aliases `ZEUR`/`XXBT`) — ten legs at `(0.45, "EUR")`, `ETH/BTC` and `SOL/BTC` at `(2e-05, "BTC")`; `fx_eur_notional` lands as a pure, production-uncalled EUR→BTC term (the `size_order` precedent). `VenueState`/`InstrumentConstraints` go symbol-keyed and gain `costmin_quote`, with `VENUE_SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 → 2 so a pre- and post-deploy venue record are distinguishable on inspection. The cycle journal moves to `SCHEMA_VERSION = 2` with `{1, 2}` loadable, the gate replay becomes schema-aware, the soak's parallel loaders take the same contraction, and the deployable is re-ratified as registry record 47. **None of it is deployed** — the code has landed and is green; the fleet still runs the ten-leg build.
- **The strategy did not change, and that is the branch's load-bearing claim (D1).** `CrossfreqSystemConfig.assets` stays the ten EUR bases and `cli/portfolio/` is byte-identical across the entire branch — verified as an empty `git diff` against the merge base, not asserted. The widening happens strictly downstream of the model: `_expand_to_basket` maps the model's ten base-keyed outputs onto the twelve-symbol basket and emits `ETH/BTC`/`SOL/BTC` at exactly `0.0`, raising on a model base with no `/EUR` leg. Widening the `assets` knob instead would have had the momentum/A1/A2/B sleeves compute *real* weights for two legs record 44's holdout evidence never covered — an unvalidated strategy change wearing plumbing's clothes. The sibling interface `select_model_inputs` contracts a twelve-symbol grid back to the ten `/EUR` legs re-keyed by base, on a calendar unioned over **those ten only**, so a `/BTC` stamp the EUR legs lack can never shift an EUR SMA/stdev window. One implementation of each, imported by the gate replay and the soak rather than copied.
- **The branch opened by overturning a documented claim, and the claim was corrected at its source rather than annotated.** [[T0137]]'s offline multi-quote solve survey measured, through the installed adapter's compiled parse path on a loopback server (no packet left the host), that `margin_balance_asset` has exactly one call site and selects only the currency the *account summary* is denominated in; under MARGIN the OpenPositions branch is quote-agnostic, so the single exec client already **sees** the XXBT-quoted `ETH/BTC` and `SOL/BTC`. It also constructed and tripped the constraint that a second exec client per venue is impossible (`ExecutionEngine.register_client` refuses it). The real obstacle was the engine's own base-keyed EUR plumbing — exactly what this spec removes. The wrong mechanism had already been inherited by `cli/engine/node.py`'s comment, spec `00089`'s context bullet and D9, and T0137's own *Why this matters*; all four were rewritten in place with the refutation stated, because the uncorrected story reads first and more confidently than a note beneath it, and this one was actively misdirecting the cost estimate for a live-trade-path decision.
- **Three owner rulings (2026-08-14) turned that survey into scheduled work**: the `/BTC` half is pursued during 6b ahead of phase 7; DOT stays traded; and the re-plumb and the deployable re-ratification are coupled in one iteration, with the rollback risk written down rather than left to be rediscovered. Options, tradeoffs and reasoning are in the `[iter-139]` entries of `docs/research/14.phase6-decisions.md`. The sequencing consequence is recorded on [[T0018]]: its spec table is **execution order, not serial order** — `00094` runs ahead of `00090` because the order path's state machine, sizing and fill ingestion all consume this symbol keying, and building that path twice on the live-trade path is the waste.
- **The cold spec+plan review returned REWORK — 3 blocking, 7 important, 7 minor, all folded before Task 1 (`434a522c`).** **B1**: park-then-adopt was unimplementable, because the registry hashes the verdict into the chained record, so no verdict can change after append — Task 7 prepares the record's fields without appending, and the owner's attended act is the *only* append. **B2**: the deploy was specced as one surface and is actually three (D8, below). **B3**: `_previous_success` crosses the schema boundary unrouted — the first v2 cycle against a v1 predecessor reads base keys, every `.get(symbol)` misses, and the engine writes a phantom from-flat rebalance into `orders.jsonl` and the exec ledger **silently**, since the gate never reads orders. `symbol_keyed_targets` normalizes the predecessor and a fixture holding this cycle's own book except BTC pins it: exactly one order is owed where a missed normalization emits seven.
- **The gate had to survive the deploy boundary by construction, and the design's own first draft would have destroyed it (D3).** Each schema replays **and compares in its own native key space**: a v1 record's journaled pairs already *are* the ten-asset model's base keys, so its grids go to the builder untouched; a v2 record goes through `select_model_inputs` + `_expand_to_basket`. Nothing is normalized across the two — the gate compares a record against its own replay, so lifting v1 replay output to symbol keys would have made **every** pre-deploy cycle a structural mismatch and zeroed the ratified 30-day streak at the turnover, the design defeating its own goal. Proven by a straddle test run through the **real** replay path over real v1 and v2 fixture records, because `evaluate_gate` is structurally blind to schema and hand-built outcomes would prove arithmetic that was never at risk. The same contraction exposed a worse defect in `feeders.replay_stages`: the builder refuses a twelve-symbol panel with `PortfolioError`, which is **not** an `EngineError`, so it escaped `decompose_report`'s per-record catch and the command's own — the first schema-2 record would have killed `engine decompose` and `engine accum-replay` outright instead of counting one named failed cycle.
- **The soak was treated as a first-class consumer, and that is where the quietest defect lived.** `build_null` casts its book onto the same twelve-symbol space: legs at exactly zero leave gross/net/turnover/hhi alone, but `structural_metrics` divides `active_frac` by `len(weights)`, so a ten-wide null scored against a twelve-wide realized series would have biased **every** `active_frac` verdict in one direction, silently. Alongside it: `_load_canonical` and `_assemble_latest_grids` both raised `PortfolioError` on the twelve-symbol panel; `_canonical_present` probed `BTC/EUR` alone, so a canonical tree missing the `/BTC` legs read "present" and then aborted on `FileNotFoundError`; and `realized_series`, which joins against the store's `<base>/<quote>` paths, now reads every record through `symbol_keyed_targets` and takes the window's asset union so a straddling window neither aborts on the changing asset set nor mis-paths a bare `"BTC"`.
- **Record 47 is the deployable, and its metrics are not "close to" record 44's — they are the same numbers.** Measured over `data/ohlc-full` through the real production surfaces (`read_store_series` → `select_model_inputs` → `build_crossfreq_system_fast` → `_expand_to_basket`) against `record44_legs.load_union`'s ten-asset path: grids bit-identical, **0 differing bars of 27,337** on `governed_net` and `ungoverned_net`, **0 differing rows of 273,380** on `final_targets`, max absolute difference exactly **`0.0`** — a zero, not a `1e-15`. Nine registered values reproduce bit for bit at full precision; `cap_breach_bars` 1318 and `governor_engaged_bars` 7302 are exact. Appended on the owner's attended adopt (2026-08-15): `trial_id` 47, `verdict: "adopt"`, variant `P1-crossfreq-fixedthird-twelve-leg-basket-btczero`, `spec_hash ea73740d…` pinning the new spec `00095`, chain re-validated (47 records load, adopts `[15, 16, 33, 34, 37, 38, 39, 43, 44, 47]`). The engine self-test's pin moves 44 → 47 in `cli/engine/soak.py` with its integers unchanged, so the pin proves the same property it always did. Record 47 inherits record 44's holdout position **exactly** — no new out-of-sample look was taken, and the zero-holdout banner moves with the pin. Disclosures live inside the record rather than in prose: `n_trials_in_family` must be 5 (the store refuses less), deflating DSR to `0.9999992100303082`; `data/ohlc-full` carries **zero** `/BTC`-only stamps on either grid, so this dataset cannot falsify the EUR-only calendar contraction — a constructed fixture in `tests/test_engine_cycle.py` proves it instead; and `spot_drag_pct_yr` is deliberately omitted rather than copied forward unmeasured.
- **Spec `00095` is record 47's construction doc and is immutable from the moment its sha256 landed in the record** — the successor to `00038`, which record 44 pins. Its criterion is identity, not a search: no tolerance band, every re-derived leg exactly equal across the widened and ten-asset paths.
- **The concordance baseline shrinks to one ruled exception (D7).** `RULED_SELECTED_BUT_UNREACHABLE` retires to a **named empty set** rather than being deleted — the assert still references it, it anchors the retirement citation, and it is the landing place for a future genuine divergence. `RULED_TRADED_BUT_DESELECTED` stays `{"DOT/EUR"}` but its meaning changes: it now encodes the owner's keep-traded **ruling**, not an open question. No revisit deferral dangles, because the test itself is the trigger — any refresh that changes selection, DOT's re-entry included, turns it red and forces a conscious baseline edit. Both directions mutation-probed and KILLED, with the actual assertion text read rather than the script's label trusted.
- **[[T0137]] resolves and archives** with both halves delivered — the `/BTC` widening (commits `f4824796` → `f8bd0f64`, spec `00094`) and the DOT keep-traded ruling, now carried by the concordance baseline and by record 47's own notes.
- **[[T0138]] was opened during this work** (reported for transparency, per the open-topics convention): D4 makes each instrument's cost floor carry its denomination (`costmin_quote`), so the unit is **knowable** — but nothing makes it **checked**. `size_order` takes `costmin` as a bare number and D4 assigns denomination-ownership to a caller that does not exist until `00090`. A BTC floor of `0.00002` compared against a EUR notional fails **open** — every order clears the bar, silently, on the live trade path. Measured twice by two independent reviewers: a hand-built `VenueState` lying about its quote passes `runtime_concordance` and is journaled verbatim. The right guard is a call-site assertion in `00090`, not a `__post_init__` validator, which would couple an evidence dataclass to a committed constant and reject the deliberately-wrong fixtures the concordance tests need.
- **The review floor changed mid-branch on the owner's instruction, and that is recorded rather than normalized.** Tasks 1–2 (`f4824796`, `2a672f0a`) were reviewed by Claude Fable 5 per `spec-plan-locations.md`'s live-trade-path floor; the owner said to stop using the Fable floor mid-Task-3, so Tasks 3–8 were reviewed by Claude Opus 5. That contradicts the standing rule text, which is **unamended** — flagged here so a later reader does not read the drift as license. Two commits carry no `Reviewed-by:` trailer: `efee3da9` (the Task-4 reviewer's own follow-up fix, so no third party read it) and `8c3c9be5` (the record-47 append, performed in the main loop at the owner's attended adopt because a hash-chained append is unfixable after the fact). Spec/plan commits are exempt by convention, their content having been approved in the producing flow.
- **DEPLOYED 2026-08-15/16, all four surfaces, verified by value at each** (this bullet was written "none of it has run" at closeout and is rewritten in place now that the rollout is real). Order held: NAS `5f890c26237a` 13:40:38Z → ops `419feafc304f` 13:58:23Z → the secondary capture canary 15:03:34Z → the primary's Alloy config → the engine `419feafc304f` at **16:31:29Z 08-16**, one converge carrying both the `00094` and `00089` payloads. The NAS gate returned **byte-identical** (`status 1 / streak_days 35 / mismatch_total 0`), so the ratified streak survived the schema bump; `ops-postverify.sh` ALL PASS; the bake passed all eight Phase-3 checks with `continuity.py` on a pulled copy reading 0 truncated hours over 468 stream-hours, a STRONG-form prune (`deleted=574`, then `580` nightly), and an **RSS residual discharged with no step at all** — sampled at 2 h resolution THROUGH the T+17–25 h anniversary band rather than at one point past it, the band being what T0136's first close got wrong.
