The engine then published `zcrypto_venue_instruments_expected` **12** with the exec envelope in its disarmed post-restart state. **The first schema-2 cycle then verified clean by value 20:01:43Z**: twelve symbol-keyed targets with both `/BTC` legs at exactly `0.0`; **no phantom rebalance** — the v1 predecessor's base keys normalized, so `orders.jsonl` holds a note and zero order rows where a failed normalization would have emitted three full-size buys from flat; the first venue record ever written reading 12 instruments / 12 positions / 0 concordance failures; and **the ratified gate streak ADVANCING through the boundary, 35 → 37**, having scored a genuinely mixed-schema day clean — cycles 00–16 at schema 1, cycle 20 at schema 2. That is D3's straddle case, and why each schema replays and compares in its NATIVE key space. Three surprises the deploy owed and no converge performs are recorded on \[\[T0139\]\], resolved. **The order below is why it had to run this way:** **(1) NAS and ops images converge FIRST.** The gate is *scored* on the NAS (`gate-export` under the NAS image pin, after every journal pull) and re-verified on ops (`verified-replay` under the ops pin); old code on either raises `unsupported schema_version 2`, classifies the day unclean, and **zeroes the ratified streak on the scoring surface with the engine deploy fully correct**. Converging them ahead is safe because every existing record is v1 and the widened code loads `{1, 2}`. **(2) The engine LAST**, under standard discipline (canary via the secondary's capture bake, the inter-cycle window, `fleet-pins.md` recorded first, attended converge) — **and inside the converge window, before the first boundary, stage the two `/BTC` canonical parquets to the host and run the store seed**: the engine host mounts no data root, and `refresh_store` over a missing file kills the first post-converge cycle outright. **(3) Stacking with `00089`'s still-owed engine payload** is an operational call at deploy time under `fleet-deploys.md` — one converge may carry both, or two may run. All of it, the staging and back-seeding steps and the by-value checks included, is registered as [[T0139]] so the deploy's preconditions are checkable from repo state rather than reconstructed from this paragraph.
- **Deploy verification is by value, and one of the checks is arithmetic people get wrong.** The first schema-2 `cycle-HH.json` must carry twelve symbol-keyed targets with both `/BTC` legs at exactly `0.0`; `orders.jsonl` must show **no phantom rebalance** — a full-book order set there is B3's failure mode and rolls back; `venue-HH.json` must read twelve instruments; on the NAS and ops replay surfaces `validation_failures` and `mismatches` must be **unchanged** with `replayed_ok` advancing. **The streak moves only at the deploy day's 20:30 UTC evaluation** — it counts complete days, so expecting +1 immediately after the first cycle is wrong arithmetic and would false-alarm; at 20:30 it reads pre-deploy + 1 or the deploy rolls back.
- **The final whole-branch review's two deployer findings, and where each landed.** `cli/engine/command.py`'s store-presence check globbed `*/EUR/*.parquet`, so it **passed on a store missing exactly the `/BTC` half the deploy must seed**: the node would have started looking healthy and died at the first boundary, where a `failed-cycle-<HH>.json` sidecar makes that boundary unretryable at any time. **Fixed in this closeout** — the guard now tests `BASKET` membership on both grids through `store.py`'s own `_store_path` and names the absent `<symbol>@<interval>` series, converting a lost boundary into a refused start. Proven by construction, not assertion: a store holding all ten `/EUR` legs and neither `/BTC` leg started the node cleanly before the change (exit 0, "shadow node starting") and aborts naming both `/BTC` legs after it, with the complete twelve-leg store still starting. The second finding is deploy-procedural, so it now has a registered home rather than a changelog sentence: a mixed-schema soak window needs the widened legs' store history seeded **back** before the flip, or every pre-boundary cycle reads `store`-bound — carried, together with the whole three-surface deploy requirement, by [[T0139]] (opened here, reported for transparency). Two cosmetic-but-confusing post-deploy readings, measured rather than predicted: at engine start the startup seed reads the last **pre**-deploy v1 record and publishes `zcrypto_venue_instruments_loaded 10` against `_expected 12` until the first post-deploy cycle (up to 4 h) — no page, since `00089` D6 excludes both from alerting, but whoever looks will triage it as a fault; and the `target_weight` gauge's `asset` label values re-key from `"BTC"` to `"BTC/EUR"`, so every series legend on the Engine board changes at the deploy (no Grafana query breaks — `engine-dashboard.json` filters only on host).
- **No dataset-catalog change is owed.** This iteration introduces, relocates and retires no dataset: the two `/BTC` canonical series already exist in `data/ohlc-full` and are catalogued, and the widened basket changes which of them the engine *reads*, not what any dataset *is*.
- **Two stale operating surfaces fixed in this closeout.** `.claude/skills/zcrypto-refdata-sweep/SKILL.md` still named the deleted `COSTMIN_EUR` as the drift guard's subject — a live runbook pointing at a dead symbol, corrected in a separate `claude`-kind commit. And `tests/test_engine_metrics.py`'s venue-record fixture called itself "matching `write_venue_record`'s schema" while hardcoding `"schema_version": 1` — false once `VENUE_SCHEMA_VERSION` went to 2, and uncaught precisely because that constant is write-only, nothing validating a venue record against it. The **docstring** was corrected rather than the value: every pre-deploy venue record is v1 and the startup seed must keep reading them, so a pinned v1 record is deliberate v1-reader coverage and worth keeping. Also unchanged and pre-existing: `engine decompose`/`accum-replay` still catch only `EngineError`, so any *other* `PortfolioError` still gives an unhandled traceback rather than one counted failed cycle.

## 2026-08-18 — the rung-1 order path: one chokepoint, a write-ahead row, and an engine that could now place a real order but is not armed to (iter-140, spec/plan 00090, T0018 partial, T0138 + T0140 resolved)

- **What shipped: the first code in this project that can submit a real order — landed DISARMED.** **[[T0018]]'s risk-first decomposition is five specs, `00088`→`00092`. `00090` is the THIRD of those five, and the fourth spec to LAND in execution order** — `00094`, the /BTC widening, was slotted ahead of it by the owner's 2026-08-14 sequencing ruling and is not one of the five. It is the first of them with monetary blast radius (~€10–30 per leg). `cli/engine/executor.py` (1,668 lines, with 2,505 lines of tests beside it — both re-measured at the branch's end, after the whole-branch review's fixes) is the **one** module through which an order can reach the venue: TTL- and dedup-bounded probe plans picked up from `<engine_state_dir>/exec/probe-plan.json` by a 5 s Nautilus timer, sized through `size_probe_order`, run one intent at a time through a maker-first ladder, with fills, fees, positions and realized PnL ingested into the exec ledger and into five new execution metric families (a sixth new family ships on the intent side, below). Around it: `cli/engine/probeplan.py` (the plan model and its refusal walls), exec-ledger schema 2 with write-ahead rows, `validate_venue_record`, the startup position seed, four automatic kill trips, `zcrypto engine probe-plan --check`, the Engine board's execution and venue rows, and the attended probe procedure as a runbook section. **Nothing is deployed. The config renders `exec_armed = false`, no arm file exists, no order has ever been submitted, and the probe window has not run** — arming is the checklist's separate, owner-worded act on probe day, deliberately not folded into the deploy converge so that a code defect and a live-armed engine never meet before a disarmed drill has run.
- **Two properties are structural rather than conventional, and both were proven by the defect they name.** The gate is **taken, never held**: `_submit` evaluates it itself, as its first act, and accepts no verdict parameter, so no caller can carry a permission token while the arm file, the kill file or the venue change underneath it. This deliberately **supersedes the candidate shape [[T0018]] had inherited by name** — a submitting client constructible only from a fresh verdict — because a verdict object, however fresh at construction, is still a value a caller can hold. And the exec-ledger row **precedes** the venue call: a failed write-ahead refuses the submission, inverting the `00088`-era swallow at exactly this one site, because a fill with no forensic row is the state this spec exists to make impossible. Both pinned twice over: a repo-wide text walk fails if any module outside `executor.py` so much as mentions `submit_order`/`cancel_order`/`order_factory`, and two mutation probes through `infra/scripts/mutate-probe.sh` were **KILLED** for the right reason — gate-unavoidability fired on `client.submitted == []` with a concrete order tuple in the diff, the chokepoint walk on `offenders == []` naming the planted module by path.
- **The plan's own probe-1 construction was unreachable, and the harness caught it rather than scoring a false pass.** Dropping the kill file before the intent starts refuses at intent start — before subscribe — so the submit path is never entered at all; `mutate-probe.sh` reported it as rc-5 "CONTROL did not fail" instead of SURVIVED. The substitute lands the kill file *after* the intent has started, in the window between subscribing under a FULL verdict and the quote arriving, which is the only path that reaches `_submit` and is a strictly stronger construction of the taken-never-held property.
- **The maker-first ladder, and the five rulings that shaped it mid-branch.** Join the touch post-only (never improving into the spread), reprice at most 5 times on either crossing surface, time-box at 15 minutes, then cross with at most 3 price-bounded limit IOCs — never a MARKET order. Every resubmission is sized to the intent's quantity minus what **all** of its orders have filled. The rulings, each recorded because the code would otherwise read as arbitrary: **unknown plan/intent keys are REFUSED** (a typo'd `levarage` was silently parsing as a *spot* intent, dropping the leverage the operator signed off, and skipping the margin floor); **an ambiguous submit drops the plan's REMAINDER, not just its own intent** (if an order may be live, position and free balance are unknown, so the cap and margin-floor arithmetic that authorized every later intent no longer holds — the remaining intents are journaled naming the ambiguous predecessor); **ambiguity gets ONE self-describing state**, `ambiguous` added to `_OPEN_ORDER_STATES` and written by both the transport path and the unclassifiable-rejection path, on the invariant that the open set must contain every state a possibly-live order can wear; **a rejection during the time-box cancel proceeds to the fallback, while the same rejection during a revoke does not** (a revoke declares the book untradeable, a time-box declares the opposite — both directions constructed); and **the cancel-parking gap is closed in both halves** — `OrderCancelRejected` handled, plus a cancel-ack deadline and an IOC-phase watchdog — because a parked intent leaves the plan pointer non-None and the executor then silently ignores every future plan until restart, a dead engine that looks alive.
- **`reduce_only` is enforced by classification against the LEDGER, never against the adopted order's own flags.** Whether Kraken's OpenOrders echo survives adoption with a truthful `is_reduce_only` is unverifiable in the installed source (the population happens in the opaque Rust layer), so the write-ahead row is the only trusted witness: an adopted order matching a non-terminal `submitted` row whose ledgered order was a reducer is left resting, its row preserved **and re-attached**; everything else is canceled, including an order whose adopted flag reads `True`. **What re-attaching does NOT buy is the post-restart fill, and the whole-branch review corrected that claim rather than the code** — a reconciled venue order is assigned `StrategyId("EXTERNAL")` and its events publish on `events.order.<strategy_id>`, a topic this strategy is not subscribed to, so such a fill reaches no handler here and is reconciled only as venue truth. Claiming the instruments would deliver those fills and simultaneously route the owner's sanctioned hand settle into the fill handler, un-scoping the unknown-order trip the bullet below exists to keep scoped — not a trade worth making at merge time, so the docstring, the runbook's arm step and the test that pins the appender now all say what is true, and the routing question is registered as \[[T0142]\]. Review then hardened three things in that pass: every **matched** row is attached *before* any cancel goes out (a cancel is a request, not an outcome — the order can still fill between the two, and attaching only the kept orders dropped that fill); the once-only flag is set only after a **successful** open-orders read, so a raising read retries next tick instead of leaving every previous-process order unclassified for the life of the process; and **at gate level NONE the pass cancels everything, ledger-attached reducers included** — a kill file that survived the restart is precisely the state the operator pulled the switch for, and "nothing is working at the venue" must not have a restart-shaped hole.
- **The first four automatic kill trips ship, where `00088` could not build them.** A fill for an order the ledger has no row for; an order filling past its submitted quantity; an intent's orders filling past its target between them (the backstop to the ladder's remainder sizing); and, at every intent terminal, the Cache position against what the intent's own fills account for. Each is seen creating the kill file and cancelling resting orders — the trip is constructed, not asserted into existence — and an autouse guard proves every other test in the file leaves the switch alone. Review then found the fail-closed mechanism had a conditional hole: **nothing consulted `_kill_tripped` outside the trip itself**, so a kill file that could not be *written* left the process able to pick up, journal and submit the next plan while the log claimed it had stopped. The flag is now an in-process refusal at both places an order can begin, tripping-direction only, and the write-failure log line says what is actually true — this process refuses everything from here, and nothing on disk will stop the next one.
- **The critical dependency the whole trip design rests on was verified against the installed library, not against our own wiring.** The unknown-order trip is strategy-scoped so that the owner's sanctioned hand-placed settle — an account-external fill on no order of ours — structurally cannot latch the kill switch on the probe's own final act. The reviewer independently reproduced all four legs from the installed nautilus source: `Strategy.register` subscribes exactly `events.order.<own id>`; an unclaimed external order becomes `StrategyId("EXTERNAL")` on a topic `ShadowStrategy` is not subscribed to; no repo path can populate a claim list; and the wiring passes no `StrategyConfig`, so the claim list stays empty. Pinned three ways (bare strategy, wired strategy, assembled node) plus a text walk for the two names that could widen the stream. A **separate** accepted risk is recorded rather than coded around: a mid-probe settle placed *while an intent is in flight on the same instrument* can still trip the position-reconciliation trip; scoping that in code would mute the kill switch exactly when positions move, so the checklist's ordering is the control, and the runbook step names the consequence rather than merely forbidding the act.
- **A metric label would have read `1` instead of `maker`, and the same idiom would have gone into the forensic ledger row.** `LiquiditySide` is an `IntFlag` over `ReprEnum` in the pinned nautilus-trader, so `str(LiquiditySide.MAKER)` is `'1'`. Live fills would have created `zcrypto_exec_fills_total{liquidity="1"}` while the pre-registered maker/taker children read zero for the whole window — a board reporting nothing traded while money moved, on precisely the maker-vs-taker split the maker-first ladder exists to measure — and `'1'`/`'2'` would have been written into the fill row too (pre-existing from the ladder commit, fixed here rather than deferred). Every test was blind to it because the fill fake carried plain strings; it now carries the real enum member. **Then the fallback turned out to be worse than the bug**: `liquidity_side_to_str` does not merely raise on an out-of-range value — on `3` it hard-aborts the process with a Rust capacity-overflow panic (SIGABRT, exit 134) that no `except BaseException` can catch, and `3` is constructible as `MAKER | TAKER` because IntFlag keeps out-of-range composites under its KEEP boundary. Replaced with a membership test derived from the enum itself, verified total over every input class; the pin *returning at all* is half its assertion, since against the previous code it took the whole pytest process down.
- **The dashboard row was charted, never suppressed — and nine panels nearly shipped rendering EUR as minutes.** `tests/test_dashboards_cover_metrics.py` was deliberately left RED across the metrics-task boundary rather than muted, and closed by charting: an execution row (orders by outcome, fills by liquidity, fees, per-symbol position, realized PnL, gate level) plus the venue row `00089`'s four families had been waiting for, with the two venue rules gaining `__dashboardUid__`/`__panelId__` and restoring iter-127's a-firing-alert-points-at-its-panel invariant. The review caught that panel 14's `fieldConfig` had been copied **wholesale** onto 8 of 9 new panels, seconds unit and 16500 cycle-cadence threshold included — Grafana auto-scales seconds, so a €78 realized PnL rendered as "1.30 min" on a board that watches real money — and that panel 71's threshold line sat at the cycle-cadence bar while its own alert fires at 18000, with its description claiming the line *was* that alert's bar. Units and thresholds are now per panel. The Engine board's head text was rewritten in the same change: its "no position, no fill anywhere in this fleet's telemetry" and "`data/engine-journal` is the sole source of truth for realized state" claims become false the moment these families ship, so it is now an intent-vs-execution legend — carrying the one asymmetry that matters, that **positions are seeded across an engine restart and realized PnL is not**, so a flat zero after a restart is a gap in the record rather than a claim that nothing happened.
- **26 new active series, measured off the built registry rather than estimated** (8 order outcomes including `ambiguous` + 3 liquidity children + fees + 12 position + PnL + the limit-bound counter). The spec's estimate of ~25 was wrong on its own premise before `ambiguous` was counted, and 26 is the count after `no_liquidity_side` was pre-registered as a real child — an unattributed fill is still a fill, and counting it as taker would lie about exactly the split being measured. Admission moved in the same change, both directions of the T0051 trap: the capture keep-regex, the Alloy required-list, and six reasoned `NOT_A_FAULT_SIGNAL` entries. Against the 819 last measured fleet-wide that is ~845, inside spec `00043`'s <1k target — **a live re-measure is owed at the deploy**, since 819 is a recorded number, not one this branch could measure without network.
- **`ambiguous` is not foldable into `refused`, and the label set is derived rather than hand-listed.** "Refused" asserts that no order exists; after a submission whose venue outcome was never established, that claim is unavailable — so labelling it `refused` would put back into the metric the lie the ambiguity ruling removed from the ledger. A test derives the outcome label set from the executor's own call sites. Fees are never summed across currencies: a commission that is not EUR/ZEUR counts its fill and not its money, and the same rule governs the realized-PnL sum, whose `Money | None` is skipped rather than coerced. **The whole-branch review then found the counter's one real accounting hole**: `_on_cancel_ack` wrote row state `venue_canceled` in both of its unrequested-cancel arms but incremented `zcrypto_exec_orders_total{outcome="venue_canceled"}` in only one — the resting-phase crossing surface — so an IOC's returned unfilled remainder advanced `submitted` with no terminal outcome behind it, and through an unfilled fallback ladder the outcomes stopped summing to the submissions on the one surface an operator watches live. The derived-label test passes either way, so the pin is a test that drives the whole ladder — a time-box cancel plus three returned IOC remainders — and reads the counter by value: three `venue_canceled`, four `submitted`, one terminal outcome per submission. The row write and the increment now sit once *above* the branch rather than once per arm, so the two cannot drift apart again.
- **[[T0121]]'s counter sub-item ships on the intent side of the same pass** — `_limits_bound` in `cli/engine/cycle.py` recomputes the §10 whole-book stack over the forming row exactly as `replay_stages` does, feeding `zcrypto_engine_limit_bound_total` through `_CycleGauges`. Intent-prefixed on purpose: the limits bind on the intent book this cycle just built, not on anything the executor did with it. `limit_bound` is `bool | None`, and the `None` is load-bearing — the §10 stack validates its input and raises on anything non-finite where the sleeve-gross sum beside it does not, so a raising recomputation answers **"no answer", never a `False` that would read as a quiet book**, with the cycle still succeeding and its record on disk. The topic stays `partial`: its decompose-table sub-item remains open.
- **The probe checklist landed as an operating surface, and its review caught the step that would have erased the evidence.** `infra/runbooks/README.md` gains `engine-probe-window`: five phases (pre-probe, arm, drill before money, execute, disarm) with human gates G1–G6, every command's flag, path and expected output verified against the code that provides it. The restore-check step said "delete whatever you find" after naming only the arm file and the plan file — but a state-dir restore can also resurrect the **kill file**, which no code path anywhere clears, which a human engaged, and which carries the timestamp and reason that tripped it. Read literally, the step had an operator erasing the latch and its forensic text uninvestigated at exactly the moment it was trying to tell them something. It now acts per file by name: delete `armed` and `probe-plan.json` and only those two, treat a restored `kill` as a finding to work through its own section, leave `restart-hold`. Two further corrections in the same round: the settle step said settling mid-flight "will" create the kill file, which over-claims (the trip compares only the in-flight intent's own instrument, and live propagation of a hand-placed settle is unproven on the installed adapter) — it now says CAN, names why the outcome is unpredictable, and puts the deterrent on the preconditions where it belongs; and two structures the source documents left implicit are written as the code behaves — `exec_armed` is hardcoded in the role template with **no `-e` override**, so arming is a reviewed one-line diff plus a converge, and a plan must be **renamed** into place, never written in place, because a partially-written file is parsed, refused and deleted.
- **The verification sweep's first run was a false all-clear, and catching it is the finding.** zsh does not word-split an unquoted variable, and a stderr redirect hid grep's own error, so the sweep returned clean without having looked — caught by validating the filter before trusting its zero, per the standing rule that an empty filtered query is not an absent event. The re-run then surfaced a real hole in a **shared** guard: `tests/test_internal_terms_not_operator_visible.py`'s `\bD\d{1,2}\b` can never match `D5a`, because there is no word boundary between a digit and a following letter — so every lettered decision number escaped the operator-surface guard entirely, and the lettered forms are the most-cited decisions the specs have (`D5a` ×35, `D6a` ×21, `D1c` ×16, plus five more). Ruled fixed NOW as its own typed commit rather than registered, since the branch was open and the sweep already predicted green, and proven 2×2: with `D5a` planted in a Prometheus HELP string the old pattern passed 226 and the widened one failed naming file, line and token; plant removed, 226 again — so the widening admits no false positive on any scanned surface.
- **Two dispositions recorded as decisions rather than left as prose.** The four near-duplicate venue-record scans are an **accepted DROP, not a deferral** — zero behavioral gain, and consolidating mid-branch would have widened a safety-critical diff; a future task that touches those scans may consolidate then. And a margin closer bypasses both notional caps by construction (it is sized from `abs(held)`, so it is position-bounded and exposure-reducing) — stated here so a later reader does not read it as a missing check.
- **Three of the branch's claims were confirmed read-only against the live disarmed engine before merge**, which is the cheapest half of the probe checklist and needed no arming: the gate read returned `level=none` with reasons exactly `config_not_armed,arm_file_absent,restart_hold` — the three-reason resting state, confirming both the expected string and that the containerised CLI invocation composes on the host; the newest execution record read schema 1 with **zero** submitted rows, so nothing has ever been submitted; and the newest venue record read schema 2, twelve instruments, all twelve positions flat, concordance clean, balances `{'EUR': 99.84}`. That last value settles something the spec had marked unprovable without a host: the free-cash key is spelled **`EUR`, not `ZEUR`**, so the sizing path's ZEUR-then-EUR fallback resolves on its second arm in production, and the planned probe's margin floor — `(30/2 + 30/2) × 2.5 = 75 EUR` — clears the live balance with room. Nothing was armed, written, or converged to obtain any of it.
- **The suite is green at 3668 passed, 0 skipped, 450 s**, in data-dependent mode (`data/ohlc-full` present, so both gated regressions ran), with the full pre-commit gate clean (17 hooks) — re-measured at the branch's END rather than left at the 3666 the mid-branch closeout recorded, because two review rounds landed a test each beside it afterwards. The "0 skipped" proved more than it was asked to: roughly 20 environment-gated skip sites all had their preconditions met. Every one of the thirteen implementation tasks was reviewed by a different agent than its implementer, at the Fable floor `spec-plan-locations.md` sets for the live trade path — implementers Claude Sonnet 5 (the ledger, venue validation, the plan model, the sizing seam, the dashboards) and Claude Opus 5 (the chokepoint, the ladder, reconciliation, the trips, the wiring, the metrics, the runbook, the verification); primary reviewer Claude Fable 5 on all thirteen, with fix rounds re-reviewed by Claude Fable 5 or Claude Sonnet 5. **Those `Reviewed-by:` trailers are attached to their commits**: a branch-wide trailer pass ran at the branch's end, so **every code, test and docs commit on the branch — this closeout among them — carries reviewer attribution naming an agent other than that commit's implementer**, at the Fable floor this path requires (Claude Fable 5 on the large majority, Claude Sonnet 5 on the follow-up fix/test rounds; on a handful the reviewing agent was a *different agent of the same model* as the implementer, which the trailer pair alone cannot show). **What carries no `Reviewed-by:` is a CLASS, not a list**: `commit-messages.md`'s standing exemptions — the spec, plan and closeout-docs commits whose content the owner approved in the producing flow — plus the owner-run whole-branch review round's own commits, whose trailers the owner attaches directly rather than through a reviewing agent. Named as a class deliberately, and for the same reason no commit count appears here: any enumeration is falsified by the next commit that lands beside it. That is not hypothetical — it has now happened twice to this one sentence, first as a mid-branch wording claiming the trailer pass was still owed, then as a list of three spec/plan SHAs that stopped being the whole set the moment the pre-merge confirmation commit landed.
- **[[T0138]] and [[T0140]] resolve and archive** — the costmin denomination guard landed at the comparison site with the mismatched-denomination defect constructed and seen to trip it (and its fail-open direction pinned separately), and the venue record's write-only schema stamp became a schema-aware validator that refuses rather than normalizes, with the exec ledger given the identical treatment. **[[T0120]] is consciously re-deferred to `00092`**, not left unexamined: its trigger arguably fired at this spec, but `00090`'s reconciliation is post-terminal and startup-time rather than a standing poll loop, so it defines no continuous call rate to divide a REST budget against, and an interval set here would be a number chosen against an absent denominator.
- **[[T0141]] was opened in this closeout** (reported for transparency, per the open-topics convention): `infra/runbooks/README.md` now holds **19 procedure sections across 743 lines** against its own stated ~12 threshold, this iteration's five-phase probe procedure being the largest single addition. Not split here deliberately — it is a separate nameable component, and the 21 `<a name=…>` anchors must stay byte-identical because 17 alert summaries cite them as a paged responder's only next step, so folding a file migration into a safety-critical checklist commit would mix a procedure a reviewer must actually read with a mechanical move. `tests/test_infra_alert_rules.py` hardcodes the single-file path and must be widened first. **[[T0142]] was opened by the whole-branch review** for the adopted-order routing question above — whether to claim the basket instruments and gain post-restart fill observability at the cost of routing the owner's sanctioned settle into the unknown-order trip. Its trigger is the engine's own config: while `exec_armed` renders `false` by default the probe is attended and a mid-window restart is an abort, so the gap costs a present operator one venue-truth read; the moment continuous arming lands, a restart is routine and an unaccounted fill becomes a permanent hole in the record the reconciliation trip is measured against.
- **No dataset-catalog change is owed.** This iteration introduces, relocates and retires no dataset — the execution and venue ledgers are operational state under `data/engine-journal`, following the same uncatalogued, decisions-log-recorded treatment as the journal they sit beside.

## 2026-08-18 — the runbook splits by subsystem, and its README becomes a pure index (T0141 resolved)

- **19 procedure sections across 743 lines, against the ~12 the file's own Scope section allows.** The seam is **subsystem**, which is what that rule prescribes: `capture.md` (3), `zaccess.md` (4), `ops.md` (4), `engine.md` (7), `reference-data.md` (1). The open question the topic named — where `engine-probe-window` and `refdata-sweep-due` land under a subsystem seam — was answered by following the seam rather than inventing a second one: the probe window is engine material and sits last in `engine.md`, where the two exec-alert sections referencing it still read "below"; the reference-data sweep gets its own file, whose entry paragraph says a reminder came due rather than that an alert fired. **The README keeps the Scope and holds no procedure sections at all** — an index that also holds the leftovers has no rule against gaining one more, which is exactly how one file reached nineteen; a pure index gives every future section an obvious home or makes it mint a new subsystem file.
- **The guard was widened and proven to bite before a single section moved.** `tests/test_infra_alert_rules.py` had hardcoded `RUNBOOK = infra/runbooks/README.md` and a path-literal link regex, so it was both the split's safety net and its blocker — it would have gone green resolving citations against a file the sections had left. It now globs every `*.md` under `infra/runbooks/` and captures **both halves** of a citation, because a link resolves against the file it names, plus a second assertion pinning each anchor to exactly one file (copying a section without deleting the original leaves every citation resolving). Proven through `infra/scripts/mutate-probe.sh` on a committed tree, reading **which** assertion fired each time: a mutated anchor and a right-anchor-wrong-file citation both fired the resolve test with different diagnostics, and a duplicated anchor fired the uniqueness test — baseline green unmutated in every run.
- **Byte-identity proven by set equality, not by eye.** The 19 chunks between thematic breaks and the 20 anchors are identical pre- and post-split, concatenated section bodies hashing to `6a4f4c88…65e0bcbe` on both sides, with relative order preserved inside each file so every "the sibling procedure above" still points where it says. The anchor count is **20, not the 21** the topic and the previous entry recorded: the 21st `grep` hit was the Scope section's own prose mention of `<a name=…>`, and one section carries two anchors rather than two sections carrying two each.
- **25 anchor citations updated in the same change** — 17 alert summaries, 3 engine-board panel descriptions, the clamp comment in `cli/archive/settle.py`, and 4 across three topic files (`T0103` ×2, `T0142`, and the archived `T0105`). A repo-wide sweep confirms all 25 resolve to the file that defines their anchor; separately re-routed, as bare paths carrying no anchor: the root README's `probe-plan` Usage row, `T0018`, `T0049` ×4 and the index's `T0049` bullet. The dashboard reference regex and mdformat's allowlist stop naming `README.md` and now cover the directory. Four citations inside executed plans are deliberately left as point-in-time records of the alert YAML as authored then.
- **The index's own rows were the last unwatched citation surface.** They link sideways — `](capture.md#anchor)`, relative, no `infra/runbooks/` prefix — so the path-aware pattern structurally cannot see them, leaving the README both the one surface no guard read and the surface that matters most: a summary's path resolves to the index, and the row is the responder's next tap. Closed for the same reason the dashboard gap was, and pinned in both directions, since they fail differently — a row naming a file that does not define the anchor, and a section no row routes to at all. Two further mutate-probe rounds, again read by which assertion fired; the first attempt's control did **not** bite and that was the harness working, not the guard failing — the probe filtered on `-k runbook` and the new test's name carries no such token, so it was deselected rather than passing.
- **The dashboard citations gained a guard they never had.** Panel descriptions cite sections exactly as summaries do and are read at the same moment — a responder who followed the notification's panel link is already on the board — but nothing checked them, since the summaries' test reads `alerts.yaml` and stops. Found while updating them and closed in the same change rather than parked, with its own mutate-probe round; the anchor half of the link pattern stopped accepting `.`, which a description's closing full stop was otherwise swallowing.
- **One action is left open at merge, and it is the branch's only one: `infra/scripts/grafana-push.sh` must run once, after the merge, to re-ship the summaries and the Engine board.** Registered in the archived topic's `## Resolution` rather than left in a report. Nothing is degraded before the merge — a summary carries a repo *path*, not a URL, and it resolves against `develop`, where the README still holds every section — and inside the merge→push window the link is **degraded, not dead**: the file exists, the responder lands at the top of the pure index, and every section is listed there by its alert `uid` with a one-tap link, which is one extra hop rather than the fragment-with-no-next-step this guard exists to prevent. Annotation-only: no rule added or removed, so no prune, no bake, no converge.

- Round 4 of the guidance-corpus refinement (branch `claude/refine-rules-round4`, stacked on five cherry-picked claude-kind commits from the 00090 deploy-record branches). Harvest: 22 candidates (4 session lessons, 17 from a repo scout over PRs #279–#303 + the window's history entries, 1 standing); **17 graduated jointly** — 8 into `agent-ops.md`, 3 into protected `fleet-deploys.md` (per-edit sign-off), 2 into `docs-style.md`, 1 each into `spec-plan-locations.md`, the `topic-ops` and `iteration-closeout` skills, and `grafana-push.sh`'s header; 5 stay in memory (niche or single-surface).
- Staleness sweep: 10 findings. The `zcrypto-rollout-image` and `zcrypto-bump-alloy` skills re-trued from `run.sh` to `converge.sh` (six `timeout 600`-wrapped real converges de-wrapped — the orphaned-child hazard, on the live primary included); the stale six-check count retired on two surfaces — the period records deliberately keeping "six checks" are `docs/plans/00085-quote-aware-notional-ladder.md` and this file's earlier six-check entry; `iteration-closeout`'s phase-serial routing corrected (a phase's decisions log has ONE serial; deriving it from the phase's other docs was false for Phases 4 and 5); Grafana notification templates joined `operator-facing-text.md`'s in-scope list; CLAUDE.md's commit-gate sentence admits `ansible-lint`.
- The `WP<N>` ban is mechanized: `test_wp_tokens_stay_out_of_git_tracked_files` walks `git ls-files` with a two-way allowlist (a carrier that stops matching is a stale entry, never a silent pass); both trip directions constructed and seen to fire; the three unrecorded carriers rephrased on the user's word, leaving exactly the recorded exception.
- Cold diff review at the Fable floor raised 1 Important + 4 Minor, all closed: the NAS fingerprint bullet's invented `journal.py` scoping corrected to the transitive `cli.*` replay closure `gate_cache.py` digests (627 s re-framed as the smaller-journal cold figure); the ops-Alloy condense got its blast radius back; the committed-pin claim scoped to the capture image.
- Net always-loaded delta: 46,772 → 51,030 bytes (+9.1 %), user-approved; modal-language floor 90 → 98 with no per-file decrease. Memory inbox after the round: 5 items.

## 2026-08-20 — the rung-1 order path reaches the fleet, and fleet-pins becomes a state file (deploy of iter-140)

- **The deploy is complete and every leg was verified by outcome.** Three converge legs, in order: secondary capture 08-18 14:07:41Z → 31 h bake → engine 08-19 21:27:23Z → primary capture 08-20 05:21:51Z, all three tiers now on `636012cc00d9` (spec `00090`, revision `732598ff`, AVX). The bake gate passed, and all eight of the pre-re-pin checks were read and quoted before the primary followed, including `continuity.py` on a PULLED copy at 0 truncated / 0 missing / 0.0006 % over 44 h × 12 streams and a prune in the STRONG form (`deleted=528`). **The engine is verified DISARMED** — `exec_armed` renders false, no arm file, gate reasons exactly `config_not_armed,arm_file_absent,restart_hold`; arming remains the probe checklist's separate owner-worded act.
- **The series re-measure iter-140 declared owed at the deploy is discharged: 884 active fleet-wide**, against spec `00043`'s <1k target and the 819 recorded at the iter-127 rollout closeout (2026-08-05/06) — ops 345, zcrypto 219, nas 144, zcrypto-red 135, zaccess 39, plus 2 label-only strays. **The ~845 prediction was built on a stale premise, not a miscount**: it added iter-140's own 26 to a five-week-old baseline while four other deploys landed in between (the `00088` envelope 08-12, the twelve-leg widening 08-15, `00087` tape-bars on ops, `00089` venue truth 08-16), so the +39 over that figure is spread across hosts the prediction never modelled — measured deltas ops +15, zcrypto +44, zcrypto-red +6. Live `zcrypto_exec_*` cardinality is 31, which reconciles exactly against the registry rather than exceeding it: iter-140 contributed 25 exec-prefixed series (its 26th, `zcrypto_engine_limit_bound_total`, is engine-prefixed) and `00088`'s six envelope gauges account for the rest. Inside target, with the headroom now ~12 %.
- **A single-host capture re-pin costs ~zero data — the row that said otherwise was wrong twice before it was right.** It first estimated "~1 min" (conflating the ansible task window with container downtime), then "4.5 s lost" — but a per-host gap is not loss: the measured 4.3–4.8 s per stream was covered continuously by the other host, and a healed hour books only whatever its splice leaves UNFILLED — here, nothing. (Both hosts dark in one window is the state that books outright, and it did not occur.) Confirmed by the ledger rather than assumed: across the reconciler run that first covered the primary's restart hour, the residual and healable gap counters were byte-identical to their pre-window values. The real constraint is never restarting **both** capture hosts close together.
- **The canary rule now says what a bake is for.** A passed gate completes a rollout only when the primary follows — digest asymmetry between the two capture hosts is an in-flight state of an OPEN rollout, never a resting one, and a hold needs a recorded reason **and** a bound. The bake qualifies the IMAGE as a runtime (deps, base layers, entrypoint, memory, shipping) always, and the capture code when it changed; an engine payload's own proof is its next disarmed boundary cycles, never the bake. Both landed after the owner challenged the previous framing, which had treated the asymmetry as normal and left the engine-only case looking pointless.
- **`docs/reference/fleet-pins.md` is a STATE file, not a changelog** — 46,977 bytes of accumulated proof narrative (which had grown an internal contradiction: one row said the capture asymmetry persisted while its neighbour said it was closed) down to 6,671 at this branch's tip. Every row is re-trued at each re-pin; each converge's evidence — checks read, values quoted, bake form — now lives in that update's **commit message**, so `git log --follow` on the file is the deploy chronicle. The file's operating lessons were harvested into the rollout skill first (FULL-hours gate arithmetic with a worked example, the RSS residual read as a curve through anniversary bands, and Phase 5 inverted to put evidence in the commit message). The Ansible substring contract every role's pins assert depends on was verified against the result before it landed.
- Smaller corrections travel with it: the `/BTC` store legs' REST-only provenance is recorded in `data-catalog-full.md` (canonical is frozen at 2026-03-31, so a re-seed reproduces the hole, and `zcrypto engine seed` is workstation-only), and `infra/grafana/alerts.yaml` now names a deliberate blind spot beside the verify-replay rules — no rule watches `ops_verify_replay_reused`, so a busted checkpoint silently reverts every sweep to a ~8 h full rescan while all three rules read healthy.

## 2026-08-20 — iter-141: the reconciler says WHY the fleet went dark, without changing what it books ([[T0143]] resolved, spec `00096`)

- **The problem, twice in the ledger.** `zcrypto_reconcile_residual_gap_seconds_total` books permanent, unrecoverable L2 loss when neither capture host recorded anything for a window, and it cannot tell "we failed to record what the venue sent" from "the venue sent nothing." Two venue outages — 2026-08-06 (10,588.382751 s) and 2026-08-20 (6,251.349974 s) — are booked identically to 2026-07-13, which was a real capture defect. The counter drives the highest-severity rule in the system and is monotonic, so a venue outage leaves an indelible "permanent loss" record and trains the operator to discount the one alert that must never be discounted.
- **The booking is ruled UNCHANGED, and that is the spec's first decision rather than a limitation.** `residual_gap` books the ABSENCE of data, never fault attribution. The verdict rides beside it as a parallel view and is never subtracted. Proven by construction, not asserted: with the classifier stubbed to return absurd values the booked seconds are byte-identical, and identical again when the same fixture runs through the pre-change tree.
- **The discriminator is cross-host agreement, and it is sound rather than coincidental** — `cli/capture/command.py` stores Kraken's own payload timestamp, never local receipt time, so two independent hosts receiving the same message record byte-identical values by construction and a host that was not receiving cannot manufacture one.
- **What the verdict actually weighs, stated honestly.** Interior evidence is BRACKET evidence: it proves both hosts were receiving *at those instants*, never anything about the dark windows themselves. The verdict rests on the interior being brief, which makes a synchronised two-host failure-and-recovery implausible. That framing arrived only after three successive review rounds each constructed a different false `venue_silent`; the rule was then re-derived rather than patched a fourth time — exactly two windows, an interior `update` required, relative dominance, and a measured 300 s absolute bound with the real event 3.0× inside it.
- **Recorded durably, not just logged**: the `both_streams_silent` record gains `verdict` plus its evidence, and the exporter gains `zcrypto_reconcile_dark_episode_seconds_total{verdict=...}` whose three labels partition the booked seconds so the metric checks itself. Records written before the discriminator existed count `undetermined` — the counter never retroactively claims knowledge the system did not have, and the series is therefore non-zero from its first scrape rather than tripping the registered-at-zero staleness trap.
- **The system's only `severity: critical` reconcile rule finally has a runbook.** `infra/runbooks/ops.md` had no section for `zcrypto-reconcile-residual-gap` at all. It has one now, with the verdict as a numbered triage step — and the alert summary shrank from 1,074 characters to 612 once the runbook existed to carry the detail.
- **Verified against the whole live archive before merge, not against fixtures alone**: every `both_streams_silent` record replayed from the read-only NAS mount reproduces its booked seconds exactly, the recomputed windows match the ledgered windows on every hour, 2026-08-20 reads `venue_silent` (12/12 pairs agreeing across a 98.497572 s interior carrying 90 updates), and 2026-07-13 reads `undetermined` — the discriminator refuses to excuse the one historical event that genuinely was ours.
- **Two halves, one registered.** Venue status turned out to be complementary rather than redundant: it caught 08-06 and missed 08-20, cross-host does the reverse. The uncovered half — nothing writes Kraken's status messages into the archive, and the public endpoint reports current state only — is [[T0144]], whose trigger deliberately rides the residual-gap page via the runbook step, so it is evaluated by someone already reading rather than someone who must remember to poll.
- Every spec/plan commit on this branch was cold-reviewed at the Fable floor before execution and again as a whole branch; the reviewer attributions for the implementation commits are carried here rather than on their own commits (`commit-messages.md`'s sanctioned fallback), since amending them would mean a second history rewrite on a branch that already survived one.

## 2026-08-20 — the dark-episode verdict reaches the fleet, and two apt pins are retired (deploy of iter-141)

- **The verdict is live on `zcrypto-ops`.** Converge to `6b4c13899653` at 18:33:37Z, the owed liquidations roll performed as its own act at 18:35:26Z and verified from the container, Grafana push reporting no orphaned rules, `ops-postverify.sh` all-pass. Verified BY VALUE at the next tick rather than by exit code: `zcrypto_reconcile_dark_episode_seconds_total` reads `venue_silent` 0.0, `capture_divergent` 0.0, `undetermined` 21887.369457 against `residual_gap` 21887.369457 **unchanged** — the partition is exact, and the historical records count `undetermined` exactly as designed, since `_decided` never re-decides them.
- **The residual-gap page is firing, and it is the venue event, not the deploy** — `activeAt` 09:27:00Z, nine hours before the converge, against `increase(residual[24h])` = 6255.69 matching the 6251.35 s booked that morning. It self-resolves ~24 h after that booking. Recorded because the first live firing of a rule after its triage line changes is exactly when someone mistakes correlation for cause.
- **A guard that had never fired, found by converging by hand.** The ops role's pins assert probed `docker inspect … liquidations-poll` — the entrypoint COMMAND — while the role's own compose template names the container `zcrypto-ops-liquidations`. `docker inspect` returned rc=1 every time and the assert's `rc == 0` gate skipped it silently, on every ops converge the host has ever run, guarding what its own `fail_msg` calls "the only rollback operand this host has". Root-caused to two independent literals for one name and fixed with a shared role default, plus a drift test proven by reintroducing the exact original literal. The converge that exposed it was safe: what the guard would have checked was verified by hand first.
- **zaccess's `alloy` and `caddy` stop being pinned and held; the repo now FOLLOWS apt.** These are third-party apt packages, not images whose digest is ours to choose, and the pin had already broken the edge: an upstream 1.18.1-1 made the role request a downgrade `ansible.builtin.apt` refuses by default, the task is third in the role, and the host dropped from the play — taking the pinned-leaves and Caddyfile tasks, which *are* the D16 client-cert revocation path, with it. A pin meant to protect the mTLS edge had disabled its one emergency operation.
- **The holds are actively cleared rather than merely dropped**, so a host provisioned from an older tree converges back to the followed state. Caddy rides the same ruling on an operational argument: `apt-mark hold` makes `apt upgrade` skip a package silently, and nothing in that output says which entries are third-party — the burden lands on the human exactly when a long upgradeable list makes it hardest. Its safety does not rest on the version: the Caddyfile task already validates the new config against the **installed** binary, and a Caddy that cannot parse its config does not start, so the failure direction is closed.
- **Both packages are removed from `fleet-pins.md` rather than restated there as "readings"** — a reading has no producer and no consumer, so it drifts into a confidently-wrong number the first time anyone runs `apt upgrade`. Their absence is now the record, with the reasoning at both the file and the role. Accepted limitation, written rather than fixed: no alert fires on a caddy-only outage, tolerable because this tier carries interactive access only and a dead edge is found by a human failing to reach it.

## 2026-08-20 — Kraken announced both venue outages, and the repo said otherwise ([[T0144]] resolved as a refutation)

- **Reviewing the day's alerts refuted a claim merged hours earlier.** Six instances of `Capture · Kraken reports the venue is not online` fired at 07:07–07:16Z — `maintenance` → `cancel_only` → `post_only`, on **both** capture hosts, the same ladder as 2026-08-06. Spec `00096`, the hygiene map and [[T0144]] all said 2026-08-20 was the event Kraken "posted nothing" for, and built a *complementarity* argument on it: venue status catches one event, cross-host catches the other. That asymmetry does not exist.
- **The root defect was collapsing three Kraken sources into one phrase.** The WebSocket `status` channel (capture receives it, counts it, archives it nowhere); the REST `SystemStatus` endpoint (the execution gate reads it, current-state only); and the public status page (a human reads it by eye). [[T0143]] recorded the *status page's* silence correctly and narrowly; the spec promoted that into a claim about the WS channel. The conclusions all survive — cross-host still classifies 08-20, D1 still rules the booking untouched — but the stated motivation was false, and a wrong motivation attached to a right conclusion is still a durable doc telling a future reader something untrue.
- **[[T0144]] resolved as a measured refutation, with the remedy consciously dropped rather than deferred.** Two measurements killed it: 2026-08-06's dark windows are *adjacent*, so its interior span is a zero-length instant no archived status row could fall inside; and the `status` frame carries no venue timestamp at all — only `system`, `version` and an `effectiveTime` observed solely as `None` — so an archived row would hold **local receipt time**, inheriting none of the cross-host verifiability that makes the discriminator sound. That answers the topic's own second next-step, in the negative, from code already in the repo. No successor is owed: every candidate trigger fires only when the remedy provably cannot help.
- **Two runbook methods were unsound and are corrected.** The residual-gap triage told an operator to check the venue-status counter "for that hour", but neither obvious form works — the counter latches until the capture daemon restarts, so bare presence at H+2 h can be a different day's event, and `increase()` is blind to a first sighting, which is exactly the shape of a host that restarted shortly before the event (today's primary did, 1 h 40 m before). Step 4 now sends the reader to the alert's `activeAt` or the log's own timestamped frames, and makes writing the hygiene-map row an imperative — that map, not the counter, is what survives retention.
- **A latched silence can blind the next event entirely, and the runbook now says so.** Silencing the venue-not-online latch is correct and prescribed; carrying it past a capture restart is not. A restart zeroes the counter, so the next event's states are first sightings again, and the recurrence rule's `increase()` cannot see a first sighting — a brand-new outage under a stale silence pages nothing at all. The instruction is now per host, deleted at each host's own next restart, with the reason attached so it is not read as tidiness.
- **Two topics opened from the sweep, and one of them refuted a premise this very entry started out asserting.** [[T0145]]: all four `both_streams_silent` episodes match a **published** "Kraken Website and API Maintenance" entry on Kraken's status API — components including REST and WebSocket, each created **48–145 h ahead**, our capture going dark 1.1–4.3 s after the published start. The 07:01 clustering is not a signature to investigate but a calendar nothing here reads, and [[T0101]] had treated two of these as *unannounced* WS service restarts when both were published six days in advance. It also refutes, in passing, a clause this branch itself wrote — that no retroactively readable form of venue status exists; the maintenance feed retains history. What survives is narrower and still decisive: that feed is third-party HTTP, and D1 keeps soft telemetry out of the booking path. [[T0146]]: the spread calibration's caveat discloses one of the **two** frozen-book intervals inside its own committed window.
- No code changed, no converge, no Grafana push: nothing on a phone-visible surface asserted the false claim, and the exporter already folds a verdict-less record into `undetermined`.

## 2026-08-21 — the spread calibration discloses both of its frozen intervals ([[T0146]] resolved)

- **The caveat named one frozen-book interval; there are two.** `captured-spread-calibration.md` warned that a venue-dark interval sits inside its committed window and is not excluded from the means, naming 2026-08-06. The 2026-07-27 episode is inside the window too and went unnamed — it raised no venue-status alert at the time, so nothing prompted anyone to look.
- **Both were re-measured, and the method changed to the honest one.** The caveat had quoted the venue's *reported* status-ladder span (~1,020 s), but what it is about is fabricated rows in the panel's 1 s grid. Both intervals are now counted the same way — rows with `stale_seconds > 30`, the [[T0104]] column that separates a genuinely quiet second from a carried-forward one — giving 173.2 rows per pair for 07-27 and 852.2 for 08-06, **1,025.3 together, 0.0780 %** of the window.
- **The published percentage needed no change; its attribution did.** The stated 0.078 % turns out to be almost exactly right for the **pair**, while being attributed to one interval it overstates by about a fifth. A figure that is accidentally correct in aggregate and wrong in attribution is the harder kind to catch, so the caveat now shows the split rather than a single number.
- **The sweep was exhaustive, and that is what makes it a resolution rather than a patch**: all 4,380 hour-files of the window present and readable, **exactly two** of its 365 hours carrying any frozen row at all. There is no third interval to find. Threshold sensitivity is immaterial — the total moves ≤7 % relative across a 10–60 s cut, and the two-hour result is stable for any threshold above ~17 s.
- **The hygiene map gained the 2026-07-27 row it never had**, so a continuity-sensitive analysis over that date is flagged like the other two venue episodes rather than silently passing. Found by the review of this change; the map had flagged 08-06 and 08-20 only.
- Unchanged deliberately: the calibration's numbers, its conclusion, and the window. This was a disclosure understating its own exposure, never a reason to distrust the result — and the conclusion it protects (disclosed rather than trimmed, because ending the window early costs the ≥2-week exit bar) stands.
- The question of intervals predating the window is answered rather than carried: 2026-07-13 is the only earlier episode, ten days before the window opens. Re-examining the *superseded* windows is an explicit drop — historical figures with no live consumer.

## 2026-08-21 — the venue publishes its own outage calendar, and the fleet now schedules around it ([[T0145]] resolved)

- **Every fleet-dark episode was a Kraken maintenance published days in advance.** All four `both_streams_silent` records match a "Kraken Website and API Maintenance" entry on `status.kraken.com/api/v2/scheduled-maintenances.json` — components including **REST and WebSocket**, created **48–145 h ahead** — with capture going dark **1.1–4.3 s** after each published start, roughly biweekly at 07:01–07:16 UTC. The 07:01 clustering that [[T0101]] parked as an unexplained signature was a calendar nothing in the repo read, and two of the episodes it filed as *unannounced* WS service restarts had been published six days ahead.
- **The exclusion landed on both surfaces an operator actually consults**: `.claude/rules/fleet-deploys.md`'s `## Deploys` for converges, and `docs/reference/fleet.md`'s reboot `Schedule:` line, because the ~83 s reboot gap creates the identical conflation. It carries the component filter — most of that feed is delistings that touch nothing we capture — and the trap that entries appear only **2–6 days ahead**, so an empty feed a week out is not evidence the window is clear.
- **Discharged on a retrospective test rather than a manufactured converge**, and the rejected alternative is the more instructive half. Scheduling a converge purely to execute the rule, then rolling it back, was declined: no capture re-pin is owed (capture and engine both run `636012cc00d9`; it is ops that is ahead, and pins match the service rather than the repo), the rollback is itself a second converge, and it would have cost two restarts on the unbackfillable path to prove nothing that mattered — the only unproven part, that a human consults the rule, is equally unproven for every other rule here and gates none of them.
- **The proof that replaced it**: every time-bearing timestamp `fleet-pins.md` records anywhere in its history — 52, spanning 2026-07-26 to 2026-08-20 — checked against the four published windows. **Zero** ever landed inside one; the closest approach is **99.2 minutes** — the 2026-08-20 primary capture re-pin at 05:21:51, one hour thirty-nine before that same morning's outage. A rule that has never bound is not a rule that cannot bind, and the near-miss is what separates them. The topic states the sweep's two blind spots — the file postdates the 2026-07-13 window, and 11 pinned dates carry no clock time — because a clean result is only as good as its population.
- **The `ripe_when` was the real defect.** It demanded "a converge has been placed using it", making the topic hostage to unrelated work and inventing a gate applied to nothing else in `.claude/rules/`. Holding the branch for it would also have kept the guidance *off* `develop` — unavailable at exactly the moment it is meant to be read.

## 2026-08-21 — the reconcile cycle stops scaling with market volume ([[T0147]] partial, spec `00097`)

- **The defect, found sideways.** While triaging why `Reconciler · residual gap increased` had "kept firing", the ops writer cycle turned out to be taking **1,371 s against its own 1,800 s tick** — and its duration is simply the daily segment-byte series pushed through a sliding 48 h sum (315 s at the 2026-08-16 weekend floor, 20–21 MB/day in-window; 1,371 s with 92 and 129 MB/day in-window). A systemd trigger that fires while the unit is still `activating` is dropped, not queued, so sustained ~150 MB/day would have silently halved the booking cadence with nothing paging below the 3 h staleness backstop. The alert itself was a non-event: `increase(...[24h])` holds one booking red for a day, and it cleared on its own 24 h after the step's scrape, counter flat throughout.
- **~97 % of the cycle was Python-object timestamp arithmetic** — profiled on real data: 60 % `PySeries.to_list`, 20 % `fleet_dark_windows`, and actual parquet decode **2 %**. Two independent fixes compose: vectorize the arithmetic onto int64-microsecond arrays (removes the constant), and skip settled hours behind a fingerprint (removes the scaling).
- **Measured on the live mirrors, one 72 h window, all three runs started inside the same UTC hour**: develop (the exact merge-base) **1428.79 s** → branch cold **120.23 s** → branch warm **15.90 s**, i.e. **89.9×**. develop ran last with a warm page cache, so the ratios are conservative.
- **Byte-equality is the contract, and it is proven rather than argued.** The golden replay's ledgers are byte-identical (same sha256 with `at` popped), microsecond spans and the 2026-08-20 dark hour's `venue_silent` verdict line reproduced verbatim. That window contained no heal, so the mint path is covered separately: a constructed branch-vs-develop A/B over 7 scenarios × detect/mint with **24 minted parquet sha256s, zero differences**, plus a cache-on vs cache-off run with every minted parquet byte-identical.
- **The skip-cache is built to fail open, never wrong.** An hour is skipped only when its fingerprint matches, the recorded examination was late and clean, no expected file is absent, and the cycle changed nothing about that hour. Two properties took two review rounds to get right: the fingerprint's *presence* must come from the same `scan_hours` result the examination uses rather than a fresh `stat` — otherwise a mirror landing between scan and fingerprint is recorded as examined though never read, and that hour is skipped forever — and the overlay must be fingerprinted too, or a correct hand-repair is silently suppressed. Anything unreadable, corrupt, mode-mismatched or stale reads as no cache at all; a rotating two-hour audit catches a poisoned hour within ~11 h, well inside the 48 h window, and any divergence pages and drops the whole cache.
- **Two operator surfaces, and one design lesson.** `zcrypto_reconcile_cycle_duration_seconds` and `zcrypto_reconcile_hours_skipped` are published and charted, with the `zcrypto-reconcile-cycle-duration` warning rule staged for a post-merge push. The skip gauge is **incremented inside the skip branch**, never derived from the planned count — proven by a discriminating mutation pair in which the plan-derived form passes the very test the observed form fails. The same defect was found and fixed in the completion log line the runbook tells the operator to read.
- **Docs that had drifted, corrected in place**: the residual-gap runbook's staleness arithmetic (the stamp now lands at cycle completion), and `infra/nas/README.md`'s "the overlay carries nothing else" invariant, which had become false and whose ledger-correction procedure now carries the obligation to delete the cache in the same act.

## 2026-08-21 — the 00097 rollout: 1,636 s → 18 s in production ([[T0147]] resolved)

- **The old image's final cycle was the defect's closing argument**: started 17:42:54Z, pre-converge, it ran **1,636 s — 91 % of its own 1,800 s tick** (up from the 1,371 s that opened the investigation ~9.5 h earlier, the same morning) and cleared the 18:12 trigger by 110 seconds. Volume kept climbing while the fix was built.
- **The converge** (`06919e5dc50c`, revision `52b12ca1`, 18:03:00Z measured from the written file's mtime, evidence in the pins commit): preview read in full, every diff line a digest substitution, every role gate passed on its own merits — "pins override accepted" *skipped*. The liquidations roll followed as its own act at 18:03:52Z, digest read back from the container, first poll clean one second later.
- **The two cycles the resolution was defined on**: cache build **101 s** (`skipped=0 audited=0`), steady state **18 s** (`skipped=42 audited=2`; 42+2+4 never-late = 48 exactly). Totals identical across old and new (`healed_s=18850.2 residual_s=21887.4 failures=0`) — the cache changed which hours were examined, never what any hour decided. **90.9× in production against the golden replay's predicted 89.9×.**
- **Telemetry verified by value**: `cycle_duration_seconds = 16.956187`, `hours_skipped = 42`, `last_success` end-stamped `18:42:37` matching the journal's `Finished` line to the second. The `zcrypto-reconcile-cycle-duration` rule was pushed only after that first sample existed and its first evaluation read the live series (`health=ok`, `Normal` at 16.96 s vs 1,500). `ops-postverify.sh` ALL PASS, residual/healable counters unbumped.

## 2026-08-21 — trial 43's "unrebuildable" runners were recovered, and the transcript that held them was pruned 27 minutes later ([[T0148]])

- **[[T0125]] recorded trial 43 as unrebuildable; it was not.** Its `run_ref` names scratchpad scripts and the scratchpad was gone, but Claude session transcripts record every `Write` and `Edit` verbatim — and one local transcript *was* the iter-080/081 session, the scripts having run from that session's own scratchpad, the exact path `run_ref` records. All five scripts were reconstructed by replaying the initial `Write` then every `Edit` in order with each `old_string` required to match: **13/13 operations, 0 failures.**
- **The reproduction, not the replay, is the proof — and the distinction matters.** A clean replay only shows the recorded Edit chain is self-consistent; it cannot see a shell-side mutation or an edit after the last recorded one. What settles it is behaviour: the recovered stage-1 driver, run 2026-08-21 against committed code, printed `governed noc Sharpe: 1.5366 full / 1.5319 decisive`, `maxDD 13.31%`, `cap-breach 1265`, `gov-engaged 7290`, and — the strongest single line — `equal-weight fallback bars: 180 warm-up + 10638 zero-vol degenerate`. **180 and 10638 are registry row 43's `weight_warmup_bars` and `weight_zero_vol_fallback_bars`**, and they fall out of the computation rather than being fitted. Every upstream gate matched first (B 1.2455, A1 1.3798, arms 1.3274 / 1.3017 / 1.3585, benchmark 1.2128 / 1.2447).
- **This is recovery, which is why [[T0125]]'s "reconstruction is a trap" objection does not apply.** That objection is that a rebuild from the variant string "proves only that it was tuned until it did". These are the original bytes, and the same transcript independently recorded their 2026-07-10 output — the thing a reconstruction can never have.
- **The source transcript was pruned four minutes after it was read, and 27 minutes before the commit that preserved it.** A 30-day retention sweep took the four transcripts older than 2026-07-22; the source was dated 07-21. It exists nowhere on disk. **The git history is now the only surviving copy and the replay is permanently unfalsifiable** — the topic's own stated hazard ("workstation-local, unbacked, prunable") firing inside the session that documented it, and the strongest possible argument for committing bytes rather than a pointer.
- **Scope stated rather than implied.** Only the stage-1 driver was re-run; the other four scripts rest on the replay alone, and the run covers **15 of row 43's 35 metrics**. The 20 uncovered include every SPA cell, `dsr`, both cost-stress figures, and `fixed_third_counterfactual_sharpe_ann` — which is the 43-vs-44 number a future measurement would re-read. Two substitutions were required, both forced by *upstream* artifacts also lost and both validated against **registered** figures; the `bench_noc` one is Sharpe-only, sufficient for stage 1 and explicitly not beyond it.
- **The record was then corrected in place, with the ruling untouched (2026-08-21, same branch — the recovery evaluation was delegated to the session).** [[T0125]]'s archived file, [[T0090]]'s "NOT POSSIBLE" finding, the master plan's §12 gate-limitations paragraph, and the index bullet were each rewritten where they stand, per `agent-ops.md`'s read-correctly-cold rule; the re-grounding ruling and the forward `run_ref` guard were deliberately not touched, and nothing re-couples the gate to trial 43. Point-in-time carriers deliberately left and named: the two 2026-08-03 entries above this one, and spec `00095`'s "remains unrebuildable" line — immutable, because record 47's `spec_hash` pins that file. The reproduction was also repeated on a second workstation (2 min 07 s, every figure identical to row 43 including the discriminating 180/10638 fallback bars), so the recovery rests on two machines, not one.
- **The recovery ends as maintained code, not as a museum — trial 43 now has an instrument exactly as trial 44 does.** The seven files were committed first and guarded while they sat in HEAD the way `infra/nas/rrsync` is (both hook exemptions plus a tripwire test pinning all seven sha256s, its three defect classes constructed and seen to trip it), because bytes whose only value is being unmodified need a hook that *notices*. Then trial 43's stage-1 construction was ported into **`cli/portfolio/record43_book.py`** — the treatment `record44_legs.py` already gives row 44 — and the frozen directory, its two exemptions and its tripwire left HEAD in the same branch. Nothing is lost by that deletion: it sits ON TOP of the recovery commit and this repo merges with merge commits, so `git log --all --full-history --diff-filter=A -- 'docs/reference/trial43-recovered-runners/*'` then `git show <that-commit>:<path>` retrieves any original, and the module's docstring carries that recipe plus all seven sha256s as the authenticity anchor. A pointer to a directory has become code that runs.
- **The port reproduces row 43 from committed code, and one import dissolved the blocker.** `uv run python -m cli.portfolio.record43_book` (2 min 06 s): 1.5365681705210812 / 1.531911194152131, maxDD 0.13314492965246028 / 0.19460943509752548, cap-breach **1265**, governor **7290**, fallback bars **180 + 10638**, drag 0.0339796422351736, criterion flags 1/0 — every one matching row 43 at its registered precision, with the B sleeve's elementwise agreement against the committed builder reading a **max abs diff of exactly 0.0**. The recovered variant's two upstream-forced substitutions carry over, but the second stops being a substitution: the module **imports `record44_legs.benchmark_4h_net_of_cost`**, so one committed constructor serves both records and there is no second series to pin — which is precisely the elementwise precondition [[T0148]]'s maker–taker sweep was blocked on, dissolved by construction rather than discharged by a comparison script. `tests/test_record43_book.py` mirrors its record-44 sibling: the reproduction is data-gated (it RAN here, 2 min 07 s — a skip would have proven nothing), and four unit tests that always run cover what record 44 has no analogue for, the adaptive sleeve-weight rule (warm-up fallback, any-degenerate fallback, the window ending at k−1) and the dense-rank governor day index.
- **The measurement [[T0125]] recorded as permanently unanswerable was taken, and the instrument was validated at full precision first.** The stress axis is the registered one, retrieved from git history rather than invented — both drivers wrote `noc[k] - turn[k] * 0.003`, re-governed through the same daily cadence, with positions and sleeve weights **not** rebuilt. On it, record 43 reads 1.5365681705210812 / 1.300757484521457 / 1.239988597323478 at ×1.0 / ×1.5 / ×2.0 and record 44 reads 1.560907676587497 / 1.3028688330561256 / 1.2106292366788787 — all six matching the registry, and record 44's two stress figures **bit-identical** to the values spec `00095` measured independently through the production surfaces. Re-governing record 44's own ×1.0 net reproduces its builder's governed series at max abs diff exactly **0.0**. Both halves are therefore anchored outside the sweeping module before a single swept point counts.
- **The answer is that there is no crossing to find, and the honest version is the more useful one.** Swept at a 0.002 step over \[×0.70, ×3.00\] — 1151 points, spanning the whole measured execution band and far past any executable cost — the 43-vs-44 ordering **changes sign 117 times**: cost → Sharpe is a jagged step function because the drawdown governor re-engages on the net-of-cost series. **Neither book holds a durable lead anywhere in the range**: record 43 leads at 478 points (widest −0.1539 at ×2.75), record 44 at 673 (widest +0.2082 at ×2.506), and both still lead within 0.01 of the ceiling (43 at ×2.99, 44 at ×3.00). A first pass at a 0.01 step stopping at ×2.00 reported ×1.8534 as a persistent reversal — **that was a grid artifact and is withdrawn**: at 0.002 record 44 leads again at ×1.874 by +0.0016 (76× the grid's smallest counted margin of 2.1e-05, so resolution rather than float noise), and across \[×2.0, ×3.0\] at a 0.02 step it leads at 34 of 51 points, up to +0.1894. Inside the band the same instability holds — 351 points, 128 record-43 leads against 223 for record 44, 19 sign changes, margins −0.0438 to +0.1486 — and at the **maker** half the maker-first ruling targets (≤×1.0) record 43 leads at **107 of 150** points. So record 44's **+0.0243 ADOPT margin is a point convention, not a regime**: it exists at the ×1.0 headroom basis and the sign is unstable either side of it. That is decision-support and nothing more: per [[T0125]] the go/no-go reads the benchmark-relative basis, nothing re-couples to trial 43, and record 44's other adoption reason — no adaptive-weight state to build, journal or reconcile — is untouched. If anything the measurement **strengthens** the re-grounding: a counterfactual this unstable could never have carried a gate, whichever way it happened to point.

## 2026-08-22 — iter-143: adopted orders' fills become observable, without widening the kill trip ([[T0142]] resolved, spec `00098`)

- **The topic's dilemma was a false binary, and dissolving it is the whole design.** T0142 posed claim-the-instruments (observability, but the operator's sanctioned hand settle routes into the unknown-order kill trip mid-window) against stay-blind. Measured against the installed nautilus 1.230.0: the engine publishes a reconciled EXTERNAL order's events **unconditionally** on `events.order.EXTERNAL` — the topic simply had no subscriber. The engine now subscribes it and filters by disposition against the ledger-vouched `_attached` rows: **matched** fills append to their forensic row, move the fill/fee counters, and latch the overfill trip exactly as an own order's fill does (a ledgered adopted row is this engine's own pre-restart order; divergence on it is the same class); terminal acks close their rows — the adopt pass's own cancel acks now close rows that previously read possibly-live on every future scan; **unmatched** events are counted and logged, and reach no trip, no row, no cancel. `external_order_claims` stays unset, and the claim is enforced structurally: the text-ban guard admits `msgbus` in exactly `cli/engine/node.py` at exactly **one occurrence** — count-pinned after a review planted an uncalled wildcard subscription in the allowlisted file and watched the file-level form pass — with a vacuity assert (`>100 files walked`) closing the off-root hole where the walk silently found nothing.
- **The no-pop symmetry was the review loop earning its cost on the spec itself.** The spec's first ruling had the external path pop rows on terminal acks; the Task 2 review reproduced the race the spec's own D4 cites (Kraken's ownTrades and openOrders are separate WS channels, no cross-stream ordering), where a fill racing a cancel ack arrives unmatched — counted but never journaled, on the one path built to restore the no-fill-without-a-record invariant. Amended: **neither path ever removes rows from `_attached`**; a late fill within the ledgered quantity journals as a detached append, one beyond it trips the overfill arm, on either path. Completion is instead marked once via the in-memory row-state mirror when `filled_qty` reaches the ledgered quantity — nautilus publishes no separate terminal event after a resting order's final fill.
- **The library boundary is pinned two ways, and the pending 1.231.0 bump is pre-cleared.** A real `MessageBus` test publishes a genuine `OrderFilled` on the topic string the installed engine's own format produces; a second test **registers a real strategy** against a real bus/portfolio/cache and derives the expected topic from the library's own register-time subscriptions — so an engine-side prefix change fails in the suite, not in production. The 1.231.0 wheel (PR #270) was read directly: `_get_order_events_topic` and `EXTERNAL_STRATEGY_ID` byte-identical.
