- **One counter, lifecycle complete on arrival**: `zcrypto_exec_external_events_total{disposition="matched"|"unmatched"}`, eagerly registered at 0 for both labels, admitted to the capture keep-regex (70 enumerated names — which is why **the deploy must converge `--tags capture,engine`, never `--tags engine` alone**), charted on the exec-outcomes panel (raw counter deliberately: the absolute unmatched count is the forensic datum, and the shared axis's sibling is raw), and entered in `NOT_A_FAULT_SIGNAL` — a forensic instrument, not a page; a nonzero unmatched count is information.
- **The whole-branch review at the Fable floor found the question the spec never asked, and it changed the shipped behaviour.** Two lenses independently traced the same thing: `system/kernel.py` awaits execution reconciliation **strictly before** `trader.start()` — which is what runs `on_start` and installs this subscription — and the message bus has no replay for late subscribers (probed against the installed build). So a fill that happened while the process was DOWN is replayed onto `events.order.EXTERNAL` before any subscriber exists and reaches nobody; a fill landing before the first adopt tick matches nothing and is counted `unmatched`; and an order that fully filled or was canceled while down never reaches `orders_open` at all, so its row is never even attached. **None of the three is created by this branch** — `_adopt_resting_orders`' body predates it — but D1–D4's claims are true only of the window the subscription can reach, so the record would have overclaimed.
- **Spec D7 closes all three from the one place that holds venue truth for exactly those fills.** The reconciled order's own `filled_qty` carries them, because the engine applies an event to the order and publishes it in one synchronous body on one event loop — which is also why the repair cannot double-count what the subscription delivers: any quantity resident at snapshot time was published at or before the snapshot, so it was either never delivered or dropped unmatched. The adopt pass now reads the cache **wide** (closed orders included — the closed-while-down case is absent from the open read by definition), indexes it by client order id, and takes one of four arms on an `_OVERFILL_TOLERANCE` dead-band: silent inside it; a positive delta journaled as a **repair, never as a fill** (there is no fee behind it, and a fills increment with no fee would make the two counters disagree in a way the ledger cannot explain); a repair past the ledgered quantity journaled and *then* tripping; and a **negative** delta — the ledger claiming more filled than the venue reports — tripping rather than being clamped, because that is divergence in the direction that makes the engine believe it reduced more than it did. A closed order additionally gets its terminal state from a dedicated `OrderStatus` map, independent of any delta: a cancel-while-down with zero fills is the commonest shape and has no delta at all.
- **Two review rounds on D7 itself, each catching something the code would have shipped.** The cold review returned REVISE on ten findings — the repair arm had no dead-band (a clean multi-fill restart differs by ulps, so it would have journaled a phantom repair on **every** restart), the terminal write was coupled to the delta (losing the commonest closed-while-down shape forever), and the map named was keyed on event class names with no `FILLED` entry. The task review then found a live-path defect in the implementation: the sweep wrapped its ledger writes and its `_trip_kill` calls in one `try`, so a read-only journal would have **suppressed a boot-time kill** — reproduced by construction on both trip arms and contrasted against the live-overfill path that still latches, and the inverse of the very precedent D7 cites (`_record_trip_fill` scopes its `try` to the ledger call alone so a ledger failure can never cost the trip). Each write now carries its own, with the mirror tied to the attempted fill rather than the write's success.
- **A forward hazard closed on the way past**: `filter_unclaimed_external_orders` was neither set nor pinned. Flipped by a future upstream default, reconciliation returns `None` for VENUE-tagged unclaimed orders, the adopted order never enters the cache, and the startup pass neither attaches nor **cancels** a previous process's resting order — with the kill switch's own cancel sweep reading the same cache and unable to reach it either, the Kraken adapter diverting the later fill reports before the engine's ERROR, and every test green. Both knobs are now explicit and pinned by the config-shape test, whose failure is the only thing that would surface an upstream flip.
- **Found mid-iteration and fixed in place per the standing directive** (no new topics): the dashboard-text guard walked `title`/`description`/`content` but not `legendFormat` — an internal token planted in a legend shipped unguarded (proven both ways: killed post-fix, survived pre-fix). The walk now scans legends; all 206 legend values across the four boards pass it; the rule's scope list names the surface in the same change.
- **Doc surfaces moved with the behaviour**: `_adopt_resting_orders`' docstring and the runbook's arm step rewritten in place (the arm step now scopes "no trip" to fill-time trips — the position-reconciliation trip under the settle preconditions is untouched by this change and can still fire); the claim swept across `cli/`, `infra/`, `tests/`, `docs/` with point-in-time carriers named and left; the `[iter-143]` decisions-log entry records all three of T0142's candidates rejected as written, after a review caught the first draft misstating one and omitting another.
- **Suite**: **3806 passed, 0 failed** in full (data-gated regression tests executed, 660 s); every implementation, fix, test, and doc-surface commit carries a `Reviewed-by:` trailer from a different Opus-floor agent (the spec/plan commits ride the user-approved producing flow and the cold spec+plan review); the whole-branch Fable-floor review (live trade path) runs before push. T0142 archived ahead of its never-fired ripeness trigger by owner fiat; the hand-settle measurement's order-event half is instrumented permanently by the unmatched counter, its position-propagation half stays homed in the probe-window runbook.
## 2026-08-22 — the one-sleeve era ended, and every number derived under it was re-measured ([[T0149]] resolved)

- **The reversal [[T0124]] built an alert for arrived.** At the 2026-08-22 00:00 UTC cycle `zcrypto_engine_active_sleeves` stepped **1 → 3**: B armed to 0.1801192405996337 gross and A1 to 0.05523539758932526, both from exactly zero, where they had sat since ~2025-10-30 and ~2025-11-09. `Engine · sleeve composition changed` fired at 00:08:30Z. The *previous* such transition went unobserved for ~9 months; this one was announced in nine minutes and closed out the same day.
- **The alert's own explanation of what it meant was falsified by the transition it announced.** Rule comment and runbook both said gross moves roughly in proportion to the count — a third sleeve arming "close to a tripling". Measured on the final book (`sum|target_weight|`, which equals the journal's `sum|final_targets|`): **0.10613643820896906 → 0.12248545184035509**, a factor of **1.154**, and back to **0.10446994532433734** by the 12:00 cycle — *below* where it started, because A2's own gross fell 0.6368186292538144 → 0.3914650337570651 while the other two armed. Both surfaces corrected in place. The rule-COMMENT edits are payload-neutral (parsed rule sets hash identically), but this branch does not get away without a push: the alert's own `summary` — the text that reaches Slack — carried the same falsified claim and is rewritten, the engine dashboard's panel description likewise, and the notification-repeat below is a payload field. **A `grafana-push` is owed**, and the rule must be read back afterwards to confirm `notification_settings.repeat_interval` survived: the provisioning API drops unknown fields silently and the push script's read-back checks only datasource UIDs.
- **A derivation trap, caught and recorded**: the combined book is *not* in general the summed sleeve gross ÷ 3 (nor the final book that ÷ 6) — the ⅓ combination nets opposing sleeve positions away asset by asset (`cancellation_ratio` exists to name how much survives). Here nothing cancelled (ratio 1.0, verified to within one ulp on both sides of the step), but the ledger's read recipe now says to read `sum(abs(zcrypto_engine_target_weight))` directly rather than derive it.
- **[[T0116]]'s re-derivation trigger had fired — "either dormant sleeve re-arms" — and all three of its numbers were re-measured rather than scaled**, after validating the instrument: `accum-replay` over the registered window reproduces the registered band at €1,000 as **51.1 / 115.7 bps** against its recorded 51.2 / 115.7, three of four weekly means identical to the decimal.
- **The band was re-derived across the whole NAV curve, and the gate-relevant result is one row.** One continuous replay 07-11 → 08-22 (257 cycles, 0 failed), nearest-rank p95 throughout, reproduces T0116's registered curve to within a decimal on every cell (its 136 cycles against these 138) — and shows the pre-registered **10 bps band, which T0116 recorded as meetable at €10,000 (8.9), is now unmet at every NAV measured (€10,000 reads 12.7)**. At rung 3's own €1,000 the p95 widened ×1.28 (115.7 → 148.1) while the median did not move (51.1 → 54.3), and T0116 pinned p95 as the gate's statistic.
- **Two caveats on that ×1.28, both against the headline.** The replay initialises held quantity to zero at its first cycle, so a standalone run of 08-15 → 08-22 reads 95.0/168.1 — pure flat-start artifact — against 53.9/148.1 for the identical 46 cycles carried from 07-11. But the registered window is the start of the chain and carries the same artifact: drop its first two days and it reads 49.7/**91.0**, making the de-transiented widening ≈**×1.63**. The direction survives either way; the magnitude is a range, not a point. And the window's own median book gross is **2.312 %**, *below* the registered 5.297 % — it separates neither the gross step nor the composition step, so a band the gate can rest on still needs T0116's ≥3 complete ISO weeks. That instruction now lives on the operating surface (the alert's runbook step 3, with the command and the initialisation trap) rather than in an archived topic.
- **T0116's mechanism was right and my first explanation was wrong.** I claimed the larger target moves "still miss `ordermin` and land in drift"; the placement counts in the same window refute it — placement *rises* with gross (21 % of cycles over the registered window, 39 % over 08-19..08-21, 50 % on 08-22; 15 intended orders cleared both floors across 08-19..08-21 against 2 across 08-15..08-18). What actually fails is T0116's *inference*: the floor is not monotone in book size, because each asset's gap saturates toward a roughly fixed `ordermin × close` euro amount. Recorded because the wrong mechanism would have sent the next re-derivation looking in the wrong place.
- Stated rather than glossed: the band window's own median book gross is **2.312 %**, *below* the registered window's 5.297 % — the doubling belongs to its last 22 cycles alone, so that window separates neither the gross step nor the composition step. A clean three-sleeve band still wants T0116's ≥3 complete ISO weeks.
- **The alert and its runbook had never drawn the binding-floor distinction** — though T0116's findings and `14.phase6b-orientation.md` both already had. Read from the engine's own intended orders: `ordermin` (a base-unit quantity floor) binds by one to two orders of magnitude, while `costmin` is a flat €0.45 that nine of ten legs cleared at the step — only DOGE missed it, and **none** cleared ordermin. Swept journal-wide, **63 of 1,572 intended orders (4.0 %) clear both floors, in 44 of 256 cycles**: placement tracks the size of the target moves, not the sleeve count. The step cycle placed nothing and its nearest miss was BTC at 95.8 % of its floor — striking, but the journal-wide maximum is **ETH at 749.2 % on 08-19 16:00**, three days earlier with one sleeve live, in a cycle that placed five of its eight. The topic's own opening speculation — that more sleeves would spread exposure and shrink notionals — is refuted: the same **ten** legs carried weight before and after, so the step made orders larger, not smaller.
- **Expected gross roughly doubled, and mostly before the composition changed**: median final gross per cycle reads 5.297 % over the registered window (reproducing its 5.371 %), 3.320 % over 08-10 → 08-18, **9.625 % over 08-19 → 08-21 with one sleeve still live**, and 10.977 % over the four three-sleeve cycles. Two events, not one — the re-derivation should not conflate them.
- **T0116's companion check discharged in the same pass**: the venue minimums are byte-identical between the 2026-07-07 snapshot the registered figures rest on and the 2026-08-04 one — 0 of 10 bases moved — so none of the above is contaminated by a minimums change ([[T0113]]).
- The fixed ⅓ weights are **reserved, not settled**: no change is proposed and the owner has not been asked, so the question goes to them in this branch's PR. Altering what registry record 47 ratified is a re-ratification rather than a tuning, and nothing measured here argues for one — the sleeves agreed in sign throughout (cancellation ratio 1.0).
- **The alert's delivery was wrong for its class, and that is a separate fix.** One composition change produced **five** Slack messages by 19:33Z (00:09 / 04:09 / 08:10 / 12:14 / 16:14), on track for about seven before the condition aged out — all from a SINGLE firing instance (`activeAt 00:08:30Z`, `changes(active_sleeves[26h])` a steady 1) re-notified at the default repeat. An announcement whose runbook opens "Nothing is broken" should not page seven times. `repeat_interval: 12h` now sits on the rule's own `notification_settings` — scoped there rather than in the policy tree, which is shared by all 73 rules and is not committed as code. 12h rather than the full 26h window is a deliberate trade: a SECOND composition step reuses the same alert instance (identical labels; the count lives in an annotation, invisible to dedup), so the repeat interval IS the detection latency for it, and 26h would have silenced the rule for a whole window.
- Nothing was live on any of it: `zcrypto_exec_armed` 0 and `zcrypto_exec_kill_tripped` 0 throughout, gate unaffected (`zcrypto_gate_status` 1, streak 39 → 42).

## 2026-08-23 — iter-145: the weekly tracking-error comparison, the ledger reader behind its cost half, and a trip for an engine that has quietly stopped placing (spec `00091`, [[T0018]] and [[T0090]] still partial)

- **Three components in three risk tiers, and the spec says so up front because they do not ship alike.** A read-only weekly report (`zcrypto engine tracking-report`), a read-only reader for the owner's Kraken ledger export, and a **kill-switch trip on the live trade path**. An earlier draft of the spec carried only the first and silently dropped the other two — which is exactly how a read-only framing smuggles a mutating guard onto the order path — so the risk table is the spec's opening rather than a footnote.
- **The realized half is measured in the SAME quantity as the floor, and every way of getting that wrong was a real draft.** Held accumulates **signed base quantity**, never EUR: a EUR-denominated held compares a price-stale held against a freshly priced target and reports zero drift across a pure price move, which `accumulation_payload`'s own docstring names as the anti-pattern. It is **base-keyed with the two `/BTC` legs excluded and counted**, not contracted — `select_model_inputs` *drops* them rather than contracting them, so folding an `ETH/BTC` fill into `held["ETH"]` would inflate held against a target that never included it. Attribution is **post-decision**, through the fill's own submitted row, never by comparing a fill's wall-clock stamp against a boundary (which lands a fill arriving minutes late on the next boundary). And one key space per run, with a window straddling the journal's schema bump saying so in its output. The report and the trip call the **same** drift function, so the number a human bands and the number the engine trips on cannot diverge.
- **A pure price move DOES move realized drift, and that is the signal rather than an artifact.** An earlier spec claim that a price move "re-prices target and held together" was false and is withdrawn: at NAV 1,000 with the close moving 50,000 → 60,000, a one-fill book reads target 0.01667 BTC against held 0.02 — **2,000 bps**. NAV is held constant by design, so an engine that keeps trading tracks the target while one that has stopped accumulates drift without bound, which is precisely the divergence the whole comparison exists to detect.
- **It fails closed rather than reporting a number it cannot stand behind.** A week with no fills reads *no data*, never zero drift. `NO_LIQUIDITY_SIDE` is a name the venue's own enum yields, so such a fill stays in the drift half, leaves the blend, and is counted; only a value the enum cannot name at all — `"1"`, the `IntFlag` defect this repo once shipped into its own forensic ledger — aborts the run, and the reader matches the **uppercase** name the ledger actually stores, because a lowercase-only match would abort every real fill while every lowercase fixture passed. A non-EUR `fee_currency` disables the **cost half alone** (the `/BTC` legs legitimately pay BTC-denominated fees), and the euro test imports the executor's `_EUR_CODES` rather than testing `== "EUR"`, so a `ZEUR` fill cannot quietly drop out as a foreign currency.
- **Built before its input exists, so a true-positive carries the proof rather than fixtures.** `--simulated-fills` feeds the floor policy's own placements through the entire reader over the real engine journal, and the assertion that bites is that the realized weekly means **reproduce the floor's, week for week** — a wrong side, a wrong quantity, or a fill booked at the wrong boundary cannot survive it, while "it produced three decided weeks" survives all three. Eligibility is fail-closed in the same spirit: absent `--gate-from` **no** week is decided whatever the numbers say, proven on the one complete week that decided itself under the inverted default. The flag validates nothing against the journal on purpose — the first counting week is a fact about the deployment — so the runbook now says which week to pass and how to confirm it landed.
- **The cost half proposes and never applies, and it prices the fee term rather than the summed cost.** It reports the realized maker share, the blend it implies, the registered constant and a proposal with `n`, the split and the per-fill cost's **min/median/max** (a spread, because a handful of probe-scale fills cannot support a parametric dispersion) — against `CrossfreqSystemConfig.fee_per_side`, **imported rather than written as a literal**. Quoting a fee-only realized rate against the `fee + spread` sum would silently delete the `spread_per_side` term [[T0090]]'s ruling exists to keep separate. It writes nothing: that constant is an input registry record 47 was validated under.
- **The ledger-export reader exists for the one cost no fill can carry.** A margin position's rollover fee is charged by the venue against the *position*, so a cost basis built from fills alone omits it. Every consumed export row must match a journaled fill by `trade_id` or be a rollover row; an unmatched row fails the **reconciliation** — named ids, non-zero exit, and the cost half refusing to publish a blend over a ledger it could not reconcile — while the drift half still prints, because taking the whole instrument down denies the operator the numbers they need to investigate. A rollover row counts as a euro cost only when its asset passes the same euro test the fill reader uses.
- **The trip's call site is the 4-hourly boundary alert, and that is the finding three review rounds died on.** Every other execution guard sits behind an operator-written plan file, so a hook in the 5-second tick could only fire while a plan existed — never in the stopped-placing state the trip exists to catch. `_on_cycle_alert` reads no plan, no `self._plan` and no venue. It carries **no durable state**: the most recently closed ISO week is re-derived from immutable journal artifacts every boundary, idempotence coming from the kill file. Eligibility is the **journaled** per-boundary `level == "full"`, not the live config — the largest spurious-trip vector found, since the restart hold is written at every start and cleared by hand, so a week spent held would otherwise read as fully armed while the engine never traded. It refuses far more than it decides (a short week, any boundary below `full`, the week the fill series started in, records without journaled closes, a target missing a model leg, a journal whose head has been pruned), it cannot raise into the alert chain, and it publishes an unscored state rather than leaving the last verdict standing — a trip that has stopped working must not read like one that keeps passing.
- **The precondition for all of that is a schema widening: the cycle artifact now journals the `closes` it used.** Reconstructing one ISO week of `CycleStages` was **measured at 73 s** on the real journal, which inside the executor blocks the event loop for over a minute with orders possibly resting and pushes `completed_at` toward the `[B, B+30 min]` bound that is itself a gate-streak condition. The widening lives in `journal.py`, where the record's explicit key lists and its schema-aware validator are; `run_cycle` passes the base-keyed `model_h4` it already holds. The closes are an **input** rather than a derived number, so they stay true against code that changes around them, and the reader tolerating the key's absence keeps every existing artifact readable.
- **Nothing here is deployed, and this deploy is not an ordinary one.** The trip reaches the fleet only through an attended, canary-gated engine converge run **`--tags capture,engine`** — the keep-regex admitting `zcrypto_exec_tracking_state` lives in the *capture* role, so `--tags engine` alone would ship the trip with its state dark — with every reader converged before the writer of the widened record. The branch also puts `command.py`, `cycle.py` and `journal.py` inside the NAS gate-export's replay closure, so that leg replays at its measured **2490 s** cold cost and the window must be sized against that number.
- **The trip ships disarmed, and today it is structurally inert even if armed.** `tracking_band_bps` is absent (a parsed config key with `isfinite` and `> 0` refusals, not a `.get()`), and the trip is refused outright while `exec_armed` is false. Beyond that, a verdict needs **42 consecutive boundaries journaled at `full`** — and as of 2026-08-22 all 66 live exec records read `level: "none"` (`config_not_armed, arm_file_absent, restart_hold`), so no week can be gate-eligible until an armed window changes that. Counting `full` across a window's exec records is now a **verify-by-outcome step of the probe-window procedure**, and the arming procedure's first precondition is that this count reads `full` before any band is set.
- **The realized half has no input yet, and the honest output says so.** Zero fills have ever been journaled, so `tracking-report` reads *no data* on every week and the cost half can only answer that there are no euro-denominated fills in the window. **The instruments are this iteration's deliverable; the numbers are owed at rung 1** — which is exactly the split [[T0090]]'s remaining next-step now records, rather than reading as closed.
- **A residual the reviews named and this branch did not close, stated rather than parked.** The trip measures cumulatively from the engine's first ever fill, while the journal prune deletes whole day-dirs at the 60-day retention: once the day holding that first fill ages out, the position bought before the horizon is not on the host at all, and a week scored without it reads hundreds of bps high — a latched kill file on an engine that never misbehaved. The engine therefore dates its own series once, at the first boundary that can witness it, and refuses every week from the moment that date falls off the journal. A **7-day mint recency bound** turns the wrong-birth cases into loud permanent refusals instead of false kills, but four coincidences still defeat it — a birth record lost or a state directory rebuilt, fills already in existence, a prune that happened to cut at a quiet boundary, and an earliest surviving fill less than seven days old. The class answer is to journal the **position** beside the `closes`, which is named here and deliberately not done; no topic was opened for it, per the standing instruction.
- **Doc surfaces moved with the behaviour, in this branch.** The probe-window procedure gains the weekly reading as a numbered step — the command, the `--gate-from` week, and the rule that an unproducible week is *recorded as a refusal*, never shrugged off as a zero — which is the surface that makes the deliberately-absent timer safe. The sleeve-composition alert's re-derivation step gains the realized half beside `accum-replay`'s floor half. A new `engine-tracking-band` runbook section covers the tile's four states, the three arming preconditions, and the standing conditions that silently change what a scored week means (a NAV change, a basket widening). [[T0018]]'s `00091` row is re-tiered off "none (read-only)", and `README.md`'s usage documents the subcommand.
- **Suite: 3941 passed, 0 failed** in full, with the data-gated regression tests executed rather than skipped (1115 s) — the new module's own tests include the real-journal true-positive and every D5 refusal constructed and seen to trip. Each task was reviewed by an agent other than its implementer before the next began, and the live-trade-path work carries the Fable floor; the spec and plan commits ride the user-approved producing flow and its cold spec+plan review.

## 2026-08-23 — T0115's abort was never a `dispose()` race: the Rust logger double-inits; bumped to the version that fixes it ([[T0115]] resolved)

- **The mechanism is falsified and replaced, by measurement rather than argument — this entry supersedes 2026-08-09's.** `NautilusKernel.__init__` calls `init_logging()` only `if not is_logging_initialized()` and holds the returned `LogGuard`. On **1.230.0**, dropping that guard resets the flag while the Rust `log` crate's global logger stays set, so the **next** kernel re-inits and the Rust side panics at `crates/common/src/ffi/logging.rs:198` and aborts. Six lines reproduce it with no `TradingNode`, no event loop, no `dispose()` and no exec client: `init_logging()` → `True`, drop the guard + `gc.collect()` → `False`, `init_logging()` → exit 134. On **1.231.0** the same six lines read `True / True / RuntimeError: Logging subsystem already initialized`, exit 1.
- **So the abort site is `TradingNode.__init__`, and `dispose()` is only the enabler.** `loop.close()` removes the signal handlers `_setup_loop` registered, and those were the last reference keeping the kernel — and its guard — alive. Measured on the real node: stdlib loop → flag `False` after dispose, second `build_shadow_node()` exits 134; uvloop → guard alive, second node built, exit 0. The exec client was a coincidence of test ordering (`exec_enabled=False` aborts identically), and GC timing is what the old, unexplained "needs the whole suite's accumulated process state" correlation was.
- **Production is structurally unreachable, which is now a measured claim.** `cli/engine/command.py` builds exactly one node per process, so a second `init_logging()` never happens; and `nautilus_trader/system/kernel.py` selects uvloop whenever `"pytest" not in sys.modules`, under which the guard does not drop at all. 2026-08-09's "the live engine executes the aborting path on every shutdown" was derived correctly from a wrong mechanism: the engine does reach `loop.close()`, but that branch is inert unless a second node follows it.
- **A month of investigation had no evidence for a mechanical reason, and that is the finding with production consequences.** `_setup_loop` registers an asyncio signal handler for **SIGABRT**, and `loop.add_signal_handler` installs CPython's own no-op C handler over faulthandler's. A/B on one interpreter: a full faulthandler dump plus C stack becomes **zero bytes** at the same exit 134, purely because a node was built first. The repo carried zero `faulthandler` references, so the live engine had the same blindness.
- **`zcrypto engine run` now re-arms faulthandler immediately after `build_shadow_node`.** `disable()` before `enable()` is load-bearing — `enable()` returns early when faulthandler already considers itself enabled and cannot reinstall the clobbered handler on its own. The `enable()` raise is swallowed: it raises when `sys.stderr` has no fileno, and a diagnostic must never stop the engine from starting. Five child-process arms pin it (unclobbered dumps; armed-then-built silent; `enable()`-only silent; production silent without the re-arm and dumping with it), and the call site plus its ordering are pinned against the real `engine run`. **Asserting on `signal.getsignal(SIGABRT)` would have proved nothing** — identical on both sides, because faulthandler installs below CPython's cache of Python-level handlers.
- **`infra/scripts/nautilus-dispose-probe.py` is retired for `nautilus-logger-guard-probe.py`, and the retirement is the sharper lesson.** The old probe spawned pytest-free children, so every child it ever ran selected uvloop — the one arrangement in which the guard cannot drop. It was blind on **every** version, which is why it returned 18/18 clean on 1.230.0, the version whose defect is known present, and its docstring's "exercises the shape of the production shutdown" was true of production and false of the CI failure it was aimed at. The replacement is two-sided — arm 1 the bare reproducer, arm 2 the pytest arrangement, arm 3 the production arrangement — reading DIRTY / DIRTY / clean on 1.230.0, and it can exonerate a future version where its predecessor could only ever escalate.
- **The test fence stays and its comment is rewritten.** One `TradingNode` per process is what upstream prescribes, so spec `00078`'s containment was right on its own terms; only its stated reasoning (an io_uring poller, an abort off a non-Python thread, a race inside `dispose()`) was wrong, on every point.
- **Nothing is filed upstream — decided, not deferred.** v1 is EOL with security backports only and `system/kernel.py` does not exist on v2, so the bug template's "still reproduces on 2.0.0rcN" checkbox is untickable as a matter of fact; their `AI_POLICY.md` forbids an agent filing.
- **The engine container's exit-code history was checked and is INCONCLUSIVE, not clean** — no `134` found, but the current container had never shut down, dockerd logs no die events in a filterable form, and the windows around known restarts showed only the *new* container starting. It is recorded as "no evidence either way"; the unreachability result does not rest on it.
- **`nautilus-trader` bumped 1.230.0 → 1.231.0 here, and that is what closes the topic.** The abort is fixed upstream: on 1.231.0 the guard no longer resets (`is_logging_initialized()` stays `True` after the drop) and a second `init_logging()` raises `RuntimeError: Logging subsystem already initialized` instead of panicking. All three probe arms clean — two nodes now coexist in one process under a stdlib loop *and* under uvloop. Full suite on the new version: **3792 passed, 21 skipped**, the same count as on 1.230.0.
- **A second logger is still REFUSED, only no longer fatal — and the probe's own verdict had to be fixed to say so.** As first written it required `second-init CLEAN` for a fixed verdict, so it reported 1.231.0 as "the trap is still present": true of the restriction, false of the abort, and exactly the misreading the instrument exists to prevent. It now separates the two questions and says which one the version answers.
- **The fence stays as posture, not necessity.** With the abort gone it is no longer load-bearing for survival; one `TradingNode` per process is still what upstream prescribes, so a clean probe is not licence to build two.
- **The order-semantics gate MOVED rather than lapsed, and that is the part with real money behind it.** `docs/reference/adapter-verification/1.230.0.md` bound its ~€0.20 attended re-run to "before the engine trades", which had been read as blocking the bump PR. Nothing trades until the owner arms the engine by hand (measured at the bump: `zcrypto_exec_armed 0`, `zcrypto_exec_gate_level 0`, no arm file), so the re-run is a precondition of **arming**. **It has since RUN for 1.231.0 — PASS on all six probes, attended 2026-08-23 (below).** The obligation lives where it can bite — a pre-probe step in `infra/runbooks/engine.md` that refuses arming unless the running nautilus version has its own verification doc — because a rule recorded only in a research doc is invisible at execution time. 1.231.0 also brings new TLS-destructor machinery (upstream #4496/#4516) in the same failure class, so that re-run is real work.
- **[[T0115]] is RESOLVED and archived.** Nothing of it remains: mechanism found and old account falsified, abort fixed in the pinned version, production never reachable, blind instrument replaced, fence and reasoning corrected, faulthandler re-arm landed, upstream filing decided against. The two obligations that outlived it went somewhere that can enforce them — the arming checklist and [[T0085]] — rather than lapsing with the archive.
- **A review catch worth recording, because the fix had re-created the very blindness it removes.** The re-arm first took faulthandler's default `sys.stderr`, wrapped in a swallow. Under any harness whose stderr has no `fileno()` — Click's `CliRunner`, so five existing tests — `enable()` raises *after* `disable()` has already run, leaving the process with faulthandler **off**. Measured: an armed faulthandler reads `False` after the sequence, and a pytest session that ran those tests ended with native-crash dumps silently disabled. It now passes `file=2`: an fd needs no `fileno()`, so the call cannot fail that way and needs no exception handling, and a fatal-signal dump belongs on the real stderr fd anyway. The `engine run` test fixture stubs the module besides, so no test retargets this process's faulthandler.
- **The arming obligation became two mechanical guards reading ONE committed record**, `cli/engine/order-semantics-verified.json`. The engine Ansible role refuses a converge rendering `exec_armed = true` on a version absent from it (`-e arming_override="<reason>"`, reason-required); `cli.engine.execgate` refuses the runtime gate when the RUNNING interpreter's `nautilus_trader` is absent from it (`reasons=…,nautilus_unverified`). Neither subsumes the other — arming takes two keys and the arm file is placed by hand long after any converge — and the record lives under `cli/` because the engine image copies only that directory, so a copy under `infra/` would be unreachable from the running engine. Adding a version to it IS the act that records the re-run: a reviewed diff, not a memory.
- **The record is shape-checked before membership is tested, and that is not defensive padding.** Jinja's `in` is SUBSTRING containment on a string and KEY containment on a mapping, so a record degraded to `"1.230.0, 1.232.0"` would vouch for an unverified `1.232.0`, and a bare `"1.230.0"` would vouch for the prefix `"1.230"`. Anything that is not a proper sequence collapses to `[]`, which vouches for nothing — the guard's contract is cannot-vouch ⇒ refuse, and a malformed record is exactly a cannot-vouch.
- **The six-probe harness is now committed code rather than a scratchpad driver** — `infra/scripts/kraken-order-semantics-probe.py`, procedure in `infra/runbooks/order-semantics-verification.md`. Spec `00039` chose a scratchpad driver "per the trial-driver precedent", which fitted a one-off entry criterion; the obligation is now recurring and mechanically enforced, and probes are only comparable across versions if they are the SAME probes. It is dry-run by default, gates submission behind `--apply` and the money probe behind a second flag, refuses rather than clamps on its notional ceiling, and cancels only by order — never `cancel_all_orders`, because the live engine shares the account.
- **1.231.0 PASSED all six probes, attended 2026-08-23, so arming is unblocked.** Cost €0.16 — two €0.08 commissions plus €0.00093 of spread — and the account's own balance moved €99.84 → €99.68, which is the same figure reached from venue truth rather than the harness's arithmetic. Recorded in `docs/reference/adapter-verification/1.231.0.md`; the 1.230.0 record is renamed `…-1.230.0.md` so the two read as one series. **Post-only semantics are unchanged**, and the event stream proves it rather than the verdict: 4a/4c/4d each carry an `OrderPendingCancel` (our cancel) while 4b goes accepted → canceled in the same millisecond with none, so that is Kraken cancelling it and the engine's existing rule still holds. Taker fee still exactly 0.80 %/side.
- **Three review rounds preceded the live run, and two of the findings were the reason it was worth doing.** A per-order notional ceiling could have REFUSED probe 5's *closing* leg — the buy fills at a price the plan did not know — stranding a real position while the run printed "nothing was submitted" and exited as a clean refusal. And the first fix for the post-only check matched the substring `post_only`, which also matches the attribute NAME in `due_post_only=False`, so every rejection would still have passed: an always-green guard replaced by a differently-spelled always-green guard. The predicate is now a pure function whose selftest was SEEN to fail against the buggy expression.

## 2026-08-23 — every open/partial `ripe_when` becomes a trigger again, and two that had already fired were adjudicated

- **The field had drifted into three jobs and only one of them is its own.** A `ripe_when` answers "what observable event makes this live work?", and it is read at every index review, where a paragraph costs more than it informs. Across the 25 open/partial topics it was also carrying status reports, derivation arguments, and history. All 25 now state the trigger and nothing else; the longest OLD field was T0018's at **1,474 characters** (now removed entirely), and the longest surviving fell 1,003 → 52 (T0044 — 50 plus the two quotes YAML needs). Across all 25 the field's total fell **9,265 → 2,471**, a reduction of 6,794; across the 21 that changed, 8,915 → 2,121. A first pass at these numbers measured only each field's FIRST line and so under-counted by T0018's 757-character continuation — corrected here, and the reason the arithmetic is quoted at all is that it is the one part of this entry a reader can check.
- **Five were MOVES, not trims, and the difference was measured rather than assumed.** Before removing any prose the body was checked for the claim it carried. T0044's `250,000` threshold and its "why size, not cycle time" reasoning existed nowhere else; likewise T0132's nine-failed-cold-review history and its "normalise first" answer, T0065's consciously-dropped 1h early-promotion window, T0129's 42,000-sample derivation, and T0133's re-freeze rationale. Each moved into the body first. The other trims were verified against the body claim-by-claim — nine specific assertions checked, all already present.
- **The review-and-drain rule was executed, not just the reformat.** Every trigger was evaluated. The three measurable ones were read live rather than reasoned about: `zcrypto_reconcile_ledger_records` **99** against a 250,000 trigger, `zcrypto_engine_limit_bound_total` **0**, and T0129's 30-day sample count **20,188** against ~42,000 — up from 10,435/10,838 at its 2026-08-05 derivation, so it remains measured rather than dated. One caveat worth recording rather than glossing: both hosts read an identical 20,188 where 08-05 read 10,435/10,838, which implies the series restarted around 2026-08-09 rather than accruing continuously — the trigger is still sound, but it is counting from that restart.
- **Two topics declared their own trigger already FIRED, and a fired trigger is live work.** Both were consciously re-deferred with the reason written down, on the owner's ruling. **T0018** lost its `ripe_when` entirely rather than keeping a discharged one: the gate fired 2026-08-11 (re-measured at convening — `zcrypto_gate_status` 1, streak **30**, mismatch 0, series 38 s old) and what remains is *sequenced*, not triggered, so there is no event left to name. **T0016**'s B2 and C3 legs are ripe — B2's sourcing unblocked since 2026-07-24, C3 since iter-072 — and are deliberately not picked up: the contention is attention, not readiness, with the Phase-6b go-live run-up owning it until settled. Recorded so the ripe state stops reading as an unnoticed miss.
- **A latent trap closed while here: SEVEN topics' frontmatter did not parse as YAML, and all seven predate this pass.** Three distinct causes, not one: a value opening with a backtick (T0044, T0103, T0121, T0133), one opening with `**` — which YAML reads as an alias (T0018, T0065) — and one containing `": "`, which it reads as a nested mapping (T0085). Six were fixed by quoting and two by the reshape itself dropping the offending opener, so only T0129's quoting guards a value this pass introduced. Nothing consumes the field programmatically today, which is why it had never bitten and is exactly why it would have bitten the first tool that tried. All 25 now parse.
- **Not every long trigger was wrong.** T0085's stays per-sub-item across five clauses because it genuinely gates five different things, and T0025, T0027, T0090 and T0150 needed no change at all. (T0103's trigger was already correct; only its quoting changed.) The standard is one trigger stated directly, not a character budget.

## 2026-08-23 — a periodic check stops being an open topic: T0103 re-homed to the runbook, and the drop its trigger licensed withdrawn

- **T0103's pre-decided drop is REFUSED, and the reason is a retention artifact.** The topic's remainder was "re-derive the healable-gap threshold; if the series is still flat when picked up, drop the re-derivation — a threshold cannot be fitted to a condition that never fires." Measured 2026-08-22 against Grafana Cloud, the counter was flat: `changes(...[90d])` 0, `resets(...[90d])` 0, level unmoved. Re-measured 2026-08-23 against the **ops reconcile ledger** — the counter's own source of truth — it has moved on **four distinct days** (2026-07-17, 07-27, 07-29, 08-04). All four predate Cloud's retention, which holds ~14 days from ~2026-08-09, so a 90-day selector was reading zero over a series the datasource has never seen move. The condition fires roughly every two to three weeks; dropping on "it never fires" would have dropped on a false premise.
- **The topic fell into the trap it documents.** T0103's own text registers exactly this failure against [[T0129]] — a `[30d]` selector read as thirty days of evidence over a series that had not lived that long — and then its own trigger was evaluated the same way. A trigger is only as good as the datasource it is read from, which is why the corrected `ripe_when` now NAMES the source (`/var/lib/zcrypto-ops/capture-reconciled/reconcile-ledger.jsonl`) and says never Cloud.
- **The day-attribution is trustworthy because the total reproduces the gauge exactly.** Summing `claimed_seconds or healed_seconds` over the ledger with the production expression from `cli/archive/command.py` yields `18850.166148999993` against a live gauge of `18850.166148999993`. A plausible-looking parse that missed the `healed_seconds` fallback gave two days instead of four — caught by reading the summing expression rather than inferring the field, and the exact-match is what distinguishes the corrected parse from the first one.
- **Refusing the drop did not make the re-derivation ripe, and the distinction is the point.** Only two of the four days are post-fix steady state: the 2026-07-17 drill was staged rather than organic, and the 2026-07-27 blackout's value is the 96.4112 % fiction this topic exists to have corrected. Fitting a threshold to either is fitting to a known-wrong signal, which the topic forbids in the same breath. **The 2026-08-22 re-basing had silently dropped the "from steady state after the 2026-07-28 counter fix" qualifier** — load-bearing, because without it the two untrustworthy days count and the trigger reads as already fired. Restored.
- **A first reading of the two post-fix values was wrong, in the direction that matters.** The rule divides by live pair count before comparing, so its threshold is **600 s per pair**, not 600 pair-seconds total. At 12 pairs the two events are 44.5440 s and 41.2144 s per pair — **7.4 % and 6.9 % of the threshold**, not the "within 11 % and 18 % of firing" an un-divided comparison produced. Same numbers, opposite conclusion, and the divisor is the whole difference. The re-derivation therefore starts from a threshold real events have come nowhere near.
- **T0103 is RESOLVED by re-homing, not by doing the work — and the owner's framing is what got it there.** Its remainder was never an investigation: no unknown, no pending fix, no decision owed. It was *watch a counter; when three post-fix days accumulate, re-derive a threshold* — a periodic check with a defined action, which is a runbook shape rather than a topic shape. That is [[T0113]]'s precedent exactly: a recurring routine becomes durable by living where the operator meets it, not by staying on a list. It now lives at `infra/runbooks/ops.md#healable-threshold-rederivation-due`, carrying the trigger, the count-from-the-ledger-never-from-Cloud procedure, the post-fix cutoff, the production summing expression, and an explicit **re-arm** step — because the one-off 2026-08-27 Slack prompt would otherwise expire and leave the check unowned again.
- **The same change wrote a runbook section that a live alert had never had.** `zcrypto-reconcile-healable-gap-rate` can page an operator and had no section, while both its reconciler siblings did — found only because re-homing the routine required asking where its signal is documented. The runbook's own scope rule is what made the placement decidable: procedures for a signal that fires, never a backlog, so the section states that three qualifying days makes the re-derivation *due and real work*, to be opened as its own item against data that then exists.
- **[[T0045]] is resolved as a conscious DROP** (owner's ruling at the 2026-08-23 grooming), which is a decision the topic had itself demanded rather than indefinite parking. Byte-exact CRC re-attestation would cost a schema migration on the unbackfillable live capture stream and cover only post-change, top-10-depth data; OPS-3's continuity-replay has run since 2026-07-15 without CRC re-derivation and has surfaced no discrepancy the structural check could not localise — the topic's own stated condition for revisiting. The gap stays documented as a known limit; a future discrepancy earns a new topic, not a revival.
- **[[T0025]]'s trigger loses the leg that had already been answered.** It read "…or a live-trading universe refresh is about to run"; that leg fired on 2026-08-13 when the refresh ran and published `universe-20260813/` needing nothing from the ledger. A trigger that fires and is adjudicated to *no* every time is not a trigger — it only guarantees the next refresh re-derives the same answer. What remains is the leg that would make the work live: a selected pair changing identity. Verified against Kraken's published delistings 2026-08-23 — none touches the twelve.

## 2026-08-23 — the cycle record learns the NAV it priced against, and the drift it feeds stops depending on a live config value

- **The NAV spurious-kill vector is closed by arithmetic rather than by a habit.** Targets are `weight × nav / close` and drift divides by `nav`, so a `shadow_nav_eur` change re-scored an already-closed, healthy week against the new denominator — halving NAV roughly doubled every reading. Nothing refused it; the only thing standing against it was a runbook sentence asking the operator to disarm the band across any NAV change. `nav` is now journaled per cycle and `realized_drift` scores each cycle under the value that was live for IT, with the caller's scalar as the fallback for records predating the key. **Both** producers of `CycleStages` carry it — `executor._stage` on the trip path and `feeders.replay_stages` on the `tracking-report` path — and that pairing is load-bearing rather than incidental: the report is where the band is re-derived and the trip is armed against that band, so fixing one surface alone leaves the human banding at one NAV while the engine trips at another. The first implementation did exactly that; review caught it, and a mutation probe then showed the four tests shipped alongside it could not have. The runbook sentence stays REQUIRED until the engine converges onto this code, and is re-trued in that deploy — the arithmetic replaces the habit only once the habit's premise is gone.
- **The owner's ruling dissolved the decision the topic had parked.** It was framed as *journal NAV* **or** *refuse when the live value differs* — but the topic's own text notes the second "needs somewhere to have recorded the old value, which is the first". Both branches needed the journaling, so the real question was narrower: what the trip does once NAV is journaled. Scoring each cycle under its own is correct by construction — drift is a per-cycle quantity and `drift_bps` already took `nav` per call — so no refusal is needed at all, and a week straddling the widening scores each half right instead of refusing.
- **`held` is journaled and deliberately NOT read, which is the part worth remembering.** The obvious move is to journal the position and immediately have the trip read it instead of accumulating fills, retiring the three guards that exist only to detect a truncated fill history. That is wrong at the widening: a week scored shortly after deploy spans cycles written before it, and a scorer taking journaled positions for some cycles and fill-accumulated ones for others mixes two position sources inside one week. It becomes safe only when the 60-day retention horizon has passed the deploy — so the topic goes `partial` with a `ripe_when` that is a clock rather than a task, and the three guards stay live and correct until then.
- **`held` comes from the venue read, not the model**, narrowed to the model's BASE key space over the /EUR legs: a /BTC leg is dropped rather than folded in, which would double-count that base under two quotes. It is absent when the venue read failed, because a zeroed book would read as FLAT — a real position — where absence is the honest answer.
- **A read-only pin had to be narrowed, and narrowing beat deleting.** `test_targets_are_identical_with_and_without_venue_state` proves venue truth is journaled but never consulted, and asserted the two cycle artifacts were byte-identical. The record now carries one deliberately venue-derived field, so that assertion could not stand as written. It now asserts the adversarial venue read WAS journaled, then neutralises that one field and compares the rest byte-for-byte through the real serializer — everything the venue must not touch pinned exactly as before, plus a positive assertion that was not there.
- **`evidence_fingerprint` covers neither field, and a test now says so.** It hashes what a REPLAY verdict depends on; these are drift-scoring inputs a replay never reads, exactly as `closes` is. Pinned so a later widening cannot quietly fold them in and invalidate every cached verdict.
- **One asymmetry inside the weekly report is deliberate.** Its realized half is scored per journaled cycle; its FLOOR half (`accumulation_payload`) stays on the caller's live scalar, because the two answer different questions — realized drift is past tense and must not be re-denominated, while the floor asks what is unavoidable at today's size and holds NAV constant by design. The seam that leaves: across a NAV change a week's numerator and its floor are quoted at different NAVs, so that verdict is advisory, and `--simulated-fills` inherits the same seam. Stated at the call site so it does not get "fixed" later.
- **Deploy cost unchanged from the topic's warning**: `journal.py` sits inside the gate-export replay closure transitively via `command.py`, so the NAS gate-export replays cold at the measured 2490 s. Both fields landing together is one converge and one such window instead of two.

## 2026-08-24 — the manifest zoo is removed rather than read

- **Nine cold-review rounds had failed on the wrong question.** Two prior attempts wrote a generic manifest CONSUMER over five writers emitting four `series` shapes, and the blocking findings MOVED each round — round 1 "the holdout is unregisterable", round 4 "`derivatives-funding`/`-oi` are unregisterable" — which is what a pile of special cases looks like from outside. Spec `00099` normalises the WRITERS instead. A four-reviewer cold panel, one of them tasked explicitly with proving this was failure ten, returned SHAPE SOUND four times out of four.
- **`series` is keyed by the parquet's path relative to the dataset root.** The path IS the key, so no consumer ever derives one — which is what flattens the four shapes into one and what makes path-bound verification possible at all. The measurement that settled it: `derivatives-funding` and `derivatives-oi` share the identical `series[SYMBOL].sha256` structure yet lay files out under different names, so shape alone never determined the path.
- **The digest's ordering had to be declared, not inherited.** There was no single "existing recipe": `backfill.py` sorted interval keys as strings (`'1440' < '240' < '60'`) and `reach.py` as integers, so no one recipe could reproduce both. Ordering is now by series key, and `ohlc-reach`'s continuous digest re-anchored `8a826898…` → `356826a1…` as a result — provably a re-ordering of identical content, because the converter refuses unless every recomputed series hash matches what the legacy manifest attested.
- **A manifest declares its own `identity` digest.** `ohlc-reach`'s identity is its continuous subset while every other set's is set-wide; without the declaration a caller would still need per-set knowledge — the removed special case one layer up.
- **Migration was manifest-only and measured**: the converter rebuilds each manifest from the parquets already on disk. 128 parquet files on the workstation, **zero bytes changed**. The hub was converted the same day from its own parquets read over the read-only mount, after all six manifests were backed up byte-identically.
- **[[T0133]]'s parked consumer is discharged in production.** Every hub set now supplies path bindings — 36/12/10/10/30, plus the holdout's 10 from the committed sidecar — so two series swapped inside a manifest-attested set are refused at fetch and at read. Both were invisible before.
- **The external freeze is excluded by construction**, carrying no `schema_version`; the committed sidecar is the normalised form of its attestation content. Loosening the contract to fit a set this repo does not write is what killed round 1.
- **The branch's own defect is the lesson worth keeping.** The converter's first version preserved only top-level legacy keys while the guard test asserted only top-level fields — with a comment citing reach's per-row seam evidence as its motivation. It passed against the defect it named, and running it erased the seam record of `data/ohlc-reach-20260813`. Derivable fields were reconstructed and labelled, each derivation validated against the hub's surviving legacy copy. A later review narrowed the loss again: `rest_first` was derivable for the DETACHED rows (a detached segment is the whole REST window, so `rest_first == first_ts`, 10/10 on the originals), so only the 12 continuous rows' `rest_first`/`overlap_bars` and the detached rows' `gap_bars` are genuinely gone. Three vacuous guards on one branch, each caught by a reviewer rather than by the suite — and twice the first estimate of what was lost was too pessimistic, which is its own reason to measure before reporting.

## 2026-08-24 — refine-rules round 5: the corpus stops telling stories

- **Six lessons graduated from the round-5 harvest window** — five as clauses and bullets in `agent-ops.md` (discriminating fixtures on guard-proving, a grep hit is not a call site, snapshot unversioned data before rewriting, `--amend` targets HEAD, push-only workflows outside PR CI's reach) and the NAS transfer-path facts into `fleet.md`'s nas row.
- **Eight staleness findings fixed, all user-approved**: the rollout checklist stopped demanding a freshness read its own abort table strikes through; the dependabot skill lost four defects including a jq expression that hid every pending check behind any concluded one (reproduced) and a bare `git stash pop` that could dump the user's own pre-existing stash; two skills stopped citing an archived topic and a rotted principle range.
- **The condense pass applied the owner's cold-agent standard corpus-wide** — every sentence must tell a cold agent when to do what and how. Cut: tense-narration of before-states, incident history with its dates/counts/topic-IDs, rationale stacked past the one-clause why, and mechanics duplicated from self-documenting scripts. Ten edits in the protected `fleet-deploys.md`, each individually signed off.
- **The cold diff review earned its keep**: it caught a condensed clause that contradicted its own gate (bump-alloy's dry-start), a one-clause why cut exactly where `docs-style` says to keep it (the design-settled Fable floor), and a graduation that landed weaker than its staged original ("code" admits string literals; "call site" does not). All three fixed before anything was deleted from memory.
- **Net: always-loaded 54,530 → 53,638 (−892 bytes) while absorbing five new lessons**; modal floor 102 → 100 with both decreases itemized inside deleted narration, no prohibition weakened.
- Two protected-file residuals flagged for the owner rather than edited: the compose-digest bullet no longer names the two confusable digest vars (`ops_alloy_digest`/`ops_image_digest`) that made the mistake live, and the engine re-pin bullet's value-not-presence rationale now lives only in the Alert-rule lifecycle section.

## 2026-08-25 — the engine crosses to nautilus v2, and the attended pass clears it for arming

- **The engine is on nautilus-trader 2.x, pinned `===2.0.0rc4.dev20260825`.** The `===` is arbitrary equality and it is not cosmetic: the index publishes both `X` and `X+<local build>`, PEP 440 ignores a local version segment when matching `==` and orders `X+local` **above** `X`, so `==` produced a lock with TWO nautilus entries — x86_64 on the develop build, aarch64 and macOS on the nightly — one lockfile handing different platforms different artifacts, silently, because the two builds are functionally identical. The lock now carries one entry, and twelve packages left with the flip: pandas, pyarrow, msgspec, fsspec and uvloop among them were nautilus's transitive dependencies, and v2 declares almost none of them.
- **The flip was a single commit and the branch was red from it**, by design — the pin resolves from `pyproject.toml`, so there is no state in which the suite is green on v2 while the project is pinned to v1. That commit's own baseline was the work-list (48 failed, 4024 passed, 8 collection errors), and every task after it closed a named part of it.
- **The branch's governing principle, now at the head of spec `00100`, is what makes this more than a port**: *if we had only the functional requirement and no v1 code, what would we build on v2?* A branch-wide sweep asked it of every area, and what it returned is the substance of the migration rather than a coda to it — the startup watchdog deleted, the test harness rebuilt on real library types, the terminal-state map collapsed to one, the startup sweep narrowed, and the probe harness rebuilt so the probes are the node's own callbacks. Two things it explicitly does not license, stated with it: weakening a live-trade-path guard, and adopting a v2 feature because its name resembles something we hand-rolled.
- **The startup watchdog is DELETED (D14), and the measurement that removed it also showed it had never been right.** `engine run` armed a 120 s `threading.Timer` that force-exited on a not-RUNNING read. Measured on the pinned wheel: the node already aborts its own failed start in every condition the watchdog named — a data client pointed at a closed loopback port raises `RuntimeError: data-connect timeout` at `NodeState.STOPPED`, an unmeetable reconciliation budget raises `Startup reconciliation timeout reached` — so the timer could never fire on any of them. The one state left in which it *could* fire is a node that reached RUNNING and began a graceful shutdown inside the window, where `os._exit(1)` truncates the residual-event drain and the disconnect **on the live trade path** and skips `logging.shutdown()`. And the budget mis-modelled the machine: `LiveNodeConfig` carries five timeout phases (connection 60 s, portfolio 10 s, reconciliation 30 s, disconnection 10 s, shutdown 5 s) while the hand-rolled sum took two and called them the entire budget — our copy re-derived a five-phase state machine from two, and the timer held 20 s of margin rather than the 30 s its design assumed.
- **Deleting it put the whole load on one claim — the raise escapes and the alerting sees it — and that claim had a hole in the class the engine is most exposed to.** `cli.__main__:run` caught `Exception`; a Rust panic surfaces as `pyo3_runtime.PanicException`, which pyo3 derives from `BaseException` **precisely so `except Exception` does not catch it**. The engine holds a node whose core is compiled Rust, so a panic would have fallen through to a Rich traceback on stderr with no level for Alloy to label: the process dies, the alerting never sees it, and detection falls back to a missing boundary artifact zeroing the gate streak hours later. Everything but `SystemExit` and `KeyboardInterrupt` is now logged, and the widening reaches every `zcrypto` subcommand, not just the engine.
- **A fee-less fill used to lose its forensic row while the ladder had already credited the quantity.** `OrderFilled.commission` is declared `Money | None` and constructs absent on the pinned wheel; `_fill_payload` read `float(...)` and `.currency.code` off it bare, and that raise lands in `on_order_event`'s blanket except — which logs and continues — after `_on_fill` has already credited `active.filled`. A ladder carrying a quantity the ledger has no row for, on the one invariant that path exists to hold. The EUR counter a few hundred lines up was already None-safe; the row was not. An absent commission now writes `fee: null` and still writes the row.
- **The `===` pin broke the arming backstop in the direction that gets a guard routed around.** The engine role extracted the pin with `nautilus-trader==([^"\s]+)`, which consumed two of the three `=` and captured the third into the version — so once a legitimate attended pass added the version to the record, the armed converge would still have refused while its own `fail_msg` named that exact version as recorded. Fail-closed, so nothing unsafe could be armed; the damage is the opposite one, that the guard could never be SATISFIED again, leaving `-e arming_override=…` as the only exit at exactly the moment the money guard is meant to hold. The test that should have caught it re-derived the pin with a COPY of the guard's own regex, so the mangled string matched itself; it now parses the pin with `tomllib`, which cannot share the guard's mistakes.
- **Stopping the node FLUSHES its pending clock alerts — found by measurement during the harness rebuild, and it was a real-money defect.** An interrupted `--probes all` run left **nine** probe rows in its evidence file: SIGINT at 27 s into a 60 s settle window, and the settle alert plus every remaining deadline fired during the shutdown, so the whole remaining sequence ran after the operator pressed Ctrl-C. That run was dry and only printed; with `--apply` those same steps would have SUBMITTED, and commands issued from a stopped strategy still reach the execution engine (measured) — so an abort could have opened four resting orders and a market position on the way out, none of them covered by the `on_stop` sweep that had already run. One stopping flag now drops the remaining steps (naming how many, in the evidence file) and refuses at `submit`, the single choke point every probe order passes through. A probe already in flight is deliberately not abandoned: that order exists at the venue and its evidence is owed.
- **The probe harness's coroutine trampoline was built, measured working end to end, and then discarded.** Its only purpose was keeping `async def` bodies alive inside a library that never asks for them, and a 100 ms polling loop rode along inside it. What shipped is a step queue plus one waiting primitive — a predicate, a clock alert and a continuation, where an already-satisfied wait continues immediately and arms nothing. The same rebuild stopped the harness holding `Order` objects: a held order never advances past `INITIALIZED`, and `teardown()` classified `INITIALIZED` as "never submitted" and returned a clean bill while real orders rested at the venue on the account the engine trades. It now keeps client order ids and reads `cache.order(ClientOrderId(...))` wherever status, fill, average price or closedness matters, and "never submitted" is the harness's own record of what it handed to `submit_order` rather than an inference from a status.
- **The stub-driven suite was this branch's recurring defect class, and it is answered with a guard rather than another patch.** Each stub is a hand-written restatement of a nautilus contract with nothing verifying the restatement, which is why both cold-review rounds produced blocking findings with one root. The sharpest instance: `cli/engine/venuestate.py`'s `_to_float` and `str(instrument.id)` coercions are load-bearing in production and pure no-ops under every fixture in the file — `mutate-probe.sh` scored both **SURVIVED**, control proven, so deleting `_to_float` left the suite GREEN while the live boundary would throw into a swallowing handler and zero the ratified gate streak. The harness now reads a real `CurrencyPair` back out of a real `Cache` and both mutations are **KILLED**. The generalisation is a fidelity guard that walks every `test_engine_*.py` module — derived from the directory rather than a hand-typed list, and reaching doubles bound through assignments the earlier AST walk never visited — checking each double against the library type it restates.
- **One terminal-state map, proven total, on both row-state paths.** `_EXTERNAL_TERMINAL_STATES` keyed row states on event CLASS NAMES — an open string space over which no totality statement is even expressible — beside an `OrderStatus`-keyed map that carried a real proof against the library's own closed set. The string map is deleted and `_on_external_event` reads `cache.order(...).status` through the survivor. Every disagreement between them was enumerated by driving a real `LimitOrder` through the library's own events rather than reasoned about; the reachable ones are refused-transition shapes — `ownTrades` and `openOrders` are separate Kraken WS channels with no cross-stream ordering guarantee — where the old map wrote a permanent misstatement into a terminal row that never re-attaches.
- **The startup sweep stopped reading the account's whole order history.** The adopt pass read `cache.orders(venue=...)` — the entire order index, closed orders included — then re-filtered it in Python to answer questions about at most a two-day window of ledger rows. It now classifies over `orders_open(venue=...)` and asks `cache.order(ClientOrderId(coid))` per row, so O(account history) becomes O(rows in the window). The one justification for keeping a single wide read was "two reads can disagree", checked and refuted: `cli/engine/executor.py` contains zero `async`/`await`, and no other Python code can interleave between two statements of `on_timer` on the event-loop thread.
- **The version tripwire moved from a constant to the guard that bites.** `test_pinned_version` asserted a hardcoded version, so it reddened at every bump; on a nightly channel that is noise, and noise gets rubber-stamped, which is how a tripwire dies. It now checks the installed version against the version `pyproject.toml` pins — catching a stale venv, a resolver surprise and the `===` local-segment trap — and the routing the deliberate failure used to provide is replaced, not dropped: the tripwire is now the arming gate's own refusal, met where it blocks the money instead of a test run. That refusal had no coverage on the real interpreter (every gate test injects a version reader) and now has it.
- **The branch's own residue was worked off after the closeout, and two items of it were live-path.** `_venue_terminal_state`'s cache-read failure branch was unguarded: narrowing its `except Exception` to a never-raised type left the executor and node suites fully green, so the `try` read as redundant while in production the adopt pass's own cancel triggers `RuntimeError('Already mutably borrowed')` and the escape costs an adopted row its event payload AND its terminal close, leaving it open forever. Now a parametrized pair over one fixture — same event, Cache readable and raising — proven with three probe verdicts: KILLED with the new test, KILLED with the new test alone, SURVIVED with it deselected. And the interface pin's "every nautilus name imported anywhere under `cli/`" was a claim nothing checked; an AST walk of the tree found three unpinned, including `StrategyConfig`, which `node.py` imports from `nautilus_trader.trading` while the pin exercised only `nautilus_trader.config` — a dropped re-export would have passed the pin in full and killed `node.py` at import. Coverage is now derived from the tree rather than asserted. Alongside: spec `00098`'s D1/D4/D6/Verification stopped ruling the `msgbus` arrangement D2 replaced (its guard bullet sanctioned an allowlist entry the shipped guard does not have), `engine run --help` stopped naming a class that exists nowhere, and the probe runbook's exit-2 row stopped telling an operator to re-run over an open position.

- **The attended ~€0.20 order-semantics pass RAN against v2 and PASSED on all six probes** — 2026-08-26, the first live run of the rebuilt harness, recorded in `docs/reference/adapter-verification/2.0.0rc4.dev20260825.md`. `cli/engine/order-semantics-verified.json` now reads `["1.230.0", "1.231.0", "2.0.0rc4.dev20260825"]`, and **both arming guards clear the pinned version** — constructed rather than inferred: the runtime gate resolves the running interpreter's `2.0.0rc4.dev20260825` as recorded, and the engine role's arming assert evaluates TRUE against the committed `pyproject.toml` pin and the committed record with `exec_armed = true` rendered, while a control that drops the pinned version from the record makes it refuse. Cost €0.16 in commissions; the account's own balance moved 99.6772 → 99.5173 across the money leg, a drop of 0.1599 against an expected 0.16001 — consistent to a hundredth of a cent but not exact, and the residual is recorded rather than rounded away. The credential reached the harness through `infra/scripts/probe-with-vaulted-key.sh`, which decrypts the vaulted trade key straight into a hardcoded target's environment and never onto a command line, a file or an operator's shell — so the run could be permitted narrowly instead of granting a general secrets-reading capability. D1 was amended on the owner's ruling in the same stretch: the pinned nightly IS the deployable version, because RUNG 1 is probe scale and holding a finished migration behind an unpublished 2.0.0 would keep the engine trading on a version entering maintenance. **The pin is FROZEN at that string until the engine is armed on it** — `order-semantics-verification.md` §1.6's freeze rule, with §2.1 settling the record's granularity: exact complete strings, matched exactly, as the *interpreter* reports them.
- **What is NOT done, and must not read as done: the engine converge onto a v2 image, and two owed checks that block arming rather than the converge.** The fleet still runs the v1 image on both capture hosts and on the engine, so the converge is owed and inherits its own gates — the canary rule (there is no engine secondary, so the secondary's capture bake at the v2 pin IS the gate, and it has not been taken), the 4-hourly inter-cycle window, and a `fleet-pins.md` row before the re-pin. The two checks the pass could not discharge are registered in the record's own `## Owed checks not discharged by this pass` section — the first boundary cycle after the run, and the healthy-boot `unmatched` baseline — which `infra/runbooks/engine.md`'s pre-probe step 3 reads and refuses to arm past. A **disarmed** converge is unaffected by either arming guard.
- **[[T0085]]'s nautilus sub-item is rewritten around that.** The 1.231.0 converge it named is **cancelled**; of the three steps it stated in order — freeze the version, run the attended pass, then converge — the first two are discharged on this branch, and only the engine converge onto a v2 image is left, its `ripe_when` waiting on the secondary's capture bake at the v2 pin. Its logger-guard half closed earlier on this branch by **retirement**: every premise of the defect it watched is measurably gone from the v2 wheel, so re-expressing it would build a guard for a door with no production caller, and nothing is owed at the next bump on that account.
- **One topic carries what the migration could not reach, and its trigger has not fired.** [[T0152]] — D2's external-order observer has its publish leg established from source and its delivery leg in a backtest, and the join needs a genuine venue-sourced external order, which needs live Kraken reconciliation that no backtest and no workstation run can produce; it now also carries the healthy-boot `unmatched` baseline owed before any alert is thresholded on that counter, ordered ahead of the deliberate-external-order read.
- **The seam neither cold review reached was found, ruled on, and closed inside the branch — [[T0154]], opened and resolved.** The exec engine mints terminal events of its own — an `INFLIGHT_TIMEOUT` rejection, a cancel ack for a cancel the venue never answered, a whole order to close a position discrepancy — all default-ON, all marked only by a `reconciliation` bool nothing under `cli/` read. The three routes did not fail alike, which is why "it fails safe" was never an available summary: the position route is contained by D3's unmatched filter, the rejection route halted in the ambiguous arm only because `INFLIGHT_TIMEOUT` matches no marker string, and the **cancel route drove `_fallback` on an ack nobody at the venue gave** — the executor crossing with an IOC while the original may still rest. Spec `00100` **D15** now rules: `OrderRejected`/`OrderCanceled`/`OrderExpired` carrying the flag take the ambiguous exit from ABOVE the name dispatch, which also turns the rejection route's string-match accident into a decision and makes a future `_KRAKEN_ERROR_MARKERS` entry harmless. **Both order-event surfaces read it** — on the adopted-order stream there is no intent to strand and the row carries the outcome, decided by reading the venue's order, which a minted terminal has already driven to `CANCELED`; so a minted terminal writes no row state there, leaving the event as evidence, the entry attached, and the row inside the set the next startup re-attaches from, instead of closing it with a venue claim nobody made. **Fills are excluded deliberately** — a reconciled fill is the venue's own report transcribed late, only its trade id minted, so its row, its quantity credit and its published fill are owed; dropping the credit would make the next resubmission over-ask by exactly that quantity, the same double exposure in the other direction. The three in-flight knobs are now stated at their current values as a complement, never a substitute: no Kraken REST ack latency is measured, so no other number was derivable. Proven on the pair a one-sided fixture cannot judge — the same event class, the same time-boxed intent, flag false still falls back and resubmits, flag true submits nothing and refuses every later intent — and by two mutation probes, both KILLED with controls proven, failing on opposite assertions. **The one sub-item that genuinely could not be done — the rate, which needs a live exec client — moved to `infra/runbooks/engine.md` §1 Pre-probe step 5** rather than dying in the archive, placed after the healthy-boot `unmatched` baseline it must be read against.
- **[[T0153]]'s quantity half closed too, and the measurement that closed it is that the event never arrives.** `OrderFillVoided` — the venue undoing a fill it already reported — had no reversal anywhere: not the journaled `filled_qty`, not the overfill trip's in-process mirror, not the counters. The chain from upstream source says the only Kraken-reachable producer is the mass-status reconciliation the node runs BEFORE `start_trader()`, so no handler is subscribed when it publishes, and both periodic checks are off and route through a path that mints a void only for a `Voided` report Kraken never sends. So no live arm was added — it would be a guard on a door with no caller — and the surface is the Cache: a venue order whose `filled_qty` the library already lowered. 00098 D7's sweep already latches on exactly that shortfall, but it is fed the re-attach set, which by construction omits the `filled` row a withdrawal of a completing fill lands on. `closed_submitted_rows` is the complement of the same predicate, so the two are total over `submitted`; `_reconcile_finished_rows` asks one question in one direction and latches on a shortfall. **Nothing is reversed** — spec 00100 D16 weighs subtracting the quantity (`update_submitted_row` would take a negative with no floor, the trip's base moves under it, and a corrected ledger no longer shows that it ever disagreed), routing to `_strand_ambiguous` (the wrong analogy: a withdrawal is the venue speaking, not the engine guessing) and demoting the row's state (the row and the `filled` counter would disagree forever to say what the appended event already says). The counters are fixed in meaning rather than retracted: fills this engine was told happened and paid for. Guarded on the paired fixture the withdrawal MOVES — a voided PARTIAL fill leaves the order `ACCEPTED` and would pass under either behaviour — with the un-voided arm as the true positive and a raising-journal arm proving the write cannot swallow the latch.

## 2026-08-27 — the engine's idle data socket stops spending a connection budget it shares with capture (iter-146)

- **One literal, four guards, one runbook section — spec `00101`.** `ws_idle_timeout_ms=0` on the engine's Kraken data client.
